[arXiv:2610.22331] KnowGPO: Knowledge-Grouped Policy Optimization for Search Agents

  • arXiv ID: 2610.22331 (2610.22331v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-10-15
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.AI
  • Eje Cognitivo: Process Reward Models (PRM) & Verificación Paso a Paso
  • Autores: Siqi Chen, Zhengxi Lu, Yuchen Yan, Xingyu Wu, Qipeng Chen, Xin Zhang et al.
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22331)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22331) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.22331.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.22331.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Deep search agents solve knowledge-intensive tasks through long-horizon, multi-turn interactions, where assigning credit to individual search decisions is fundamentally difficult. Outcome-based reinforcement learning provides only a single terminal reward shared across an entire trajectory. Recent process-supervision methods aim to assign credit at finer granularity, but they typically anchor credit to either surface-level trajectory states or model-internal signals, both of which become unreliable in deep search where observations are heterogeneous and retrieved contexts are long and noisy. We propose KnowGPO, a reinforcement learning framework that aligns trajectories by the task-relevant knowledge they have acquired rather than by their surface observations. For each query, KnowGPO decomposes the information required to derive the answer into atomic knowledge conditions, and represents the state of a trajectory at each step by the subset of conditions it has satisfied. Trajectories sharing the same knowledge state are grouped for within-group comparison, and the incremental knowledge gain between consecutive steps serves as an environment-grounded process reward. Experiments on Xbench, BrowseComp-ZH, and GAIA show KnowGPO consistently outperforms outcome-only (GRPO) and process-supervision baselines (GiGPO, IGPO) by 3.8 to 4.2 points, with our 8B model rivaling or exceeding most 32B open-source systems.

Reglas Operativas Extraídas (Aplicación en Inferencia y Razonamiento)

1. [Directiva de Process Reward Models (PRM) & Verificación Paso a Paso]: Evaluar y puntuar la validez lógica y normativa de cada paso intermedio del razonamiento (encuadre procesal, legitimación, presupuestos fácticos, subsunción normativa, costas) en lugar de evaluar únicamente la conclusión final.

2. [Supervisión Granular por Pasos (Step-Level PRM)]: Validar secuencialmente cada considerando o nodo de decisión. Si un paso intermedio presenta incertidumbre o ambigüedad, podar esa rama de razonamiento y recalcular desde el último antecedente verificado.

3. [Descomposición Agéntica y Uso de Herramientas]: Dividir la tarea compleja en subtareas atómicas verificables (extracción de hechos -> búsqueda de precedentes -> encuadre legal -> redacción -> control de calidad), invocando herramientas externas de recuperación antes de razonar en abstracto.

4. [Búsqueda Deliberativa y Consenso Interno]: Generar y contrastar múltiples líneas argumentales (argumento principal, eventuales defensas y criterio de Alzada) seleccionando la solución que maximice la coherencia interna y la estabilidad frente a una eventual apelación.

[arXiv:2610.21496] OPRD: On-Policy Reminder Distillation for Long-Horizon Coding Agents

  • arXiv ID: 2610.21496 (2610.21496v2)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-10-15
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.CL
  • Eje Cognitivo: Process Reward Models (PRM) & Verificación Paso a Paso
  • Autores: Yuechen Luo, Zhiyi Lai, Miao Ziqi, Zihang Xu, Jingxuan Xu, Songwei Yu et al.
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21496)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21496) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.21496.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.21496.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Reliable repository-level coding requires agents not only to generate correct code but also to preserve constraints and respond to tool feedback across long interactions. However, our diagnostic analysis shows that agents often fail to act on relevant information already available in context, while final Pass/Fail outcomes provide only sparse process supervision. We introduce On-Policy Reminder Distillation (OPRD), which combines Multi-Scope Reminder supervision with outcome-based routing on reminder-free student rollouts. Successful rollouts provide autoregressive self-imitation targets, whereas failed rollouts receive Reminder-conditioned reverse-KL supervision from a teacher that re-evaluates the same student-generated prefixes. A Global Reminder converts final-test diagnostics into trajectory-level guidance, while Turn-local Reminders apply only to the next decision following the relevant environment observation. The teacher and student share a two-stage initialization from Coding SFT and Instruction-Following (IF) SFT. At inference, the student operates without Reminders. On SWE-bench Verified, SWE-bench Pro, and SWE-bench Multilingual, OPRD improves the benchmark-averaged task resolution rate by 11.3 points over the open-source Qwen3.5-4B base model. Under the same diagnostic protocol, it reduces the aggregate error-event count from 446 to 222, a 50.2% reduction.

[arXiv:2610.21362] LTM: Latent Transition Based In-Context Memory for Vision-Language-Action Models

  • arXiv ID: 2610.21362 (2610.21362v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-10-15
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.RO, cs.AI
  • Eje Cognitivo: Process Reward Models (PRM) & Verificación Paso a Paso
  • Autores: Qixin Sun, WenLong Gao, Xiansheng Chen, Zhongyuan Wang, Si Liu, Pengwei Wang
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21362)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21362) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.21362.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.21362.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Robotic manipulation often requires remembering interactions that are no longer visible. We introduce Latent Transition Memory (LTM), which stores history as compact latent transitions between consecutive observations. Instantiated on $π_{0.5}$ as LTM-$π$, our method uses a frozen latent action encoder, a lightweight adapter, and causal KV caching for incremental inference without memory-specific semantic annotations or external reasoning-model calls. With DreamDojo transitions, LTM-$π$ achieves 58.54% success on RoboMME and 80.5% on LIBERO-Mem, performing best among baselines without external reasoning models or memory-specific annotations. Ablations support the contribution of transition content beyond temporal cues and the efficiency of delayed memory admission. Under a 200-token memory budget, success remains 84.00%, versus 87.50% without compression. Finally, real-robot counting success improves from 11.25% to 82.50%. Together, these results demonstrate the effectiveness, scalability, and efficiency of latent transition memory, highlighting its capacity for complex temporal information, bounded-context operation, and real-world robotic control.

[arXiv:2610.21346] ARGUS: An Agenda-Driven Framework for Reliable User Simulation

  • arXiv ID: 2610.21346 (2610.21346v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-10-15
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.AI
  • Eje Cognitivo: Process Reward Models (PRM) & Verificación Paso a Paso
  • Autores: Hang Zheng, Nan Jiang, Zichen Zhu, Zixu Yang, Zhiyang Tang, Shuai Fan et al.
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21346)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21346) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.21346.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.21346.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> High-fidelity user simulators are essential for evaluating and training agents in interactive environments. However, current LLM-based simulators struggle with reliability issues, such as goal omission and policy non-compliance. We propose ARGUS (Agenda-dRiven Goal-oriented User Simulator), a neuro-symbolic framework that maintains the user's goal state using a dynamic agenda stack manipulated via symbolic operations. By rigorously decoupling state tracking from natural language generation, ARGUS facilitates strict goal progression and constraint-aligned interactions. For cost-efficient deployment, we distill ARGUS into compact models using a unified pipeline: evaluator-guided SFT followed by online RL with dense step-level rewards. To rigorously evaluate behavioral fidelity, we introduce a rubric-based metric Composite Behavioral Score (CBS). Evaluations on $τ^3$-Bench and MultiWOZ demonstrate the significant superiority of the ARGUS architecture and the efficacy of our training pipeline. Code and data will be released soon.

[arXiv:2611.16104] Show Your Work in Swahili: Reinforcement Learning for Native-Language Mathematical Reasoning

  • arXiv ID: 2611.16104 (2611.16104v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-11-11
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.CL, cs.AI
  • Eje Cognitivo: Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)
  • Autores: Jessica Lundin, Katherine Rosenfeld, David Adelani, Geoffrey Mosoti Nyakiongora, Halfan Ngowo, Sloan Coffin et al.

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> We present Fikiria, a two-stage SFT+GRPO recipe for Swahili mathematical reasoning. Its reward multiplies a format gate by a weighted sum of answer accuracy and the ChrF\texttt{++} similarity of the Swahili reasoning to an in-language reference chain of thought, so that training targets the surface fidelity of the reasoning trace and not only the final answer. We train on a 2.3k-problem Swahili dataset created by translating GSM8K with GPT-4.1 and filtering with SSA-COMET. On AfriMGSM Swahili, Fikiria-AfriqueQwen-14B reaches 79.2\%. In a small human evaluation with co-author annotators, GPT-4o's outputs were preferred overall. ChrF\texttt{++} measures surface fidelity, not the validity of individual reasoning steps.

[arXiv:2611.15680] SpaceMCP: Vision Language Model Spatial Reasoning via Model Context Protocol

  • arXiv ID: 2611.15680 (2611.15680v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-11-11
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.LG
  • Eje Cognitivo: Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)
  • Autores: Ameen Izhac, Nobuko Yoshida, Ronald Clark
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15680)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15680) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.15680.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.15680.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Despite impressive advances in large language models (LLM), vision-language models (VLM) still exhibit limited spatial reasoning capabilities. Recent progress in feed-forward 3D reconstruction has enabled precise, real-time geometric understanding from visual inputs. In this work, we propose SpaceMCP, a framework that leverages these advances to introduce an MCP-style paradigm for spatial reasoning. Our approach augments a VLM with structured function calls, allowing it to query a scene graph for spatial information such as distances, object positioning, and camera properties. The scene graph is constructed in real time using feed-forward 3D reconstruction to recover metric geometry, combined with open-vocabulary segmentation to identify and localize relevant objects within the scene. Paired with the VLM's strong reasoning capabilities, the VLM is able to combine this primitive information to solve more complex spatial tasks. Without any training, SpaceMCP achieves state-of-the-art results on VSI-Bench, MMSI-Bench and VSTI-Bench, outperforming recent spatially post-trained VLMs and improving its base VLM by 15-22 points per benchmark. We argue that, given the contrast between the relatively slow progress in native VLM spatial reasoning and the rapid advances in feed-forward 3D computer vision, this paradigm offers a promising path forward for enabling robust spatial reasoning.

[arXiv:2611.15449] Reading the Unreadable: Text-Aware Image Super-Resolution Needs Reasoning

  • arXiv ID: 2611.15449 (2611.15449v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-11-11
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.CV, cs.AI
  • Eje Cognitivo: Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)
  • Autores: Jaeseong Lee, Jinwoo Kim, Jinho Jeong, Seon Joo Kim
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15449)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15449) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.15449.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.15449.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Text-aware image super-resolution (TAISR) aims to recover high-resolution images from low-resolution inputs while preserving textual content. Existing methods treat this as a visual restoration problem, yet severely degraded text often cannot be read from local visual evidence alone. Humans instead read it by reasoning over image context, logical patterns, and prior knowledge. This raises a fundamental question: Does TAISR need reasoning? To answer this, we introduce two diagnostic tools. The first, ReasonText, is a human-curated benchmark that separates reasoning-required text from locally recoverable text. The second, Ground-Truth Text-Crop Accuracy (GTTCA), is a recognition-based metric that measures text restoration quality without text detection errors. Evaluating twelve recent super-resolution (SR) models, we find a consistent and substantial performance drop on reasoning-required text, revealing a systematic blind spot. Two observations point to a remedy. One is that text-conditioned SR models restore text substantially better when given ground-truth text. The other is that multimodal large language models (MLLMs) can read degraded text in low-resolution images through reasoning. We therefore propose Reasoning Transfer via Captioning (RTC), a training-free plug-in that uses an external MLLM to infer the degraded text and feeds the resulting caption to a text-conditioned SR model. RTC consistently improves all six text-conditioned SR models we evaluate, especially on reasoning-required text. Surprisingly, a real-world SR model with RTC can even outperform the TAISR model with RTC, suggesting that reasoning should be a core design axis for future TAISR.

[arXiv:2611.15225] Can MLLMs Reason over Surround-View Driving Videos? A Multi-View Autonomous Driving QA Benchmark

  • arXiv ID: 2611.15225 (2611.15225v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-11-11
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.AI
  • Eje Cognitivo: Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)
  • Autores: Shunming Liu, Jianyi Peng, Cheng Li, Lu Hou, Pei Huang, Yajing Sun et al.
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15225)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15225) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.15225.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.15225.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Surround-view video understanding in autonomous driving is a crucial capability for Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) to achieve reliable driving reasoning. Real-world driving often requires information beyond the front-view camera. Existing MLLMs primarily focus on front-view information, while their spatio-temporal understanding and reasoning abilities remain unknown. To address this gap, we propose MVDriveBench, a high-information-density video QA benchmark for autonomous driving, characterized by synchronized surround-view inputs, spatio-temporal understanding, and reasoning-chain-oriented evaluation. We design 12 categories of QA tasks that cover the complete reasoning pipeline from low-level visual perception to high-level driving decision-making, enabling a systematic evaluation of MLLMs surround-view spatio-temporal understanding and reasoning capabilities in complex driving environments. We conduct a comprehensive evaluation of 23 open-source, closed-source, and expert MLLMs on MVDriveBench. The results show that current MLLMs still exhibit insufficient surround-view spatio-temporal reasoning ability. Meanwhile, closed-loop evaluations of specialist models in AlpaSim reveal a correlation between surround-view spatio-temporal reasoning capabilities and closed-loop driving performance, highlighting the importance of comprehensive multi-view understanding for reliable vehicle control.

[arXiv:2611.14881] MLIRBench - A Benchmark and Dataset for Compiler and Hardware-Aware Reasoning

  • arXiv ID: 2611.14881 (2611.14881v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-11-11
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.PL, cs.AI, cs.LG
  • Eje Cognitivo: Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)
  • Autores: Pranava Sai Maganti, Meghasyam Peddireddy, Muzakker Hossain, Kaining Gao, Farhan Ahmed, Ali Jannesari
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14881)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14881) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.14881.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.14881.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> In recent years, large language models (LLMs) have become increasingly popular in software engineering and have performed well on different programming tasks. However, it is still difficult to know whether these models truly understand program behavior or whether they mainly rely on patterns learned from their training data. This distinction is particularly important for intermediate representations (IRs), where operations, dataflow, and execution semantics are explicitly represented. In this paper, we introduce MLIRBench, a benchmark for evaluating how LLMs reason about MLIR programs. MLIRBench uses a multi-agent pipeline to generate program mutations, validate their behavior through execution, and construct reasoning tasks from torch and linalg program pairs. The dataset contains 1,768 validated pairs, including 888 linalg pairs and 880 torch pairs, labeled as EQUIV, DIVERGE, or ERROR. We evaluated nine models, including five open-weight and four proprietary models. Gemini 3.1 Pro achieved the highest hard-label accuracy of 92.65% across all 1,768 pairs, counting unsuccessful predictions as incorrect. Across its 1,768 scored predictions, it achieved an aggregate score of 74.18 and a judge-assigned result-behavior score of 5.04/10. These results show that strong semantic classification does not necessarily imply accurate prediction of detailed execution behavior, highlighting a gap in current LLM reasoning over compiler IRs. By exposing this gap through execution-validated program pairs, MLIRBench provides a reproducible foundation for studying semantic reasoning over compiler IRs and related tasks such as program repair and semantic classification.

[arXiv:2611.14263] Chain of Physics: Supporting Fault Diagnosis with Engineering-Inspired Large Language Model Reasoning

  • arXiv ID: 2611.14263 (2611.14263v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-11-10
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.LG, eess.SP
  • Eje Cognitivo: Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)
  • Autores: Wenqi Zhou, Leonardo Baldo, Kai Goebel, Manuel Arias Chao
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14263)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14263) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.14263.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.14263.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Fault diagnosis in complex systems requires linking observed changes in physical quantities to the failure mechanisms that produce them. Experienced engineers perform this task by combining sensor information with knowledge of system behavior and fault propagation. Large Language Models (LLMs) can support this process, but their diagnostic reasoning depends on the availability of asset-specific engineering knowledge and on how this knowledge is represented and used during inference. This work introduces Chain of Physics (CoP), an LLM-based fault-diagnosis framework built around a Constrained Knowledge Graph (CKG) generated automatically from asset-specific reference documentation. CoP addresses failure-sensor association from textual engineering knowledge. The CKG stores diagnostic chains connecting failure modes, changes in physical quantities, and monitoring sensors. During inference, semantically similar graph paths are retrieved for each question and used as both information and in-context examples for the reasoning process. Candidate failure modes are extracted from the question and analysed independently through dedicated LLM calls, which generate plausible failure-to-observation chains. A final decision module compares these chains with the available answer choices. CoP is evaluated on the FailureSensorIQ benchmark across four industrial asset classes and three LLMs. Compared with direct prompting and conventional Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG), CoP improves diagnostic accuracy across all evaluated models, with gains ranging from 7.8 to 19.6 percentage points and from 11.7 to 18.2 percentage points, respectively. The results show that structured engineering knowledge can improve LLM-based diagnostic reasoning when graph retrieval is combined with in-context examples of fault propagation.

Reglas Operativas Extraídas (Aplicación en Inferencia y Razonamiento)

1. [arXiv:2611.14209] Breakdown of Autoregressive Multi-Step Reasoning Under Concentrated vs. Diffuse Attention Head Dropout

  • arXiv ID: 2611.14209 (2611.14209v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-11-10
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.AI, cs.CL
  • Eje Cognitivo: Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)
  • Autores: Karol Struniawski
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14209)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14209) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.14209.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.14209.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> During extended wakefulness, mammalian neocortical networks experience localized periods of transient neuronal silence termed off-states. Here, we examine how analogous spatiotemporally concentrated disruptions affect sequential reasoning in autoregressive Large Language Models (LLMs). Evaluating 250 paired derivations on the GSM8K benchmark across two 8B models (Qwen3-8B and LLaMA-3.1-8B-Instruct), we contrast clustered bursts of attention head masking against budget-matched diffuse i.i.d. dropout. Concentrated bursts induce substantial reasoning degradation: at onset parameter $\mathcal{B}=0.10$ (scheduling $\sim 5.7\%$ of vulnerable head-token volume), Qwen3-8B accuracy drops from $91.2\%$ under diffuse noise to $78.8\%$ under burst dropout ($Δ= -12.4$ percentage points [pp], exact McNemar $p = 5.5 \times 10^{-6}$), while LLaMA-3.1-8B-Instruct drops from $84.0\%$ to $76.8\%$ ($Δ= -7.2$ pp, $p = 0.0198$). Conversely, dispersing the identical cumulative budget uniformly across generation steps via stratified quintile matching yields substantially milder degradation (retaining $91.2\%$ in Qwen and $84.0\%$ in LLaMA). In an exploratory factorial dissociation ($N=100$), single-step dropout density is associated with an acute drop in a single schedule draw ($-10.0$ pp on Qwen, $p_{\text{holm}} = 0.0425$), while multi-token continuity exhibits an inconclusive compounding trend ($-7.0$ pp, $p_{\text{holm}} = 0.1892$). Error analysis reveals that Qwen failures reflect derivation errors within formatted responses (conditional accuracy drop of $-11.4$ pp), whereas in LLaMA $22.2\%$ of discordant losses lack a final answer format line (conditional gap narrows to $-5.0$ pp). These findings demonstrate that autoregressive reasoning chains are particularly vulnerable to concentrated structural disruptions, providing empirical benchmarks for fault tolerance in transformer architectures.

Reglas Operativas Extraídas (Aplicación en Inferencia y Razonamiento)

[Directiva de Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)]: Estructurar cadenas de razonamiento explícitas (Chain-of-Thought deliberativo) separando estrictamente la premisa fáctica probada, la premisa normativa vigente y el juicio de subsunción, ejecutando un pase de autoverificación contra alucinaciones o saltos lógicos.

2. [Asignación Dinámica de Cómputo de Inferencia]: Calibrar la profundidad deliberativa según la complejidad del caso: aplicar resolución directa en trámites estandarizados y expandir el árbol de búsqueda (múltiples candidatos + ranking) en casos con colisión normativa o jurisprudencial.

3. [Trazabilidad y Explicabilidad Técnica]: Explicitar los fundamentos y pasos de derivación desde los datos de entrada hasta la conclusión dispositiva, garantizando auditabilidad completa del razonamiento.

[arXiv:2611.14122] Hx2Dx: From Anamnesis to Diagnosis - A Multimodal Arena for Conversational Clinical Reasoning in LLMs

  • arXiv ID: 2611.14122 (2611.14122v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-11-10
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.AI
  • Eje Cognitivo: Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)
  • Autores: Federico Ravenda, Mirko Cesarini, Daniele Montagnani, Volodymyr Karpenko, Antonietta Mira, Andrea Raballo
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14122)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14122) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.14122.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.14122.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Large language models (LLMs) achieve expert-level diagnostic accuracy in static evaluations, where the model receives a curated vignette or case report that already condenses the diagnostically relevant findings. Such evaluations measure inference over already selected evidence; the evaluations do not account for the inquiry used to obtain it. In clinical practice, however, diagnosis is an iterative process of inquiry. Clinicians take the patient's history, or anamnesis (Hx), through targeted questioning, examine the patient, and request and interpret tests, progressively narrowing the differential until they reach a final diagnosis (Dx). We introduce Hx2Dx ARENA, an interactive benchmark of 1'000 clinical cases from the New England Journal of Medicine (NEJM). Each case is a simulated encounter that proceeds from initial presentation through history taking and physical examination to diagnostic workup. We evaluate open- and closed-source LLMs under multiple configurations. Performance varies considerably across models in the interactive setting, and every model loses diagnostic accuracy relative to the static setting, where the full case is presented upfront. The ability to ask the right questions accounts for much of both the gap between models and the loss of accuracy: diagnosis improves as questions become more targeted and build a more complete clinical picture. LLMs thus reason well once the relevant data are available but remain limited in hypothesis-driven information gathering, in knowing what to ask in order to know. Closing this gap is a prerequisite for their safe integration into real-world diagnostic workflows.

2. [Supervisión Granular por Pasos (Step-Level PRM)]: Validar secuencialmente cada considerando o nodo de decisión. Si un paso intermedio presenta incertidumbre o ambigüedad, podar esa rama de razonamiento y recalcular desde el último antecedente verificado.

3. [Descomposición Agéntica y Uso de Herramientas]: Dividir la tarea compleja en subtareas atómicas verificables (extracción de hechos -> búsqueda de precedentes -> encuadre legal -> redacción -> control de calidad), invocando herramientas externas de recuperación antes de razonar en abstracto.

[arXiv:2611.14106] ProofCoder: Continuous Reasoning for Automated Software Verification

  • arXiv ID: 2611.14106 (2611.14106v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-11-10
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.SE, cs.AI
  • Eje Cognitivo: Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)
  • Autores: Martim Afonso, Inês Lynce, Maurizio Morisio, João F. Ferreira
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14106)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14106) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.14106.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.14106.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Formal verification using proof assistants provides strong correctness guarantees for software, but constructing proofs remains labor-intensive and requires significant expertise. Recent approaches based on large language models (LLMs) have shown promise in automating proof synthesis. However, these approaches typically constrain the model to a rigid tactic-generation loop, in which the surrounding system controls premise retrieval, proof-state management, and backtracking. We introduce ProofCoder, an agentic proof-synthesis system for Rocq that approaches theorem proving from a coding-agent perspective. Rather than generating tactics within a fixed loop, ProofCoder reasons continuously throughout proof search and uses proof-assistant feedback to decide when to gather additional information, inspect proof states, revise previously generated proof fragments, or backtrack to an earlier point in the proof. This allows it to iteratively construct, repair, and refine proofs, at either tactic-level or whole-proof granularity. We evaluate ProofCoder on 280 challenging verification tasks from the AutoRocq Benchmark. On the best-performing configurations, ProofCoder increases proof success from 47% to 60% over the state-of-theart AutoRocq system, corresponding to a 27.7% relative improvement at approximately half the cost. Our ablations identify continuous reasoning as a key design choice: without it, the agent solves fewer lemmas at substantially higher cost, backtracks four to six times more often, and more frequently discards its own revisions. Whole-proof and tactic-level generation achieve similar success rates but follow distinct search and repair strategies. These results suggest that effective automated software verification requires more than improved tactic selection: continuous reasoning and feedback-guided proof revision are critical ingredients for robust proof synthesis.

1. [Directiva de Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)]: Estructurar cadenas de razonamiento explícitas (Chain-of-Thought deliberativo) separando estrictamente la premisa fáctica probada, la premisa normativa vigente y el juicio de subsunción, ejecutando un pase de autoverificación contra alucinaciones o saltos lógicos.

2. [Protocolo de Verificación Intrínseca]: Implementar un paso de auditoría independiente sobre cada afirmación fáctica y cita normativa/jurisprudencial antes de consolidar la respuesta final, rechazando cualquier inferencia no respaldada por evidencia documental directa.

3. [Supervisión Granular por Pasos (Step-Level PRM)]: Validar secuencialmente cada considerando o nodo de decisión. Si un paso intermedio presenta incertidumbre o ambigüedad, podar esa rama de razonamiento y recalcular desde el último antecedente verificado.

4. [Descomposición Agéntica y Uso de Herramientas]: Dividir la tarea compleja en subtareas atómicas verificables (extracción de hechos -> búsqueda de precedentes -> encuadre legal -> redacción -> control de calidad), invocando herramientas externas de recuperación antes de razonar en abstracto.

5. [Búsqueda Deliberativa y Consenso Interno]: Generar y contrastar múltiples líneas argumentales (argumento principal, eventuales defensas y criterio de Alzada) seleccionando la solución que maximice la coherencia interna y la estabilidad frente a una eventual apelación.

[arXiv:2611.14018] Interleaving anatomical inspection and diagnostic reasoning for explainable radiology report generation

  • arXiv ID: 2611.14018 (2611.14018v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-11-10
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.CV, cs.AI
  • Eje Cognitivo: Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)
  • Autores: Chentao Huang, Runlong Lin, Zhijun Wang, Yunxiang Li, Yuanyuan Sun, Jian Wang et al.
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14018)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14018) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.14018.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.14018.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Automated radiology report generation can draft clinically plausible reports, yet the pathway from visual evidence to reported findings remains opaque, leaving the diagnostic basis of these reports unexplained and the generated reports difficult to review or revise. We introduce ARIVE (Anatomy-guided Reasoning with Interleaved Visual Evidence), an explainable report generation framework that interleaves anatomical inspection with diagnostic reasoning prior to final report composition. To support this process, we constructed 159,416 multimodal chain-of-thought (MCoT) trajectories from MIMIC-CXR, wherein visual evidence from inspected anatomical regions informs subsequent clinical interpretation. Trained on these trajectories and further optimized using anatomical and clinical-content rewards, ARIVE achieved superior clinical-efficacy performance compared with strong baselines on MIMIC-CXR and the abnormality-centered MIMIC-ABN. ARIVE's reports were competitive with those of leading models in blinded expert grading, and its MCoT trajectories earned high ratings for diagnostic traceability. Furthermore, a paired review evaluation suggests that access to MCoT can aid in identifying errors within generated reports.

[arXiv:2611.13562] An evidence-traceable benchmark of clinical reasoning in large language models

  • arXiv ID: 2611.13562 (2611.13562v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-11-10
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.CL
  • Eje Cognitivo: Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)
  • Autores: Zhiqun Zhao, Xiaodong Yang, Bruce Gu, Ming Li, Yi Fu, Song Li et al.
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.13562)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.13562) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.13562.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.13562.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Benchmark scores for medical large language models (LLMs) are difficult to interpret when scoring criteria cannot be traced to clinical evidence. Here we developed Med Evidence Bench (MEB; https://www.medevidencebench.org.cn/), comprising 1,536 physician-constructed items across three levels: guideline knowledge, standardized clinical scenarios and real-world laboratory scenarios. Each criterion stating a medical claim is linked to a versioned source-text span. Across ten LLMs, the Evidence Trace Score (ETS) was 0.975-0.997 for guideline knowledge, 0.292-0.658 for standardized scenarios and 0.182-0.447 for real-world scenarios, and rankings varied across levels. For real-world scenarios, scores for identifying clinical information (mean, 0.770) exceeded those for matching evidence (0.165) or formulating recommendations (0.160). On 1,296 criterion-level judgments, automated grading agreed with the majority vote of three physicians (macro-F1, 0.930; 95% confidence interval, 0.915-0.945), although agreement was lower for evidence application. These findings from Chinese-language materials indicate that guideline recall does not ensure patient-specific evidence use and do not establish clinical effectiveness.

[arXiv:2611.13496] Reasoning-Aware Relational Representation Learning for Open-Vocabulary Scene Graph Generation

  • arXiv ID: 2611.13496 (2611.13496v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-11-10
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.CV, cs.AI
  • Eje Cognitivo: Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)
  • Autores: Jiawei Xiao, Boya Wang, Longtian Qiu, Shan Ning, Xuming He
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.13496)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.13496) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.13496.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.13496.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Open-Vocabulary Scene Graph Generation (OV-SGG) requires models to recognize visual relationships beyond the training vocabulary, yet existing methods often rely on dataset-specific object--predicate co-occurrence patterns. Under long-tailed distributions and noisy object localization, such reliance leads to biased, entangled relation representations, limiting both rare-relation recognition and generalization to unseen predicates. To address these challenges, we propose RaRe, a reasoning-aware relational representation learning framework that transforms MLLM-generated chain-of-thought (CoT) rationales into structured relation embeddings. Rather than using CoT only as an intermediate reasoning trace for final prediction, RaRe directly optimizes the rationale-derived representation space to improve inter-relation discriminability. Specifically, RaRe first aligns rationale embeddings with textualized relation descriptions through self-supervised contrastive learning, and then refines rationale generation with a specificity-oriented reinforcement learning objective that encourages semantically object-pair-specific relational evidence. Experiments on VG150 and PSG demonstrate improved category-balanced recognition over existing baselines, while the additional gains from learning vary across datasets and proposal settings. Code available at: https://artanic30.github.io/project_pages/RaRe.

[arXiv:2611.13456] When Reasoning looks like Hidden Understanding: How Chain-of-Thought Fosters Anthropomorphism, Perceptions of Epistemic Superiority, and Cognitive Deference

  • arXiv ID: 2611.13456 (2611.13456v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-11-10
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.AI, cs.HC
  • Eje Cognitivo: Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)
  • Autores: Oliver Lack, Lana Tikhomirov, Humphrey Thompson, Carolyn Semmler, Melissa McCradden
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.13456)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.13456) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.13456.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.13456.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> As AI-based products permeate society, specifically systems using generative models, a belief system is emerging about what these products are and what they can and cannot do. One common belief concerns the perception of the form of language as evidence of 'understanding' language. As these generative models reproduce linguistic patterns rather than understand meaning, interpreting these patterns as evidence of understanding is a misattribution. This misconception is extended by the notion that generative models can 'explain their reasoning.' Anecdotally, these misconceptions which mistake the form of language for its meaning are driving cognitive deference to AI systems in a way that displaces human agency. Such mistakes involve attributions of cognitive properties to the system, allowing them to be treated like intentional 'minds'. Importantly, perceived epistemic authority and superiority make that 'mind' worth deferring to. Anthropomorphism in tandem with epistemic authority and superiority, we argue, increases the risk that users generalise from narrow task performance to broader competencies, committing the "fallacy of composition". This paper explores misconceptions of Chain of Thought (CoT) reasoning as sufficient transparent 'explanation'. We explore how anthropomorphism acts as a vehicle for cognitive deference of decision-making and perceptions of epistemic superiority in AI systems. Finally, we explore CoT's ramifications using medicine as an example. Taken together, we conclude that explainability operationalised through CoT is highly problematic and undercuts the very ethical principles it is intended to serve. While CoT may be valuable for improving output accuracy and moderation, we suggest that the practice of asking models to provide user facing reasons for their outputs should be actively discouraged, and AI literacy training should explicitly caution against its use.

[arXiv:2611.13233] Decisions by Discrimination: Differential Evidence for Medical Reasoning

  • arXiv ID: 2611.13233 (2611.13233v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-11-09
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.AI
  • Eje Cognitivo: Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)
  • Autores: Yunpeng Xiao, Yuzhang Xie, Junhao Huang, Carl Yang, Xiao Hu, Kai Shu
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.13233)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.13233) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.13233.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.13233.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Large language models (LLMs) are increasingly studied for clinical decision support, and medical question answering provides a testbed for evaluating this capability. In particular, medical multiple-choice questions (MCQs) reflect a core challenge in clinical decision-making: plausible options often share overlapping evidence, while only a few decisive factors distinguish the correct option from incorrect ones. However, existing retrieval-based and reasoning-oriented methods mainly focus on supplying broadly relevant knowledge or eliciting reasoning paths, rather than identifying the compact differential evidence that separates confusing alternatives. In this work, we present a retrieval-grounded differential evidence framework for medical MCQs. Our method extracts option-specific pieces of differential evidence and organizes them into concise evidence prompts for answer selection. This design better aligns external knowledge with differential reasoning while reducing irrelevant context. Experiments show consistent accuracy gains across multiple datasets and target LLMs. Ablation studies further show that our framework outperforms both directly using retrieved evidence chunks and closed-source LLM-generated general hints, and that its improvements are not solely explained by answer-proximal guidance, but are consistent with differential evidence serving as a compact aid for reasoning over competing alternatives. Our findings also offer insights for medical reasoning and other decision-making settings with multiple possible choices.

[arXiv:2611.13114] ASPIRE: An Agentic System-Identification Platform for Iterative Reasoning and Exploration

  • arXiv ID: 2611.13114 (2611.13114v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-11-09
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.RO, cs.AI, cs.CL, cs.LG, eess.SY
  • Eje Cognitivo: Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)
  • Autores: Erik Derner, Jiří Kubalík, Robert Babuška
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.13114)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.13114) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.13114.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.13114.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> System identification requires expert decisions on the identification method, incorporation of prior physical knowledge, and model validation. We propose ASPIRE, a framework for nonlinear system identification in which a large language model agent selects and executes the identification method. ASPIRE constructs closed-form, nonlinear static or dynamic models from measured data, optionally supplemented by a system description, the intended use of the model, and physical requirements expressed formally or in natural language. Each model includes its parameters, an estimation record, an interpretation of its terms, and an error evaluated on test data inaccessible to the agent. Formal physical requirements are verified independently. ASPIRE operates autonomously or interactively, allowing the agent to ask questions and the user to request further analyses and identification rounds. We evaluate ASPIRE on three measured systems: a mobile robot, a rotational system with dry friction, and a laboratory fermenter. Each system is tested with and without a description, in five runs per task. All 30 runs delivered valid models, at a cost of 0.96 to 3.38 US dollars per run. An assessment by the authors found that, for the 30 models recommended by the agent, one per run, the interpretation of the model and the documentation of its structure selection and parameter estimation fully satisfied 132 of 150 assessed items and partly satisfied 18. The most frequent discrepancy, found in nine models, concerned inaccurate or unsupported claims about model structure selection.

[arXiv:2611.13107] Catch It Where It Happens: Real Time Error Correction During LLM Reasoning

  • arXiv ID: 2611.13107 (2611.13107v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-11-09
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.CL
  • Eje Cognitivo: Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)
  • Autores: Rotem Elimelech, Michael Shalyt, Ido Kaminer
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.13107)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.13107) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.13107.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.13107.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Large language models (LLMs) are increasingly trusted with mathematical, scientific and engineering work, yet their mistakes are often confidently written and hard to spot. A single wrong step tends to propagate, making the model waste inference tokens following a false premise. Existing safeguards either check only the final answer and restart from scratch, or rely on a verifier LLM that competes for GPU capacity and slows down response time. We propose online, deterministic verification: many kinds of reasoning steps can be validated by code, so a step can be verified in real time, as it is written, without any model call or GPU use. Whenever a check fails, we point out the mistake and let the model continue from the same spot. We demonstrate this approach on symbolic mathematics, where derivations already contain explicit expressions that a computer algebra system can check in milliseconds. Tested on the ASyMoB benchmark with three open-weights models, our online validation improves accuracy by 1.4-5.6 percentage points, corresponding to a 1.8-14.9% net reduction in incorrect answers, while increasing token usage by only 6-7%. Because the verifier is independent of the model, the approach complements progress in LLMs rather than competing with it: every new model can benefit immediately, and the benefit grows as deterministic checkers extend to more kinds of reasoning steps.

Reglas Operativas Extraídas (Aplicación en Inferencia y Razonamiento)

1. [Directiva de Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)]: Estructurar cadenas de razonamiento explícitas (Chain-of-Thought deliberativo) separando estrictamente la premisa fáctica probada, la premisa normativa vigente y el juicio de subsunción, ejecutando un pase de autoverificación contra alucinaciones o saltos lógicos.

2. [Protocolo de Verificación Intrínseca]: Implementar un paso de auditoría independiente sobre cada afirmación fáctica y cita normativa/jurisprudencial antes de consolidar la respuesta final, rechazando cualquier inferencia no respaldada por evidencia documental directa.

3. [Asignación Dinámica de Cómputo de Inferencia]: Calibrar la profundidad deliberativa según la complejidad del caso: aplicar resolución directa en trámites estandarizados y expandir el árbol de búsqueda (múltiples candidatos + ranking) en casos con colisión normativa o jurisprudencial.

[arXiv:2611.12285] Learning Evidence-Grounded Reasoning for Context-Aware Time Series Forecasting

  • arXiv ID: 2611.12285 (2611.12285v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-11-09
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.LG
  • Eje Cognitivo: Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)
  • Autores: Xingpeng Gao, Mingyue Cheng, Daoyu Wang, Huajian Zhang, Xiaoyu Tao
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12285)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12285) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.12285.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.12285.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> LLM-driven time series forecasting has become increasingly promising by leveraging contextual features to generate future values directly or refine forecast priors. However, incorporating contextual features alone does not ensure that models can identify which features matter for forecasting and understand what they suggest about future dynamics. We argue that the fundamental challenge is how to extract predictive evidence from contextual features and when and how to leverage it to guide forecasting decisions. In this paper, we propose CastReasoner, an evidence-grounded reasoning model for context-aware time series forecasting. Specifically, CastReasoner uses a structured chain of thought (CoT) to extract predictive evidence from contextual features, infer the future dynamics suggested by this evidence, and assess these dynamics against the forecast prior to guide adaptive forecasting decisions. To enable this capability, we design a reasoning-centric two-stage post-training framework including supervised fine-tuning (SFT) and reinforcement learning (RL). First, we synthesize reasoning trajectories through controllable multi-teacher guidance and select high-quality ones using a novel student-aware difficulty metric. Second, we design fine-grained multi-objective rewards to further improve reasoning and forecasting. To address heterogeneous learning needs across domains in both stages, we introduce a convergence-guided data balancing strategy. Through this two-stage training, CastReasoner improves both reasoning quality and predictive accuracy. Extensive experiments across multi-domain forecasting benchmarks demonstrate that CastReasoner achieves superior forecasting performance.

2. [Asignación Dinámica de Cómputo de Inferencia]: Calibrar la profundidad deliberativa según la complejidad del caso: aplicar resolución directa en trámites estandarizados y expandir el árbol de búsqueda (múltiples candidatos + ranking) en casos con colisión normativa o jurisprudencial.

3. [Estructuración de Contexto y Restricciones Explícitas]: Formular las restricciones negativas (prohibición de alucinar metadatos) y las plantillas estructurales al inicio del bloque de instrucciones, utilizando precedentes reales recuperados como anclaje in-context.

[arXiv:2611.12014] Benchmarking language models and agents for scientific reasoning in evolving materials laboratories

  • arXiv ID: 2611.12014 (2611.12014v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-11-09
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.AI, cond-mat.mtrl-sci, cs.CE, cs.LG
  • Eje Cognitivo: Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)
  • Autores: Xuyu Dong, Yixuan Li, Jianguo Huang, Feng Chen, Yiyun Zhou, Bo An et al.
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12014)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12014) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.12014.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.12014.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Large language models and the multi-agent systems built around them could accelerate materials discovery by connecting scientific knowledge with experimental decisions. Existing benchmarks establish capabilities in domain knowledge, multimodal interpretation and scientific tool use. As language models increasingly support materials research workflows, their ability to distinguish closely related material states and reason accurately with quantitative evidence remains insufficiently evaluated. These abilities are essential for comparing material responses, assessing mechanisms and maintaining reliable judgements as experimental evidence accumulates. We introduce MatScope to evaluate three connected capabilities. M1 tests unaided, condition-specific reasoning through 200 tasks spanning ten research functions, using aluminium and high-entropy alloys as representative material systems. M2 tests agent-assisted transfer of related knowledge to unresolved materials questions. MatEvoBench-20 tests initial forecasts across scientific dependency chains and revisions of still-hidden responses as observations accumulate. Across eleven model deployments, including GPT-6 Astra and Claude Fable 5.1, given-input calculations and many physical explanations are handled well. Fine-grained response estimates, comparisons between similar states and quantitatively supported choices remain substantially weaker. Related literature improves selected estimates, while condition and baseline mismatches limit transfer. Progressive observations calibrate some downstream responses and destabilize earlier accurate predictions. MatScope establishes measurable targets for developing reliable scientific reasoning in future self-evolving materials laboratories and materials-aware world models.

2. [Descomposición Agéntica y Uso de Herramientas]: Dividir la tarea compleja en subtareas atómicas verificables (extracción de hechos -> búsqueda de precedentes -> encuadre legal -> redacción -> control de calidad), invocando herramientas externas de recuperación antes de razonar en abstracto.

3. [Búsqueda Deliberativa y Consenso Interno]: Generar y contrastar múltiples líneas argumentales (argumento principal, eventuales defensas y criterio de Alzada) seleccionando la solución que maximice la coherencia interna y la estabilidad frente a una eventual apelación.

[arXiv:2611.11949] Watching Itself Watch: Self-Auditing Visual Reliance for Video Reasoning

  • arXiv ID: 2611.11949 (2611.11949v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-11-09
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.CV, cs.LG
  • Eje Cognitivo: Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)
  • Autores: Rohan Dugad, Mi Luo, Kristen Grauman
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11949)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11949) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.11949.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.11949.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Video reasoning hinges on a small number of visually decisive tokens embedded in otherwise largely language-driven chains of thought. Yet prevailing post-training methods such as GRPO overlook this structure, assigning coarse trajectory-level rewards while requiring multiple rollouts and often costly external judges. We introduce Visually-Aware On-Policy Self-Distillation (VIOS), a post-training framework that identifies where a model under-utilizes visual evidence and concentrates learning at those positions. Our key insight is that a model can audit its own visual reliance: the divergence between its next-token distributions with and without the video reveals how strongly each prediction depends on the visual input. Measuring this reliance for both a student and an answer-privileged teacher exposes a per-token reliance gap, identifying positions where the teacher exploits visual evidence that the student overlooks. VIOS translates this signal into dense, targeted supervision through three complementary components: an exponential moving average (EMA) teacher that co-evolves with the student, a contrastive objective that encourages predictions to depend on the video, and a reliance-gap gate that focuses learning on visually under-utilized tokens. Crucially, VIOS requires only a single on-policy rollout from a single model, without external rewards, judges, or larger teachers. Across 12 benchmarks spanning general, temporal, spatial, knowledge-intensive, and long-video understanding, VIOS consistently improves three model backbones and outperforms contemporary video-reasoning systems, while training in over 9x fewer steps, 60x fewer FLOPs, and 111x fewer generated tokens. Project page: https://vision.cs.utexas.edu/projects/VIOS/

Reglas Operativas Extraídas (Aplicación en Inferencia y Razonamiento)

1. [Directiva de Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)]: Estructurar cadenas de razonamiento explícitas (Chain-of-Thought deliberativo) separando estrictamente la premisa fáctica probada, la premisa normativa vigente y el juicio de subsunción, ejecutando un pase de autoverificación contra alucinaciones o saltos lógicos.

2. [Supervisión Granular por Pasos (Step-Level PRM)]: Validar secuencialmente cada considerando o nodo de decisión. Si un paso intermedio presenta incertidumbre o ambigüedad, podar esa rama de razonamiento y recalcular desde el último antecedente verificado.

3. [Trazabilidad y Explicabilidad Técnica]: Explicitar los fundamentos y pasos de derivación desde los datos de entrada hasta la conclusión dispositiva, garantizando auditabilidad completa del razonamiento.

[arXiv:2611.11910] Late but Valuable: Why Deadlines Change the Measured Value of LLM Reasoning

  • arXiv ID: 2611.11910 (2611.11910v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-11-09
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.LG, cs.AI, cs.DC
  • Eje Cognitivo: Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)
  • Autores: Sina Barazandeh, Benjamin E. Lundell, Jose Lugo-Martinez
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11910)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11910) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.11910.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.11910.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Large language models (LLMs) increasingly plan and run experiments in automated laboratories. These experiments keep running while the model reasons and run on schedules with hard deadlines. If an LLM-generated answer arrives late, the system executes a safe fallback action instead, such as rejecting a sample. This creates a systems problem that standard serving metrics do not capture. An answer that matches the fallback adds no value, and answers that differ from the fallback may require more reasoning time. Reporting answer quality and latency separately can therefore overstate the value of reasoning. We test this on synthetic sample decisions, where the best choice is known exactly and a runtime enforces the deadline. We compare one LLM's fast mode, which answers directly, with its thinking mode, which reasons first. In a prespecified replay of 1,200 new decisions, thinking answers that missed a deadline were worth about five times as much as on-time ones. Multiplying the on-time rate by the mean answer value predicted about twice the value that thinking delivered. This prediction overstated thinking's advantage over fast mode by 0.0241 per decision, and by 0.0191 in a repeat on 300 new decisions. Both 95% confidence intervals excluded zero. In a single-GPU Qwen3-8B experiment using simultaneous request bursts, raising the number of requests from 8 to 56 reduced the share of thinking answers that met a 45 s deadline from 79.2% to 1.4%, although total output tripled. Evaluations of LLM agents for laboratories should therefore measure each answer's value and arrival time together and score the action that actually runs.

2. [Descomposición Agéntica y Uso de Herramientas]: Dividir la tarea compleja en subtareas atómicas verificables (extracción de hechos -> búsqueda de precedentes -> encuadre legal -> redacción -> control de calidad), invocando herramientas externas de recuperación antes de razonar en abstracto.

[arXiv:2611.11907] Layer Roles Matter: Mitigating Modality Imbalance in Multimodal Emotion Reasoning via Adaptive Layer Profiling

  • arXiv ID: 2611.11907 (2611.11907v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-11-09
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.AI
  • Eje Cognitivo: Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)
  • Autores: Pengyu Huang, Peipei Song, Beier Zhu, Zhiyuan Han, Jinpeng Hu, Xun Yang
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11907)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11907) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.11907.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.11907.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Multimodal Emotion Reasoning (MER) requires perceiving and integrating emotion cues from multiple modalities. Recent advances increasingly leverage Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) to enhance reasoning capacity. However, due to the inherent heterogeneity in conveying emotions across modalities, these models often suffer from modality imbalance and confront flawed emotion understanding. In this paper, we conduct an in-depth analysis on MER models and uncover two key empirical insights. (i) In MER, modality imbalance manifests two distinct failure modes: unimodal over-reliance and insufficient cross-modal utilization, which call for different intervention strategies. (ii) MLLM layers exhibit implicit functional hierarchies in multimodal emotion processing, playing separable roles in stabilizing unimodal representations and integrating cross-modal information. Motivated by these observations, we propose a Layer-Aware Modality Rebalancing (LAMR) framework by explicitly aligning intervention strategies with layer roles. Specifically, we first introduce two quantized metrics for adaptive layer role profiling. In each layer, Modality Entropy (ME) measures the dispersion of modality contributions, and Cross-modal Intensity (CMI) captures the strength of cross-modal interactions. Then, we respectively enhance modality equilibrium in stabilization layers and cross-modal propagation in integration layers. Extensive experiments on two comprehensive MER benchmarks demonstrate that LAMR effectively mitigates modality imbalance and improves emotion reasoning across diverse MLLM backbones, without additional training.

[arXiv:2611.11901] Adaptive Entropy-Sparing for Efficient Reasoning

  • arXiv ID: 2611.11901 (2611.11901v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-11-09
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.CL
  • Eje Cognitivo: Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)
  • Autores: Cong Jiang, Xiaofeng Zhang, Tom Ko, Zheng Zhang
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11901)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11901) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.11901.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.11901.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Large Reasoning Models (LRMs) produce explicit chains of thought to improve transparency, but they frequently overthink, generating long yet inefficient reasoning chains that inflate computational costs and risk introducing errors. While numerous reinforcement learning methods struggle to penalize verbosity to achieve conciseness, they typically apply the penalty uniformly across lengthy sequences, which leads to credit confounding, that is, the failure to distinguish essential reasoning steps from superfluous ones. In this paper, we study a measurable proxy for this confounding: high-entropy tokens that often coincide with surface transition points where reasoning re-enters verification. Based on this insight, we propose Adaptive Entropy-Sparing (AES), which selectively spares high-entropy tokens that occur before an efficient in-group reference, while penalizing overlength high-entropy suffix tokens associated with superfluous branching. Our AES thereby navigates the suppression-exploration trade-off, reducing overthinking while preserving average reasoning accuracy. We also present an entropy-length model that motivates why local uncertainty can serve as a proxy for future reasoning cost. Across eight reasoning benchmarks, AES reduces reasoning length by up to 52.9% while increasing mean accuracy by up to 3.8% over the base models, consistently outperforming existing efficiency-oriented methods. Our code is available at https://github.com/MobiusDai/AES.

[arXiv:2611.11794] ChemInsight: A Comprehensive Benchmark for Evaluating and Diagnosing Chemistry Understanding and Reasoning in Large Language Models

  • arXiv ID: 2611.11794 (2611.11794v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-11-09
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.CL, cs.LG
  • Eje Cognitivo: Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)
  • Autores: Changyu Hu, Yiming Zou, Xin Tang, Yiru Pan, Zicheng Huang, Deng Zhao et al.
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11794)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11794) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.11794.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.11794.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> We introduce ChemInsight, a benchmark designed to evaluate large language models (LLMs) on chemistry tasks that require multi-step reasoning and structured chemical understanding. We propose a two-dimensional scoring system (scope and depth) to assess both task coverage and reasoning completeness. ChemInsight consists of 203 challenging chemistry questions organized into 50 bundles, which support the two-dimensional scoring evaluation scheme. We evaluate ten representative LLMs using ChemInsight and observe consistently low performance across models, with scope scores below 30% and depth scores below 11%, substantially lower than human expert performance. We then proceed to ask (1) What factor limits the advancement of current LLMs in chemistry? (2) To what extent are statistically driven language-model training methods effective at modeling chemistry symbolic language such as SMILES? And (3) Can LLM performance in chemistry be improved through intensive training on large, domain-specific corpora?

[arXiv:2611.10974] SWEET: Mitigating Spurious Feature Reliance in LLM Reasoning via Weight Editing

  • arXiv ID: 2611.10974 (2611.10974v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-11-07
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.CL, cs.LG
  • Eje Cognitivo: Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)
  • Autores: Zhizhang Fu, Lucas Zhao, Michalis Korakakis, Mengru Ding, Lukasz Szpruch, Yue Zhang
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10974)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10974) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10974.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10974.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Reasoning in large language models can be disrupted by simple input perturbations (e.g. adding irrelevant distractors), suggesting reliance on spurious correlations. Identifying and suppressing the spurious features involved in long chain-of-thought reasoning remains challenging. We introduce Spurious-feature WEight EdiTing (SWEET), a training-free method that combines counterfactual interventions with mechanistic interpretability to identify and suppress spurious-features. SWEET extracts token- and sequence-level behavioral directions from activation contrasts between original problems and their counterfactual variants - capturing local and global patterns of spurious reasoning. It then localizes the responsible MLP neurons and attention heads and suppresses their contributions along these directions through weight edits. Using only 20 original-counterfactual pairs for direction extraction, SWEET improves accuracy by an average of 11.8 percentage points on perturbed inputs and 2 points on original inputs across five open-weight reasoning models and five reasoning benchmarks. It also reduces the relative gap between original and perturbed accuracies from 17.6% to 2.5%. Analyses identify two recurring forms of spurious reasoning, i.e. over-integrating irrelevant conditions and overthinking, and provide mechanistic evidence that SWEET improves reasoning robustness by suppressing both behavioral directions.

[arXiv:2611.10819] Selective Cooperative Perception via Blind Spot Reasoning and Agentic Dialogue with Vision-Language Models

  • arXiv ID: 2611.10819 (2611.10819v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-11-07
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.RO, cs.AI, cs.CV, cs.MA
  • Eje Cognitivo: Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)
  • Autores: Chengyuan Ma, Shixiao Liang, Hangyu Li, Zheng Li, Hang Zhou, Keke Long et al.
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10819)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10819) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10819.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10819.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Real-time cooperative perception (CP) in multi-agent traffic environments is highly resource-intensive, which posing scalability challenges for large-scale deployment. Efficiency can be significantly improved by selectively performing CP only in safety-critical scenarios and for key occluding objects. Vision-Language Models (VLMs) are well-suited for complex reasoning in dynamic and uncertain environments, making them a promising tool for this selective CP task. This study proposes a VLM-driven agentic framework for blind spot reasoning and dialogue-based CP. A mixed-model architecture is designed that integrates conventional object detection for stable perception, vision-language reasoning for identifying impactful occlusions, and a physics-based geometric module to align reasoning with real-world visibility constraints. The framework also incorporates a Chain-of-Thought (CoT) prompting mechanism to enhance interpretability and intent-aware decision-making. We collect real-world data from connected vehicles operating in urban traffic and compare the driving-critical blind spot identification and dialogue performance of four state-of-the-art VLMs. Experimental results demonstrate that our method achieves over 80% accuracy in driving-critical blind spot detection and over 90% success in dialogue-driven perception sharing. This work provides a scalable, interpretable, and bandwidth-efficient alternative to conventional CP pipelines, and offers new insights into agentic communication design for future connected traffic systems.

2. [Protocolo de Verificación Intrínseca]: Implementar un paso de auditoría independiente sobre cada afirmación fáctica y cita normativa/jurisprudencial antes de consolidar la respuesta final, rechazando cualquier inferencia no respaldada por evidencia documental directa.

3. [Asignación Dinámica de Cómputo de Inferencia]: Calibrar la profundidad deliberativa según la complejidad del caso: aplicar resolución directa en trámites estandarizados y expandir el árbol de búsqueda (múltiples candidatos + ranking) en casos con colisión normativa o jurisprudencial.

4. [Descomposición Agéntica y Uso de Herramientas]: Dividir la tarea compleja en subtareas atómicas verificables (extracción de hechos -> búsqueda de precedentes -> encuadre legal -> redacción -> control de calidad), invocando herramientas externas de recuperación antes de razonar en abstracto.

[arXiv:2611.10800] Enhancing Mathematical Reasoning in Turkish Large Language Models with Search-Based Inference and Model Merging

  • arXiv ID: 2611.10800 (2611.10800v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-11-07
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.CL, cs.AI
  • Eje Cognitivo: Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)
  • Autores: Ahmed Zeer, Osama Shbib, Muzaffer Kaan Yuce, Himmet Toprak Kesgin, Mehmet Fatih Amasyali
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10800)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10800) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10800.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10800.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> We improve Turkish mathematical reasoning in Large Language Models by combining LoRA fine-tuning, weight-space model merging, and search-based inference. Using Turkish-Llama 8B-DPO with Turkish translations of MetaMath, NuminaMath, and TigerLab, we find data quality beats scale (Mix-30k best among fine-tuned; large MetaMath underperforms). Linear merging delivers the largest gains (GSM8K up to 0.63), while Ties is modest and Breadcrumbs/Slerp degrade. Search with MCTS and Tree of Thoughts improves mean LLM-judge scores for reasoning quality on a 0-100 scale. Limitations include machine translation, limited scale, and search stochasticity.

[arXiv:2611.10609] Reason First, Retrieve What Matters: Hypothesis-Guided Keyframe Selection for Long Video Understanding

  • arXiv ID: 2611.10609 (2611.10609v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-11-07
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.CV, cs.AI
  • Eje Cognitivo: Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)
  • Autores: Yisong Hu, Junbo Wang, Yuke Li, Jiangbin Zheng
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10609)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10609) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10609.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10609.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Long video understanding requires models to identify and reason over sparse, temporally distributed evidence within limited frame budgets. Existing keyframe selection methods typically retrieve query-relevant frames from a densely sampled candidate set before inference. However, global image-text matching incurs unnecessary computation when a small set of uniformly sampled frames already provides sufficient evidence. Query relevance alone may also overlook evidence that distinguishes competing answers, while one-shot selection offers no opportunity to correct earlier selection errors. We propose HypoKFS, a training-free, plug-and-play framework that couples keyframe selection with iterative inference by multimodal large language models (MLLMs). HypoKFS first reasons over sparsely sampled frames to construct an initial hypothesis space. The resulting answer distribution determines whether additional frame selection is needed. When needed, the retained hypotheses guide the selection of discriminative frames, followed by renewed inference and hypothesis refinement. This iterative process directs additional visual computation toward unresolved hypotheses while avoiding unnecessary global frame matching. Experiments on Video-MME, LongVideoBench, and MLVU demonstrate consistent improvements across multiple MLLM backbones. HypoKFS improves accuracy by up to 6.7 percentage points and achieves 72.9% accuracy on Video-MME at only 1.3% of the reference frame-hypothesis matching cost.

Reglas Operativas Extraídas (Aplicación en Inferencia y Razonamiento)

1. [Directiva de Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)]: Estructurar cadenas de razonamiento explícitas (Chain-of-Thought deliberativo) separando estrictamente la premisa fáctica probada, la premisa normativa vigente y el juicio de subsunción, ejecutando un pase de autoverificación contra alucinaciones o saltos lógicos.

2. [Supervisión Granular por Pasos (Step-Level PRM)]: Validar secuencialmente cada considerando o nodo de decisión. Si un paso intermedio presenta incertidumbre o ambigüedad, podar esa rama de razonamiento y recalcular desde el último antecedente verificado.

3. [Asignación Dinámica de Cómputo de Inferencia]: Calibrar la profundidad deliberativa según la complejidad del caso: aplicar resolución directa en trámites estandarizados y expandir el árbol de búsqueda (múltiples candidatos + ranking) en casos con colisión normativa o jurisprudencial.

[arXiv:2611.10552] Seeing Isn't Enough: Unlocking Visual Reasoning by Bridging the Reasoning-Access Gap with TIGER

  • arXiv ID: 2611.10552 (2611.10552v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-11-07
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.AI, cs.CV, cs.LG
  • Eje Cognitivo: Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)
  • Autores: Gregory Kang Ruey Lau, Minh Huynh Nguyen, Bryan Kian Hsiang Low
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10552)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10552) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10552.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10552.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) inherit strong textual reasoning capabilities from their language backbones and text pretraining, but often fail to apply the same level of competency to visual inputs. We show that this degradation cannot be attributed to only perception failures, by evaluating models on semantically-equivalent text and rendered-text image inputs: models can accurately read text in the images and recover near-text performance when provided their own transcriptions, but just cannot reason well on the images directly. We characterize this discrepancy as a modality reasoning-access gap, where models fail to route perceived visual content into existing reasoning machinery accessible with text. To bridge this, we propose TIGER (Text-to-Image Gap-targeted training for Enhanced Reasoning), which first mines synthetic modality counterfactuals where a model succeeds on a text problem but fails when provided the corresponding rendered image, and post-trains the model on the image input via Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO), along with SFT and DPO variants. Importantly, although trained exclusively on synthetic rendered-text images with no multimodal annotations, TIGER results in model performance improvements across diverse real-world visual benchmarks including geometric diagrams (MathVerse), multi-disciplinary sciences (EMMA, MMMU), complex charts (ChartQAPro), and embodied spatial reasoning (RoboSpatial-Home). TIGER also complements existing state-of-the-art RLVR visual reasoning methods, yielding further performance gains. Analysis involving reasoning-subspace activation and activation patching provide support indicating that TIGER helps to align visual-to-reasoning pathways toward text reasoning states. Our implementation is available at https://github.com/minhngh/Tiger.

[arXiv:2611.09891] CPU-ARC: Agentic Reasoning Caching for on-CPU Reasoning Reuse

  • arXiv ID: 2611.09891 (2611.09891v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-11-06
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.AI
  • Eje Cognitivo: Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)
  • Autores: Peter Belcak, Greg Heinrich, Saurav Muralidharan, Shizhe Diao, Pavlo Molchanov
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09891)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09891) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.09891.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.09891.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Large language models reinvent the wheel all the time. They conflate task-solving with the execution of the solutions, which forces them to solve every problem from scratch. We show that once a task has been reasoned through, the application of its solution to a new problem instance can be done very efficiently and with little involvement from language models. We introduce agentic reasoning caching, a method for constructing robust CPU-executable agentic reasoning caches (ARCs) from LLM reasoning traces. ARCs show on-par performance with frontier LLMs across a range of difficult mathematical and scientific tasks, are fully explainable, continually trainable, robust to input perturbations, and 16-291x cheaper to invoke than the source model.
