[arXiv:2611.09511] V-STORM: A Structure-Aware Grounded Chain of Thought Dataset for Faithful and Fine-Grained Spatio-Temporal Video Reasoning

  • arXiv ID: 2611.09511 (2611.09511v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-11-06
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.CV, cs.AI
  • Eje Cognitivo: Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)
  • Autores: Dian Jiao, Yuqian Yuan, Jiaqi Zhu, Mingjian Gao, Siwen Tan, Yunqi Cao et al.
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09511)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09511) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.09511.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.09511.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> While MLLMs can often identify what happens in a video, they still struggle with fine-grained spatio-temporal reasoning, which requires precise grounding when and where the supporting evidence appears and composing such evidence into a coherent reasoning chain. This challenge is exacerbated by the lack of scalable data construction and evaluation pipelines that provide granular, verifiable supervision linking reasoning steps to explicit spatio-temporal evidence.To address this gap, we present V-STORM, a structure-aware framework that transforms videos into Spatio-Temporal Scene Graphs (STSGs) and derives Grounded Chain of Thought (GCoT) from validated evidence paths, making each reasoning step traceable to structured and verifiable spatio-temporal evidence.Under this framework, we construct a 260K-sample training set and a 3K-sample human-verified benchmark, both featuring GCoT annotations that link reasoning to explicit spatio-temporal evidence.Experiments reveal a clear separation between evidence localization and evidence composition: current MLLMs can often identify relevant spatio-temporal cues, yet still fail to integrate them into correct multi-step reasoning, exposing a bottleneck that answer-only evaluation largely obscures. Moreover, fine-tuning with V-STORM substantially improves both answer accuracy and grounding faithfulness, validating its effectiveness as supervision for faithful and evidence-grounded video reasoning. Our dataset, benchmark, and source code will be publicly released.

[arXiv:2611.09414] Typed-Centrality Grounding for Agentic GraphRAG: A Symbolic Approach for Agentic Reasoning

  • arXiv ID: 2611.09414 (2611.09414v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-11-06
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.AI
  • Eje Cognitivo: Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)
  • Autores: Rosario Napoli, Antonio Celesti, Massimo Villari, Maria Fazio
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09414)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09414) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.09414.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.09414.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Knowledge Graphs (KGs) and Large Language Models (LLMs) represent two approaches for knowledge representation and reasoning over heterogeneous data. Recent research has explored their integration through Graph Retrieval-Augmented Generation (GraphRAG) systems, which use KGs to provide structured context to LLMs. However, existing GraphRAG approaches treat KGs as homogeneous graphs and rely on textual similarity between the input prompt and the context, rather than using it as a source of deterministic knowledge. This work proposes an agent-based GraphRAG system that introduces a symbolic layer to integrate deterministic reasoning alongside text similarities. This approach is based on the definition of typed centralities for KGs, where centrality measures are computed separately for each specific KG's class. This information is used to construct a symbolic representation that is provided to the LLM as a deterministic source. The proposed system was mathematically validated and evaluated on a real-world POLE (Person, Object, Location, Event) dataset, comparing reasoning and information retrieval with and without the symbolic layer. The results indicate that the our approach leads to responses that are more consistent with the context, improving the generated outputs.

[arXiv:2611.09082] BrainCoT: A Multi-Task Zero-Shot Brain Signal Foundation Model with Neurometric-Anchored Chain-of-Thought Reasoning

  • arXiv ID: 2611.09082 (2611.09082v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-11-06
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.LG
  • Eje Cognitivo: Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)
  • Autores: Yiyu Gui, Mingzhi Chen, Guibo Luo, Yuchao Yang
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09082)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09082) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.09082.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.09082.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Brain signals exhibit substantial multi-center heterogeneity, strong inter-subject variability, and highly diverse task settings, which makes it difficult to build models that generalize beyond a single task. While recent brain signal foundation models leverage large-scale pretraining to learn generalizable representations, they often fall short in practice because they still require target-data-specific supervised adaptation, lack multi-task zero-shot capability, and rarely provide verifiable evidence to support their predictions. In this work, we present BrainCoT, a multi-task zero-shot Brain signal foundation model with neurometric-anchored Chain-of-Thought (CoT) reasoning. BrainCoT unifies heterogeneous tasks into an instruction-driven framework so that, after pretraining, a single model can be directly applied to multiple tasks in zero-shot settings without additional adaptation. It further introduces neurometric-anchored chain-of-thought grounded in neurometric evidence for verifiable reasoning, and adopts Decision-Consistent Rationale Training to keep process learning aligned with correct predictions. Across extensive evaluations, BrainCoT achieves an average ACC of 67.41% and AUC of 74.33% in zero-shot classification without downstream training data, outperforming the strongest linear-probing baseline trained with 1% labeled downstream data by 3.85% in ACC and 5.97% in AUC.

[arXiv:2611.09061] Does Reasoning Improve Psychological Depth in Large Language Models?

  • arXiv ID: 2611.09061 (2611.09061v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-11-06
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.CL
  • Eje Cognitivo: Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)
  • Autores: Ruichen Zheng, Yihe Wang, Fabrice Harel-Canada, Sara Khosravi, Zeynep Senahan Yildiz, Amit Sahai et al.
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09061)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09061) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.09061.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.09061.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Benchmark gains from reasoning-enhanced large language models encourage the assumption that these models should broadly perform better on complex generative tasks, but this remains underexplored in open-ended, creative domains shaped by nuances of reader experience. We tested this assumption through the lens of psychological depth in short stories. We compared DeepSeek-R1 vs. DeepSeek-V3, our closest available contrast for reasoning-oriented post-training, and GPT-5 vs. GPT-4o, through 60 blinded pairwise evaluations in which seven human annotators and an ensemble LLM-as-a-Judge rated prompt-matched story outputs. Human evaluations showed no universal reasoning advantage: GPT-5 was slightly preferred over GPT-4o (60.0-62.9% across two reasoning-effort settings), whereas DeepSeek-R1 fell behind DeepSeek-V3 (42.9%). Although this pattern was marked by low inter-annotator agreement (Krippendorff's $α= 0.070$), our annotation-quality checks suggested genuine evaluative subjectivity, plausibly reflecting distinct annotator decision schemes rather than annotation failure. In contrast, the LLM-as-a-Judge showed a strong pro-reasoning bias (98.3% of pairs vs. 55.2% of human votes), consistent across its five configurations and partly tracking sentence length. These findings suggest that, at the frontier, reasoning-related quality differences are small enough to be dominated by reader subjectivity, and that LLM-as-a-Judge evaluators can obscure this subjectivity in nuanced creative-generation tasks.

[arXiv:2611.08811] I Think, Therefore I Attack: Evaluating Thought Injection Attacks on Reasoning Agents

  • arXiv ID: 2611.08811 (2611.08811v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-11-06
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.AI, cs.CR
  • Eje Cognitivo: Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)
  • Autores: Parth Illendula, Garik Livingston, Zain Shamsi, Ravi Srinivasan, Alex Liu
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08811)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08811) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.08811.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.08811.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Large Reasoning Models (LRMs) are one of the key components of current agentic systems. LRMs augment their output with an explicit chain-of-thought reasoning stage, introducing a new attack surface for thought injection, in which untrusted text resembling the model's own deliberation is fed back to the model. We present a two-part study of this attack, performed against the prevailing large, production class open-weight models (e.g., GLM-5.3, MiniMax-M2.5, Qwen3.8-Flash-Next). We first evaluate the effectiveness of static thought-injection prompts under different styles as well as tagged and untagged conditions and find that the effects of thinking tags and payload styles are all properties of the specific target model. In the second study, we exploit this model-specificity in a containerized agentic cyber range, using indirect injection prompts styled in the form of the target model's own reasoning traces to achieve attack success rates (ASR) up to 50% on operational objectives such as reverse-shell exfiltration and repository backdooring. We also find that the same malicious objective in standard prompt injection user voice never succeeds, and thinking tags suppress the agent's tendency to disclose the injection. Our results show that imitating a reasoning model's own thought style is effective for this style of attack, and that tag stripping is an unreliable defense.

[arXiv:2611.08403] StepBack: Step-Level Error-Localized Resampling for Efficient Test-Time Reasoning

  • arXiv ID: 2611.08403 (2611.08403v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-11-05
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.AI, cs.CL, cs.LG
  • Eje Cognitivo: Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)

  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08403)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08403) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.08403.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.08403.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Self-consistency (SC) is a widely used decoding strategy for improving chain-of-thought reasoning, but it requires sampling many complete reasoning trajectories with a preset budget. Its efficient variants reduce the cost mainly by stopping full-trajectory sampling early or distributing the number of samples across questions. In this paper, we propose StepBack, a step-level error-localized resampling method for efficient test-time reasoning. Given a small set of draft trajectories, StepBack localizes likely first error positions using lightweight signals and then resamples the detected error step and its suffix, followed by majority answer voting. Empirically, StepBack matches the accuracy of SC on average across seven mathematical reasoning and multi-hop QA benchmarks, while reducing token usage by up to 41.26%. In addition, by nature, StepBack can be combined with adaptive inference methods such as Adaptive Consistency, further improving their inference-time efficiency.

Reglas Operativas Extraídas (Aplicación en Inferencia y Razonamiento)

1. [Directiva de Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)]: Estructurar cadenas de razonamiento explícitas (Chain-of-Thought deliberativo) separando estrictamente la premisa fáctica probada, la premisa normativa vigente y el juicio de subsunción, ejecutando un pase de autoverificación contra alucinaciones o saltos lógicos.

2. [Supervisión Granular por Pasos (Step-Level PRM)]: Validar secuencialmente cada considerando o nodo de decisión. Si un paso intermedio presenta incertidumbre o ambigüedad, podar esa rama de razonamiento y recalcular desde el último antecedente verificado.

3. [Asignación Dinámica de Cómputo de Inferencia]: Calibrar la profundidad deliberativa según la complejidad del caso: aplicar resolución directa en trámites estandarizados y expandir el árbol de búsqueda (múltiples candidatos + ranking) en casos con colisión normativa o jurisprudencial.

4. [Búsqueda Deliberativa y Consenso Interno]: Generar y contrastar múltiples líneas argumentales (argumento principal, eventuales defensas y criterio de Alzada) seleccionando la solución que maximice la coherencia interna y la estabilidad frente a una eventual apelación.

[arXiv:2611.08315] Benchmarking Large Language Models on Heterogeneous Cognitive and Biomedical Knowledge Graphs: The Role of Graph Grounding in Multi-Hop Reasoning and Hallucination Mitigation

  • arXiv ID: 2611.08315 (2611.08315v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-11-05
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.AI, cs.CL, cs.LG
  • Eje Cognitivo: Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)
  • Autores: Ali Sarabadani, Kheirolah Rahsepar Fard, Tahereh Jeirani
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08315)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08315) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.08315.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.08315.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Large language models (LLMs) are increasingly used in biomedical and cognitive neuroscience, but their ability to reason reliably over heterogeneous knowledge graphs (KGs) remains unclear. We benchmark five LLMs (GPT-5.1, Gemini 2.0 Pro, Llama 3, Qwen, and BioGPT) across five KGs: DrugBank, NeuroSynth, Cognitive Atlas, Brain Map, and GDPKG. We introduce a multitier query dataset to evaluate reasoning depth and factual accuracy under KG-aware (graph-grounded) and KG-agnostic conditions. Inter-annotator agreement was substantial, with Cohen's kappa of 0.781 (range: 0.753-0.807) and Fleiss' kappa of 0.77. Results show that general-purpose models, particularly GPT-5.1 and Gemini 2.0 Pro, generally outperform domain-specific models on multi-hop and high-level reasoning tasks, although performance declines as query complexity increases. KG grounding reduces hallucination rates across all evaluated models. Contrary to our initial hypothesis, domain-specific models do not consistently outperform general-purpose models on specialized tasks without graph grounding. These findings highlight the value of combining broad language-model capabilities with structured domain knowledge. We provide practical guidance for selecting LLM-KG integration strategies based on task complexity and computational constraints, and discuss hybrid neurosymbolic architectures for personalized medicine, cognitive disorder diagnosis, and explainable AI.

[arXiv:2611.08070] Can an LLM Actually Reason and Program about Neural Network's Weights or is it just memorization on steroids? Exploring "Weight-Space Programming"

  • arXiv ID: 2611.08070 (2611.08070v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-11-05
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.LG, cs.AI
  • Eje Cognitivo: Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)
  • Autores: Nazmus Sakib Ahmed
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08070)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08070) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.08070.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.08070.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Large language models (LLMs) have recently been reported to read and write the raw weights of neural networks directly, a capability termed weight-space programming. We ask whether the reading side of this capability reflects reasoning about weight flows or a combination of local arithmetic at the network's edges and memorized priors over public datasets. We test Claude Opus 5.5 on small convolutional networks of about 125,000 parameters trained on five image datasets, providing every weight as plain text with no tools, no forward passes, and no dataset or class names. The model performs accurate local calculations: batch-normalization statistics lead it to infer about three optimizer steps on networks stopped after three updates, and output-layer sign patterns recover the vehicle/animal split of CIFAR-10. The sessions report reading the first and last layers closely and sampling the middle, and several claims built on partial tracing fail verification, although we did not run a direct forward-pass test. Semantic judgments lean on familiar datasets: the model names CIFAR-10 for CIFAR-10 networks but also for all three fast-food networks, relabels KMNIST as MNIST, reads a 99.57%-accurate SAT-6 network as CIFAR-10 animal photos, and calls "frog" the output favored by a feature that fires most on trees. Permuting CIFAR-10's labels preserves the output split at the new indices, but every class pair the model interprets is then wrong, indicating reliance on remembered label order. These are exploratory results from mostly single runs. We conclude that zero-shot weight reading by this model is better described as local arithmetic plus dataset priors than as weight-flow tracing, and we provide a protocol for auditing future weight-space claims.

[arXiv:2611.07866] Do Recommenders Need to Think in Words? Latent Reasoning for Efficient LLM-Based Recommendation

  • arXiv ID: 2611.07866 (2611.07866v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-11-05
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.CL
  • Eje Cognitivo: Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)
  • Autores: Hongyang Su, Beibei Kong, Lei Cheng, Chengxiang Zhuo, Zang Li
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07866)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07866) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.07866.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.07866.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> LLM-based recommender systems increasingly generate chain-of-thought (CoT) text before each item prediction to activate the LLM's reasoning capacity, costing tens to hundreds of extra tokens per recommendation at inference. We ask whether inference-time reasoning artifacts (textual or latent) can be replaced by training-time supervision alone, without loss of accuracy. We propose LatentRec, a framework for LLM-based generative recommendation that replaces inference-time reasoning artifacts with training-time per-step head supervision. LatentRec has three components. A 2.5M-parameter Continuous Thought Module iterates K latent reasoning steps over the LLM's hidden state during training. Soft Token Selection bridges the ~14x norm gap between hidden states and input embeddings. A Per-step Reasoning Loss (PRL) supervises every intermediate state through the LLM's prediction head against the first token of the target Semantic ID. The trained LLM retains this accuracy without the module at inference, so the module can be discarded at deployment. On three Amazon benchmarks, LatentRec improves HR@10 by 4.0-5.0% (relative) over a CoT-distilled baseline at 18.3x lower latency (batch=1). Per-step training supervision consistently improves accuracy across three datasets, paid once at training rather than at every recommendation.

[arXiv:2611.07689] MCPHallu: Benchmarking Reasoning, Execution, and Memory Hallucinations in MCP Agents

  • arXiv ID: 2611.07689 (2611.07689v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-11-05
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.AI
  • Eje Cognitivo: Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)
  • Autores: Yizhen Jiang, Haowei Guo, Tianyi Bai, Zengjie Hu, Lu Ma, Zhengyang Zhao et al.
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07689)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07689) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.07689.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.07689.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Large language model agents are increasingly deployed through the Model Context Protocol (MCP), which allows them to discover and invoke tools across multiple servers at runtime. Existing MCP benchmarks primarily measure task completion; where they diagnose failures, they do so after execution rather than building specific reasoning, execution, and memory failure risks into task construction. We introduce MCPHallu, a benchmark for diagnosing hallucination-prone agent failures in MCP environments along three axes: reasoning, execution, and memory. MCPHallu contains 358 tasks across five capability domains and runs on 36 production MCP servers in isolated containers. Each task is designed with a structural trigger that makes one of four subtypes the dominant failure risk: Branch Collapse, Unreachable Goal, Tool Misuse, or Context Forgetting. This design enables subtype-level analysis rather than aggregate task success alone. We evaluate fourteen frontier LLM agents from eight vendors on 5{,}012 trajectories. Current MCP agents remain vulnerable to hallucination-inducing tool environments, achieving an average score of 0.642 on a $[0,1]$ scale (higher is better). Failures concentrate on Unreachable Goal tasks, where the average score drops to 0.458 and 23.2\% of runs pass, indicating that agents often continue issuing tool calls or claim successful completion rather than recognizing infeasible goals. Subtype rankings diverge sharply: a model's rank shifts by 6.9 of 13 positions on average across subtypes, and Branch Collapse is uncorrelated with Unreachable Goal across models ($r = -0.12$). MCPHallu is released at https://github.com/J1zz/mcp_hallu.

[arXiv:2611.07541] CST-Bench: Benchmarking Counterfactual Spatial Transition Reasoning with Causal Transition On-Policy Self-Distillation

  • arXiv ID: 2611.07541 (2611.07541v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-11-05
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.AI
  • Eje Cognitivo: Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)
  • Autores: Ying Li, Yongle Cheng, Jing Li, Xingquan Cai, Yawei Luo
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07541)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07541) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.07541.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.07541.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Spatial reasoning is a fundamental capability of multimodal large language models (MLLMs), yet existing benchmarks mainly focus on static scene understanding. When objects are moved or swapped, models must infer not only spatial relations but also how interventions change the scene state. To address this problem, we introduce CST-Bench, a benchmark for counterfactual spatial reasoning. CST-Bench contains 7,082 examples covering 6 intervention types and 12 transformation operators, with dense causal annotations including intervention sources, affected relations, expected effects, invariant relations, and metric changes. We benchmark general-purpose MLLMs on CST-Bench and further compare post-training strategies on CST-Bench and standard spatial benchmarks. Results show that methods effective for ordinary spatial reasoning can still fail under counterfactual interventions. We further formulate counterfactual spatial reasoning as causal state-transition modeling and propose CT-OPSD, which uses CST-Bench annotations to guide a teacher model in learning counterfactual state transitions and causal reasoning chains. These chains are then distilled into a student model that reasons only from real observations. Overall, experiment results establish CST-Bench as a challenging benchmark and reusable supervision resource for counterfactual spatial reasoning. CT-OPSD further provides an initial demonstration of how its annotations can support model improvement.

[arXiv:2611.07486] MEvince: Knowledge-Constrained LLM Reasoning for Technique Identification from Malware Reports

  • arXiv ID: 2611.07486 (2611.07486v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-11-05
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.CR, cs.CL, cs.IR
  • Eje Cognitivo: Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)
  • Autores: Hongyu She, Yiling He, Mengda Xie, Yiping Liu, Xingzhi Qian, Zhan Qin et al.
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07486)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07486) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.07486.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.07486.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Mapping behavioral intelligence in malware analysis reports to ATT&CK techniques supports threat analysis and defense, but reliable identification remains challenging. Supporting evidence is often scattered across long, heterogeneous reports, while closely related techniques share behavioral cues and differ only in subtle but decisive details. While large language models (LLMs) can use external knowledge to interpret reports, they may assign techniques based on plausible associations rather than sufficient evidence. We present MEvince, a knowledge-constrained LLM reasoning framework for report-level malware technique identification. Its core is a shared behavioral representation connecting report observations to technique definitions. This representation is built through agentic mining of technique documentation and supports two online stages: 1) primitive-guided extraction uses behavioral primitives to structure LLM-extracted observations, link them to source passages, and group dispersed evidence; 2) evidence-aligned retrieval matches this evidence to technique profiles through the same concepts, retaining likely candidates and confusable alternatives for LLM comparison of distinguishing behaviors. Controls at both stages validate evidence references and check technique-specific requirements. The representation also supports adaptation without parameter updates, where offline agentic audits diagnose concept weaknesses and refine extraction or retrieval policies. We evaluate MEvince against 11 baselines spanning four method families using a dataset of 610 public malware reports referenced by MITRE ATT&CK. MEvince improves exact-ID F1 over the strongest baseline by 17.4%, and its concept-guided retrieval increases gold-label candidate coverage from 31.6% to 47.9%. With a small adaptation set, offline refinement yields further gains and outperforms baselines that update model parameters.

[arXiv:2611.07482] SAVeR$^2$: Reasoning-based Safety Alignment for Large Reasoning Models via Verifiable Rewards

  • arXiv ID: 2611.07482 (2611.07482v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-11-05
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.AI
  • Eje Cognitivo: Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)
  • Autores: Yichen Sun, Qirui Liu, Yan Wang, Jianan Lin, Linbo Jiang, Shiyu Wang et al.
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07482)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07482) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.07482.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.07482.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Large Reasoning Models (LRMs) have achieved impressive performance via explicit Chain-of-Thought (CoT) reasoning, yet this process introduces critical safety risks. We formalize LRM safety objectives as requiring non-malicious reasoning and answers, safety consistency, and explicit intent identification. We argue that existing alignment methods suffer from poor adversarial generalization, a significant ``safety tax'' on reasoning, and unfaithful coarse-grained rewards, primarily due to their neglect of the LRM's intrinsic reasoning capabilities for safety. To address these, we propose \textbf{SAVeR$^2$}, a novel \textbf{S}afety \textbf{A}lignment method via \textbf{R}ewards with \textbf{R}easoning capability. SAVeR$^2$ decomposes the safety objective into four fine-grained verifiable reward signals and utilizes a difficulty-aware data selection strategy to stabilize training. Crucially, we introduce a reasoning-preserving gradient projection mechanism that analytically resolves conflicts between safety and reasoning gradients, effectively eliminating the safety tax. Extensive experiments on models ranging from 1.5B to 671B demonstrate that SAVeR$^2$ achieves superior safety performance and significantly lower over-refusal rates across multiple benchmarks without compromising general reasoning capabilities.

[arXiv:2611.07408] PSGAgent: Enhancing MLLMs' Dynamic Visual Perception for Panoptic Scene Graph Generation via Agentic Reasoning

  • arXiv ID: 2611.07408 (2611.07408v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-11-05
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.CV, cs.AI
  • Eje Cognitivo: Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)
  • Autores: Qingyun Liu, Yuankun Liu, Jiaye Feng, Jie Huang, Zonghong Dai, Haochen Li et al.
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07408)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07408) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.07408.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.07408.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Panoptic Scene Graph generation (PSG) requires holistic yet fine-grained reasoning over complex relations among objects. However, current Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) applied to PSG suffer from a perception bottleneck: they function as static observers limited by fixed-resolution views. As a result, models often overlook critical relations involving small or peripheral objects and struggle to differentiate ambiguous predicates in complex scenes. To address these limitations, we propose PSGAgent, an agentic framework that enhances visual perception for PSG through a think-act-observe loop. PSGAgent allows MLLMs to dynamically refine their visual perception using two tools: a Region Zoom-In Tool for local exploration of small or boundary relations and a Visual Highlight Tool for relation-focused segmentation that filters out irrelevant contextual noise. To optimize decision-making, we construct a two-stage training pipeline. First, we create a high-quality, error-aware Multimodal Chain-of-Thought (M-CoT) dataset that provides robust supervision for cold-start learning. Then, we design a novel hierarchical reward mechanism integrated with Group Sequence Policy Optimization (GSPO) to enable strategic and adaptive tool usage. Extensive experiments on the PSG benchmark show that PSGAgent achieves SOTA performance, demonstrating strong capability in both holistic and fine-grained scene understanding.

  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06606)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06606) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.06606.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.06606.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Large language models can solve many multi-step reasoning problems, but their reasoning remains brittle: plausible intermediate steps often lead to incorrect answers. Improving reasoning at test time requires balancing two imperfect signals: the generator, which provides diversity, and the verifier, which guides correctness. Existing approaches largely favor one side of this tradeoff. Verifier-weighted voting relies on trajectories sampled from the generator and cannot steer generation toward correct ones. By contrast, beam-style verifier-guided search can steer generation, but may over-concentrate on narrow trajectory sets and amplify verifier errors. We propose Verifier-Guided Self-Consistency (VGSC), a stochastic decoding framework that combines step-level correctness and future-outcome estimates to guide reasoning during generation. VGSC repeatedly proposes candidate next steps from the base language model and uses both signals in an acceptance/resampling procedure, while maintaining multiple trajectories for final answer-level self-consistency. We provide a probabilistic interpretation of VGSC as approximate sampling from a verifier-tilted trajectory distribution. Across multiple reasoning benchmarks, generator models, and verifier families, VGSC achieves higher average accuracy than representative baselines.

Reglas Operativas Extraídas (Aplicación en Inferencia y Razonamiento)

1. [Directiva de Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)]: Estructurar cadenas de razonamiento explícitas (Chain-of-Thought deliberativo) separando estrictamente la premisa fáctica probada, la premisa normativa vigente y el juicio de subsunción, ejecutando un pase de autoverificación contra alucinaciones o saltos lógicos.

2. [Protocolo de Verificación Intrínseca]: Implementar un paso de auditoría independiente sobre cada afirmación fáctica y cita normativa/jurisprudencial antes de consolidar la respuesta final, rechazando cualquier inferencia no respaldada por evidencia documental directa.

3. [Supervisión Granular por Pasos (Step-Level PRM)]: Validar secuencialmente cada considerando o nodo de decisión. Si un paso intermedio presenta incertidumbre o ambigüedad, podar esa rama de razonamiento y recalcular desde el último antecedente verificado.

4. [Búsqueda Deliberativa y Consenso Interno]: Generar y contrastar múltiples líneas argumentales (argumento principal, eventuales defensas y criterio de Alzada) seleccionando la solución que maximice la coherencia interna y la estabilidad frente a una eventual apelación.

[arXiv:2611.06438] Understanding the Out-of-Distribution Generalization of Chain-of-Thought Reasoning in LLMs

  • arXiv ID: 2611.06438 (2611.06438v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-11-04
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.LG, stat.ML
  • Eje Cognitivo: Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)
  • Autores: Nuo-Jing Liang, Xiao-Tong Yuan, Pan Zhou
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06438)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06438) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.06438.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.06438.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Chain-of-Thought (CoT) reasoning substantially improves Large Language Models (LLMs), yet its out-of-distribution (OOD) generalization mechanism remains theoretically underexplored. We study CoT as compositional OOD generalization: the target task lacks complete reasoning trajectories or direct input-output pairs, while training provides only in-distribution (ID) atomic step data. We show that CoT generalizes by repeatedly applying a shared single-step predictor to compose reusable ID transitions. Theoretically, let $T$ denote the number of reasoning steps in the target task, $n$ the number of training samples per sub-task, and $k$ the number of output tokens per step; then the OOD task risk is controlled by the sum of the ID sub-task risks, scaled by a coefficient of order $\mathcal{O}(k)$ that is independent of $T$, although the summed risk itself may still grow with $T$. A Rademacher-complexity analysis further shows that the estimation term is of order $\mathcal{O}(\sqrt{T/n})$, rather than linear in $T$, due to predictor sharing under a uniform capacity control on the shared class. For autoregressive Next-Token Prediction, the final-task risk is bounded by the best-in-class step-level risk scaled by the same coefficient of order $\mathcal{O}(k)$, plus an estimation term of order $\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\big(k\sqrt{T/n}\big)$. A parity-coupled construction further shows that, for the sub-task empirical risk minimizer, the expected final-task risk is at least the best in-class risk plus $Ω(\sqrt{T/n})$ when $T\le n$. Experiments on Atom-Task and GSM8K support the theory: CoT enables short-to-long and compositional generalization, while explicit state tracking is crucial. Our code is available at https://github.com/maxliang114514/CoT-OOD.

Reglas Operativas Extraídas (Aplicación en Inferencia y Razonamiento)

1. 2. [Supervisión Granular por Pasos (Step-Level PRM)]: Validar secuencialmente cada considerando o nodo de decisión. Si un paso intermedio presenta incertidumbre o ambigüedad, podar esa rama de razonamiento y recalcular desde el último antecedente verificado.

3. [Asignación Dinámica de Cómputo de Inferencia]: Calibrar la profundidad deliberativa según la complejidad del caso: aplicar resolución directa en trámites estandarizados y expandir el árbol de búsqueda (múltiples candidatos + ranking) en casos con colisión normativa o jurisprudencial.

[arXiv:2611.06259] A Systematic Benchmark for Evaluating Multimodal Personalized Reasoning of Vision-Language Models

  • arXiv ID: 2611.06259 (2611.06259v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-11-04
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.AI, cs.CV

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> People often make decisions by holistically reasoning over heterogeneous forms of personal information. In this paper, we study the relatively underexplored capability of multimodal personalized reasoning in multimodal large language models (MLLMs). To this end, we introduce \texttt{MPRBench}, a novel and comprehensive benchmark specifically designed to evaluate personalized reasoning across a wide range of tasks and real-world challenges. \texttt{MPRBench} consists of 12 sub-tasks and additionally supports interpretable analysis of representative error cases. Through extensive experiments, we find that current personalized MLLMs still struggle with personalized reasoning due to several key challenges, including inaccurate recognition of personalized concepts and sensitivity to irrelevant personal information. We hope that MPRBench will stimulate further research on personalized reasoning in MLLMs. Code and data are available at https://github.com/kkdh15/MPRBench

[arXiv:2611.06108] Learning When to Think Aloud in Latent Reasoning

  • arXiv ID: 2611.06108 (2611.06108v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-11-04
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.LG
  • Eje Cognitivo: Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)
  • Autores: Zhehao Zhou, Tongrui Su, Shengyu Zhu, Da Li, Zeyu Liu, Jun Zhu et al.
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06108)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06108) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.06108.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.06108.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Latent Chain-of-Thought (CoT) reasoning improves efficiency by shifting intermediate computation into continuous hidden states. However, fully latent methods often struggle on precision-critical tasks such as mathematical reasoning, while existing hybrid approaches retain substantial token-generation overhead. We introduce VITAL, an importance-guided hybrid reasoning framework that selectively verbalizes informative tokens as reasoning anchors while keeping most computation latent. A lightweight gating head dynamically selects between explicit token generation and direct hidden-state feedback. VITAL constructs hybrid trajectories from explicit CoT using token- importance estimation and trains the model through curriculum-based supervised training, followed by reinforcement learning with a reward that balances correctness and conciseness. Experiments show that VITAL consistently out- performs existing fully latent reasoning methods, achieving relative average accuracy gains of 9.6% on GPT-2 and 5.1% on Llama-3.2-1B at comparable reasoning lengths. Compared with hybrid baselines, VITAL achieves higher reasoning accuracy with substantially shorter reasoning trajectories on Qwen3-1.7B.

[arXiv:2611.05953] Graph-Mediated Large Language Model Systems in the Agentic Era: Reasoning, Knowledge, Retrieval, Execution, and Coordination

  • arXiv ID: 2611.05953 (2611.05953v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-11-04
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.AI
  • Eje Cognitivo: Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)
  • Autores: Haihui Liu, Yang Chen, Zice Qiu, Yun Zhang, Jialun Zhong, Wenhua Wang et al.
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05953)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05953) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.05953.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.05953.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Graphs play an increasingly important role in large language model (LLM) systems, supporting structural reasoning, knowledge grounding, retrieval, and memory. As these systems evolve toward the agentic era, graphs also organize execution dependencies and multi-agent communication, linking intermediate observations and retained information to subsequent decisions. However, understanding their contribution requires distinguishing the function of each graph operation, the component responsible for its computation, and the subsequent use of its output. This survey introduces an operation-level role--locus taxonomy for reasoning, knowledge and evidence, retrieval and memory, execution, and coordination. We compare how graph observations and external computations guide decisions, how memory representations and updates support later tasks, and how dependencies constrain execution and information delivery. These comparisons distinguish improvements in local metrics from downstream task performance while retaining the conditions under which results were reported. We then organize benchmarks, comparison designs, process diagnostics, and system costs into an evaluation framework. Finally, we identify open challenges in adaptive computation and stopping, memory maintenance, and dependency-aware recovery. The survey provides a basis for assessing when graph mechanisms are useful and for evaluating their effects across components and stages.

[arXiv:2611.05345] Adversarial Speculative Reasoning Improves Long Horizon Agents

  • arXiv ID: 2611.05345 (2611.05345v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-11-03
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.AI
  • Eje Cognitivo: Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)
  • Autores: Hongyin Luo, Wei Fang, Lijie Yang, Owen Stepan, Daniel Suit, Jeff Krapf et al.
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05345)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05345) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.05345.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.05345.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Speculative decoding has been treated purely as a systems optimization that speeds up large language model decoding without changing the model behavior. Under this view, the growing effort to train better draft models has been focused solely on acceptance rate. We argue that this view does not hold true for long-horizon agent reasoning. Measuring frontier open-weight models on complex agentic tasks with different speculative decoding approaches, we found that numerical noise grows within verification windows, and the draft acceptance results of the thinking process can change the output distribution of the model after 30 steps, thus noticeably influencing the reasoning accuracy of long-horizon agents in random directions. In this work, we improve the reasoning capability of target models by controlling such noise. While we cannot change the overall token distribution verified by the target model, we can encode critical reasoning with highest precision by training the draft model to be rejected. We identify these points in agent traces and train the draft model to favor adversarial preference. At inference the trained draft and the target part ways precisely at these decision points, verification restarts, so that critical thinking tokens are sampled from the target model and encoded under its least noisy conditions. This approach makes critical reasoning better indexed and more durable over a long horizon. On complex agentic coding benchmarks, our approach raises end-to-end success rate with no change to the target model or its output distribution. Our results recast the speculative decoding mechanism from a pure accelerator into an active allocator for context precision that improves the accuracy of long-horizon agents by training 0.06% to 0.28% of hosted parameters.

[arXiv:2611.04814] Think Less, Locally: Local Preference Learning for Reasoning Compression

  • arXiv ID: 2611.04814 (2611.04814v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-11-03
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.AI, cs.CL
  • Eje Cognitivo: Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)
  • Autores: Muyao Yuan, Liyan Xu, Weizhan Zhang, Muyan Jiao, Jiangnan Li, Jinchao Zhang et al.
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04814)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04814) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.04814.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.04814.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Large reasoning models achieve strong performance on challenging tasks, but their long chain-of-thought often incurs substantial inference costs by continuing to deliberate after sufficient information has been obtained. Existing reasoning compression methods primarily operate on reasoning trajectories, paying less attention to the local decision of whether further reasoning is necessary given the current state. We propose LoCo, a local preference learning method for reasoning compression. LoCo detects candidate overthinking states from the model's own traces, freezes the corresponding reasoning prefixes, regenerates shorter verified continuations from the same states, and trains on the resulting state-conditioned preference pairs with DPO. This formulation teaches the model when to continue reasoning and when to conclude, rather than simply encouraging shorter trajectories overall. We construct training data entirely from self-generated mathematical traces and evaluate LoCo on five mathematical and three code generation benchmarks. LoCo reduces completion length by 41.7\%--45.4\% on mathematics and 23.4\%--36.3\% on code, while maintaining or improving accuracy and achieving the best AUC across all code benchmarks. LoCo consistently improves the accuracy-efficiency trade-off on mathematics and transfers effectively to code generation despite math-only training, demonstrating the cross-domain generality of local preference learning for reasoning compression. The code and model will be publicly released at https://github.com/MuyaoYuan/LoCo.

[arXiv:2611.04787] ReSI-Math: Exploring Recoverable Capability Frontiers for Efficient Reasoning Improvement

  • arXiv ID: 2611.04787 (2611.04787v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-11-03
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.CL, cs.AI
  • Eje Cognitivo: Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)
  • Autores: Yao Cui, Zhen Yang, Yuandong Wang, Yangfu Zhu, Tianyu Wo, Zhenzhou Shao
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04787)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04787) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.04787.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.04787.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards (RLVR) has become an effective approach for improving mathematical reasoning in large language models. However, a fundamental question remains: when a model fails to solve a problem, does it truly lack the underlying capability? In this work, we reveal that direct failure does not necessarily imply inability. Some reasoning failures can be recovered by exploring the model's own trajectories, exposing a recoverable capability frontier: a policy-dependent region where solutions are not reliably generated through direct decoding but can be elicited through trajectory-level search. Based on this observation, we introduce ReSI-Math, a framework that explores recoverable capability frontiers and converts recovered capabilities into data-efficient RLVR supervision. ReSI-Math uses Self-Judge-guided evolutionary search to identify recoverable failures under a fixed policy and adaptively selects frontier problems for sequential RLVR as the policy evolves. On Qwen3-VL-8B, correct-incorrect trajectory crossover produces 272 verifier-confirmed correct offspring from 621 attempts (43.8%). After three sequential one-shot RLVR stages, the MATH500 and WE-MATH average improves from 78.71 to 80.16. Under matched single-problem RLVR, random selection reaches only 53.31 versus 54.16 for frontier-guided Q1.

[arXiv:2611.03882] ProofPaths: An AI Agent Framework for Assessing Student Reasoning

  • arXiv ID: 2611.03882 (2611.03882v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-11-02
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.AI
  • Eje Cognitivo: Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)
  • Autores: Mohsen Hariri, Vipin Chaudhary
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03882)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03882) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.03882.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.03882.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Students can use large language models to complete assignments, but a correct solution does not establish whether they understand the reasoning behind it. Individualized interviews can probe that understanding but require recurring staff effort. We introduce ProofPaths, an AI agentic framework that combines rubric-based grading with individualized and interactive interviews. Multiple grading agents assess a submission against instructor guidance, exchange critiques, and revise their judgments. Their scores determine which review supplies the grade and feedback. An interview agent asks students to justify steps in their submission and uses their answers to guide follow-up questions. Each question is linked to a retrieved submission excerpt, so instructors can compare the student's explanation with the cited passage. The system tracks assessment progress and stores grading reviews and interview transcripts for each submission. Several deterministic tests of the assessment controllers check source handling, score aggregation, and limits on correction attempts. Instructors can inspect all reviews and transcripts and override grades within the assignment workflow.

[arXiv:2611.03839] PianoBench: Evaluating Musical Perception and Reasoning in Language Models

  • arXiv ID: 2611.03839 (2611.03839v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-11-02
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.SD, cs.AI, cs.LG
  • Eje Cognitivo: Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)
  • Autores: Louis Bradshaw, Irmak Bukey, Daniel Chenyu Lin, John Thickstun, Chris Donahue
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03839)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03839) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.03839.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.03839.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Multimodal language models continue to improve on broad music understanding benchmarks, but which musical features can they perceive and reason about? We investigate this question by introducing PianoBench, a benchmark that tasks language models with reasoning about musical performances across congruent acoustic and symbolic input representations. PianoBench is derived from our new multimodal, training-scale dataset comprising more than 20,000 hours of interleaved transcripts of music and spoken musical commentary from instructional piano videos. Open-weight audio-language models achieve at most 52.8% accuracy on PianoBench, and at best only modestly outperform their accuracy on a no-audio control. In contrast, frontier models using chain of thought achieve up to 93.2% accuracy when provided transcripts of music as text, exposing a gap between perceiving musical features from audio and reasoning about musical events when they are given explicitly. These experiments suggest that instructional videos may represent an overlooked source of multimodal supervision for music. Available: https://gclef-cmu.org/pianobench.

[arXiv:2611.03834] Low-Rank Hierarchical Merging for Efficient Long-to-Short Reasoning

  • arXiv ID: 2611.03834 (2611.03834v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-11-02
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.AI
  • Eje Cognitivo: Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)
  • Autores: Zeqiu Yu, Xuesheng Zhang, Wenxiao Zhao, Shixiao Wang
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03834)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03834) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.03834.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.03834.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Long-to-Short (L2S) model merging seeks to combine the strong reasoning capability of long chain-of-thought models with the concise style of base models, but existing methods that rely on linear interpolation and first-order activation statistics fail to capture how the loss landscape responds to parameter displacement, leading to degraded accuracy on hard tasks. We propose Low-Rank Hierarchical Merging (LHM), a training-free framework that replaces heuristic statistics with lightweight second-order curvature information. LHM consists of three components: (1) a low-rank Hessian approximation that efficiently estimates per-layer Hessian traces via Hutchinson's estimator on random inputs, eliminating the need for task-specific data; (2) a Layer Second-Order Sensitivity (LSOS) index that combines Hessian trace with task-vector norm to quantify per-layer fusion difficulty; and (3) a Dual-Constraint Coefficient Mapping (DCCM) that converts LSOS scores into layer-adaptive merge coefficients while jointly satisfying a fusion-error bound and a global length-reduction target. Experiments across six mathematical benchmarks on 1.5B and 7B models show that LHM outperforms state-of-the-art methods such as ACM-TIES and ORCA, achieving over 4\% accuracy gain and up to 93.1\% reduction in output length relative to the long-CoT model.

2. [Asignación Dinámica de Cómputo de Inferencia]: Calibrar la profundidad deliberativa según la complejidad del caso: aplicar resolución directa en trámites estandarizados y expandir el árbol de búsqueda (múltiples candidatos + ranking) en casos con colisión normativa o jurisprudencial.

[arXiv:2611.03040] VERS: Steering Reasoning Models by Editing Their Activation Explanations

  • arXiv ID: 2611.03040 (2611.03040v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-11-02
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.CL, cs.LG
  • Eje Cognitivo: Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)
  • Autores: Ihor Vitenko
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03040)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03040) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.03040.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.03040.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Reasoning language models write a chain of thought before they answer, and many checks in it do not change the final answer. Natural language autoencoders (NLAs) explain a model's activations in text and rebuild activations from text, and editing an explanation can steer a model at a single token. We study whether such edits can also steer how a model reasons. We build VERS (Verbalize, Edit, Reconstruct, Steer): it turns explanation edits at the doubt points of one reasoning trace into one fixed direction, and adds this direction at paragraph breaks. On the full GSM8K test, VERS cuts generated tokens by 45.8% at an accuracy change of -0.82 percentage points (pp); on MATH-500 it saves 31.3% at -3.05 pp. At half strength, its trade-off between tokens and accuracy lies between those of the published methods SEAL and DEER. With fitted affine maps and a strength tuned for each model, the direction transfers to larger Qwen3 models, up to 235B-A22B, and saves 30-35% of tokens. On competition problems, however, VERS loses 3-7 pp at every model size, and a fixed direction did not separate necessary checks from unneeded ones.

[arXiv:2611.03007] Q-CamTuning: A Multimodal Large Language Model for Fine-Grained Quality Reasoning in Livestreaming Camera Tuning

  • arXiv ID: 2611.03007 (2611.03007v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-11-02
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.CV, cs.AI
  • Eje Cognitivo: Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)
  • Autores: Yanchao Bi, Pengfei Chen, Yulong Yan, Qian Zhang, Biao Wang, Leida Li

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> With the popularization of livestreaming applications, automatic camera tuning has become indispensable for ordinary users to enjoy high Quality of Experience (QoE). However, the existing Image Quality Assessment (IQA) methods face two challenges in camera tuning. First, they struggle to identify subtle quality difference between images, which is common in camera tuning. Second, the ability to explain fine-grained differences and to provide suggestions for camera tuning are even more lacking. To address these challenges, we first construct FGLive-Comments, an instruction tuning dataset with rich annotations of both preferences for fine-grained image pairs and quality reasoning from global and local visual evidence, which supports selective adjustment recommendations of camera parameters. Based on FGLive-Comments, we develop Q-CamTuning, a multimodal large language model for livestreaming camera tuning. Its progressive perception-evidence-guidance framework learns quality-aware visual representations, enhances fine-grained reasoning with global-local evidence, and maps quality issues to camera-parameter adjustment directions. Extensive experimental results demonstrate that Q-CamTuning outperforms existing IQA models in both quality scoring and fine-grained image pair ranking. User study also validates its ability to recommend camera tuning suggestions.

[arXiv:2611.02672] Chain of Faith: Policy-Internal Rewards for Reasoning Reliance

  • arXiv ID: 2611.02672 (2611.02672v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-11-02
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.AI, cs.LG
  • Eje Cognitivo: Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)
  • Autores: Fanyu meng, Zhaodan Kong, Xin Liu
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02672)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02672) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.02672.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.02672.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Chain-of-thought (CoT) explanations can sound plausible while having little influence on a model's final answer. We study four rewards that use the policy's own signals to encourage reliance on generated reasoning: gradient sensitivity, confidence gaps, progressive confidence gains, and reasoning--prompt swaps. We add each reward to group relative policy optimization (GRPO) and evaluate two small instruction-tuned models on GSM8K and StrategyQA using reasoning edits, paraphrases, and prompt hints. Several configurations increase answer reliance to reasoning edits, while effects on accuracy and paraphrase robustness vary. We also observe answer copying at a high reward weight and high reliance in decomposition settings with poor task accuracy. The study compares practical objectives for reasoning reliance, confirms the trade-off between reliance and performance, and proposes four lightweight rewards that can improve reasoning reliance.

[arXiv:2611.02457] Multi-Teacher Reasoning Distillation for LLM Recommendation with Self-Exploration

  • arXiv ID: 2611.02457 (2611.02457v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-11-02
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.IR, cs.AI
  • Eje Cognitivo: Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)
  • Autores: Liangbo Ning, Siyuan Liang, Yi Zhou, Yifeng Zhang, Qing Li, Wenqi Fan et al.
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02457)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02457) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.02457.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.02457.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Reasoning-augmented recommender systems (RecSys) enhance user preference modeling by generating reasoning trajectories over historical interactions before making recommendations. Knowledge distillation provides a practical paradigm for transferring such reasoning capabilities from powerful large language models to lightweight RecSys. However, existing methods typically rely on a single fixed teacher, remain constrained by the teacher knowledge ceiling, and indiscriminately distill teacher-generated trajectories without assessing their actual utility for downstream recommendation. To address these limitations, we propose MUSE (Multi-Teacher Utility-guided Selection with Self-Exploration), an adaptive reasoning distillation framework that jointly improves reasoning generation and reasoning utilization. MUSE first constructs a diverse reasoning pool using multiple teacher LLMs and progressively augments it via student self-exploration, enabling the student to leverage the strengths of different teachers and gradually explore reasoning strategies beyond teacher imitation. We then develop Rank-Gain Utility Assessment (RGUA), which evaluates each candidate according to the normalized full-catalog rank improvement of the ground-truth item and identifies the most and least recommendation-beneficial trajectories. Based on these utility-aware trajectory pairs, we introduce dual-level contrastive learning objectives that guide the student generator to prefer beneficial reasoning while training the recommendation encoder to more effectively exploit recommendation-relevant preference evidence. Extensive experiments on three real-world datasets demonstrate the effectiveness and robustness of the proposed framework.

2. [Supervisión Granular por Pasos (Step-Level PRM)]: Validar secuencialmente cada considerando o nodo de decisión. Si un paso intermedio presenta incertidumbre o ambigüedad, podar esa rama de razonamiento y recalcular desde el último antecedente verificado.

[arXiv:2611.01740] How Does Chain-of-Thought Supervision Teach Models to Reason? A Theoretical Analysis

  • arXiv ID: 2611.01740 (2611.01740v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-11-01
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.LG, cond-mat.dis-nn, stat.ML
  • Eje Cognitivo: Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)
  • Autores: Kaito Takanami, Cengiz Pehlevan
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01740)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01740) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.01740.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.01740.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Training with chain-of-thought (CoT) supervision is an effective approach for teaching models multi-step reasoning procedures. At inference time, however, models may need to continue reasoning beyond the finite teacher CoT sequences observed during training, and longer reasoning can instead degrade performance, a phenomenon known as overthinking. How the amount and structure of training-time CoT give rise to such failures remains theoretically poorly understood. To study this problem, we introduce an in-context weight prediction model trained on finite gradient descent trajectories for linear regression. Using high-dimensional asymptotic analysis, we show that the success and failure modes of long-horizon reasoning undergo phase transitions depending on the context length and the total amount of training CoT. We further distinguish two mechanisms of overthinking, error accumulation and error amplification, and show that with short training contexts, increasing the training CoT budget can worsen long-horizon error before eventually stabilizing reasoning. Experiments with fully trained linear and multi-head softmax attention further show that these qualitative behaviors persist. Our results provide a theoretical account linking properties of training-time CoT to inference-time failures and offer guidance for designing teacher CoT that supports stable reasoning.

[arXiv:2611.00554] From Weeks to Hours: Fast and Principled SFT Data Generation for Reasoning LLMs

  • arXiv ID: 2611.00554 (2611.00554v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-10-31
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.LG
  • Eje Cognitivo: Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)
  • Autores: Hongyi Henry Jin, Wenhan Yang, Meysam Ghaffari, Carlos Morato, Baharan Mirzasoleiman
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00554)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00554) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.00554.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.00554.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Reasoning-oriented post-training enables large language models (LLMs) to solve complex tasks via multi-step inference. Supervised fine-tuning (SFT) on high-quality reasoning traces is a particularly efficient approach, but its effectiveness depends critically on careful data curation. Existing pipelines rely on a costly generate-then-filter paradigm: large pools of long reasoning traces are produced by multiple teacher models and subsequently filtered for difficulty and diversity using additional LLMs, often requiring weeks of computation. In this work, we propose SFTProbe, a fundamentally different approach that bypasses this pipeline by directly predicting the quality of reasoning data. We show that a brief LoRA-based adaptation, combined with evaluating loss on only 5% of each reasoning trace, suffices to estimate both difficulty and diversity. SFTProbe is grounded in a geometric view of post-training, where pretrained LLMs lie in a low-rank, anisotropic loss basin. By probing this basin via low-rank perturbations, we construct compact loss signatures whose clustering captures diversity, while loss at curvature inflection points provides a robust proxy for difficulty. We also provide a simple criterion for teacher selection. Empirically, our approach reduces data curation time from weeks to hours while maintaining or improving performance. Remarkably, on mathematical reasoning, 5k generated examples match the highly filtered 89k-example in OpenThoughts-Math dataset, and 7.5k generated examples outperforms it by 2.5% in accuracy. We further introduce a high-quality physics reasoning dataset, and use it to train a model that outperforms an IPhO 2025 silver-medal-winning model. The code and the dataset are released at https://bigml-cs-ucla.github.io/SFTProbe-page/.

[arXiv:2611.00460] From Small to Large: Assessing Medical Reasoning, Benchmark Contamination, and Model Size in Language Models

  • arXiv ID: 2611.00460 (2611.00460v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-10-30
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.AI, cs.CL
  • Eje Cognitivo: Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)
  • Autores: Navapat Nananukul, Mayank Kejriwal
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00460)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00460) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.00460.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.00460.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Large language models (LLMs) now answer medical exam questions at or above expert level, but it is unclear whether such scores reflect clinical competence, or are instead affected by the format of the questions being asked (e.g., multiple-choice versus open-ended), prior exposure to the benchmarks, and the difficulty of the questions. These benchmarks are also public and widely used online, which may be resulting in LLM memorization. Models may perform better on questions that they have seen before. It is also not evident whether smaller models, which are more locally deployable in HIPAA-compliant hospital settings, have achieved human performance. In this study, we evaluate fourteen current models from six leading providers, one smaller and one larger model from each provider with two additional open-weight models, on 3,076 questions from four medical benchmarks (MedQA-USMLE, MedMCQA, PubMedQA, and MedBullets) under standard multiple choice, the same questions with the answer choices withheld, and reconstruction of a hidden question from its choices alone. In the standard multiple-choice setting, the strongest models exceed the human-expert baseline on MedQA-USMLE and PubMedQA, but the smaller models trail their larger models by 8.7 percentage points and only two of the six pass the human-expert bar on the licensing examination. Withholding the answer choices, however, makes every model struggle: mean accuracy falls from 82.3% to 50.9%, and the two largest drops belong to the medically specialised models. On memorization, the strongest models reconstruct one question in four from the answer choices alone, yet accuracy holds on the questions they cannot reconstruct. We further weigh accuracy against inference cost and test whether ensembling several models improves medical question answering.

[arXiv:2611.00187] CAPER: Circuit-Aware Parameter-Efficient Reasoning in LLMs for Analog Verification

  • arXiv ID: 2611.00187 (2611.00187v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-10-30
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.AR, cs.LG
  • Eje Cognitivo: Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)
  • Autores: Saion K. Roy
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00187)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00187) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.00187.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.00187.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Large language models (LLMs) are increasingly used to generate analog circuits, yet every netlist must also be verified. We propose LLMs as verification assistants for analog circuits that flag unmet specifications and reliability issues that appear under process, voltage, and temperature (PVT) variation. These issues emerge from how circuit stages combine, a structure that generic parameter-efficient fine-tuning (PEFT) methods such as LoRA do not capture. To this end, we introduce CAPER, a circuit-aware PEFT method that adds a small branch alongside LoRA to read each stage's properties from the LLM and combine them from the relevant stages. In deciding whether a feedback loop is negative, LoRA and a high-rank adapter stay at chance, whereas CAPER answers every test case correctly. For comparator latches, where 11 of 90 LLM-written designs that work under nominal conditions fail under PVT variation, CAPER likewise learns to flag such latent failures, while generic adapters stay near chance. These results show that adding circuit knowledge to PEFT makes such analog verification checks learnable through fine-tuning.

[arXiv:2610.41769] Stepwise-Querying: Distilling Reasoning without Access to Teacher Chain-of-Thought

  • arXiv ID: 2610.41769 (2610.41769v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-10-30
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.CL
  • Eje Cognitivo: Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)
  • Autores: Dicong Yu, Wenhua Wang, Tian Wang
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41769)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41769) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.41769.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.41769.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Reasoning distillation is widely used to transfer the reasoning capabilities of large language models to smaller students through teacher-generated supervision. In the black-box setting, however, teachers may expose only final answers and brief solutions, leaving intermediate calculations and validity conditions unavailable and thus limiting the reasoning supervision available for distillation. We propose Stepwise Querying, which elicits step-specific explanations without requiring access to the teacher's internal chain-of-thought (CoT) traces. Independent queries to the same teacher request intermediate derivations, validity checks, and corrections for individual steps, the assembled solutions are then used for standard supervised fine-tuning. Across two teachers, two students, and four mathematics and code benchmarks, our method achieves the highest score in 14 of 16 comparisons with three baselines. We further introduce Stepwise Explanation Information Gain (SEIG), a diagnostic based on the log-ratio of a frozen student's probabilities of correct step completion under stepwise and global explanations. Analyses on GSM8K show that incorrect-answer trajectories can remain useful when they preserve valid intermediate reasoning, while further decomposition yields limited additional gains.

Reglas Operativas Extraídas (Aplicación en Inferencia y Razonamiento)

1. [Directiva de Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)]: Estructurar cadenas de razonamiento explícitas (Chain-of-Thought deliberativo) separando estrictamente la premisa fáctica probada, la premisa normativa vigente y el juicio de subsunción, ejecutando un pase de autoverificación contra alucinaciones o saltos lógicos.

2. [Supervisión Granular por Pasos (Step-Level PRM)]: Validar secuencialmente cada considerando o nodo de decisión. Si un paso intermedio presenta incertidumbre o ambigüedad, podar esa rama de razonamiento y recalcular desde el último antecedente verificado.

3. [Descomposición Agéntica y Uso de Herramientas]: Dividir la tarea compleja en subtareas atómicas verificables (extracción de hechos -> búsqueda de precedentes -> encuadre legal -> redacción -> control de calidad), invocando herramientas externas de recuperación antes de razonar en abstracto.

[arXiv:2610.32627] LGMT: A Tool for Testing the Logical Reasoning Reliability of LLMs

  • arXiv ID: 2610.32627 (2610.32627v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-10-23
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.AI
  • Eje Cognitivo: Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)
  • Autores: Zenghui Zhou, Xiaoke Fang, Zheng Zheng
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32627)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32627) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.32627.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.32627.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Large Language Models (LLMs) increasingly serve as reasoning components in modern software systems, yet a model that answers a logical query correctly can contradict itself when the same problem is restated in an equivalent form. Static benchmarks evaluate queries in isolation and therefore fail to expose such inconsistencies. To address this limitation, we present LGMT, an open-source tool that detects reasoning inconsistencies through metamorphic relations grounded in first-order logic. Given a source test case in premise-conclusion form, LGMT generates logically equivalent follow-up cases and checks whether the model's entailment predictions agree across the pair, without ground-truth labels. The tool provides 20 built-in metamorphic relations, supports custom ones verified by the Z3 theorem prover, and runs from a CLI or a web studio, including an offline mock mode requiring no API key. Across six widely used LLMs on a bundled benchmark of 3,091 metamorphic groups, LGMT reveals inconsistency rates ranging from 20.84% to 29.44%. LGMT is publicly available on GitHub at https://github.com/MaxwelsDonc/lgmt-tool, with a demonstration video at https://youtu.be/gou4opptu1U.

2. [Descomposición Agéntica y Uso de Herramientas]: Dividir la tarea compleja en subtareas atómicas verificables (extracción de hechos -> búsqueda de precedentes -> encuadre legal -> redacción -> control de calidad), invocando herramientas externas de recuperación antes de razonar en abstracto.

3. [Estructuración de Contexto y Restricciones Explícitas]: Formular las restricciones negativas (prohibición de alucinar metadatos) y las plantillas estructurales al inicio del bloque de instrucciones, utilizando precedentes reales recuperados como anclaje in-context.

[arXiv:2610.32474] Beyond Post-Hoc Rewards: Privileged Reasoning Exploration for Multimodal Embeddings

  • arXiv ID: 2610.32474 (2610.32474v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-10-23
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.AI, cs.CL
  • Eje Cognitivo: Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)
  • Autores: Tianyu Yang, Yijun Tian, Lars Benedikt Kaesberg, Jan Philip Wahle, Bela Gipp, Terry Ruas et al.
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32474)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32474) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.32474.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.32474.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Advances in Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) have driven substantial progress in multimodal embeddings. Recent methods exploit generative capabilities by augmenting embedded inputs with self-generated reasoning to produce more informative embeddings. However, conventional retrieval supervision for training embedding models specifies pairwise relevance relations without explicitly guiding the reasoning used for input augmentation. Such reasoning is neither directly observed nor uniquely determined by the input, allowing multiple plausible traces to introduce semantic cues of varying retrieval utility. Existing approaches largely optimize reasoning conditioned on the input alone, limiting the exploration of retrieval-relevant traces. We introduce PILOT, a framework that models reasoning as a latent variable and instantiates its prior and posterior distributions as distinct reasoning modes of a shared MLLM. The prior conditions only on the input, whereas the posterior uses additional training-time relational evidence to guide reasoning exploration. To translate the posterior's broader exploration into inference-time improvements, we derive an importance-weighted variational objective and instantiate it as a prior-posterior co-evolution framework that improves both modes under a shared inference-compatible reward while selectively transferring posterior trajectories to the prior according to their utility and prior reachability. On the 78-task MMEB-V2 benchmark, PILOT outperforms the primary baseline across all 12 task groups at both 2B and 7B parameters, improving overall scores by 3.2 and 3.3 points, respectively, with the largest gains in visual-document retrieval (+6.1 and +6.4 points). Our results show that relational supervision is most valuable not as a post-hoc reward but as a signal for shaping how reasoning is explored in training reasoning-enhanced embedding models.
