2. [Trazabilidad y Explicabilidad Técnica]: Explicitar los fundamentos y pasos de derivación desde los datos de entrada hasta la conclusión dispositiva, garantizando auditabilidad completa del razonamiento.

[arXiv:2610.32308] Solvent: Long-Horizon Reasoning for Multi-Step Retrosynthesis

  • arXiv ID: 2610.32308 (2610.32308v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-10-23
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.AI
  • Eje Cognitivo: Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)
  • Autores: Yifei Wang, Botian Wang, YiFei Wang, Jiangtao Feng, Xinbo Zhang, Yawen Ouyang et al.
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32308)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32308) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.32308.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.32308.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Multi-step retrosynthesis requires recursively transforming a target molecule through successive reaction steps into purchasable building blocks, and recent work has begun to draw on large language model (LLM) reasoning to tackle the task. In its latest evolution, LLM reasoning has entered the long-horizon regime: models pursue objectives across interdependent steps, interact with environments, and adapt to feedback. This shift has markedly advanced LLM capabilities, as illustrated by breakthroughs in AI-driven mathematical research. At this frontier, we introduce Solvent, an 8B LLM that successfully extends long-horizon reasoning to multi-step retrosynthesis. Through a two-round post-training curriculum, Solvent learns to interleave iterative synthesis planning, external availability verification, and adaptive route revision. Experiments show that, following a $2.1\times$ performance boost from RL, Solvent scores 61.86% across four benchmarks, surpassing mid-scale language models and prior search-based retrosynthesis systems, and rivaling frontier trillion-parameter LLMs.

[arXiv:2610.32055] How Much Do You Trust A Dog? Quantifying Hint-Following in Reasoning Language Models

  • arXiv ID: 2610.32055 (2610.32055v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-10-23
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.CL, cs.LG
  • Eje Cognitivo: Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)
  • Autores: Anxin Guo, Chirag Pabbaraju, Sara Ahmadian, Kamesh Munagala, Aravindan Vijayaraghavan
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32055)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32055) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.32055.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.32055.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Large language models (LLMs) often exhibit sycophancy, altering their answers to align with user suggestions even on objective factual and reasoning tasks. Yet the quantitative form of this influence--- and whether it is calibrated to source reliability--- remains poorly understood. In this work, we investigate this behavior through hint-induced answer shifts on MMLU questions, evaluating prompts of the form ``[Source] thinks the answer is (X)''. We systematically vary the source to which the hint is attributed (a professor, a friend, or a dog) across three open-source model families, Qwen, Gemma, and SmolLM. We show that the change induced by the hint follows a predictable structure well-characterized by an exponential tilt: the hint induces an approximately constant mean shift in the hinted answer's log-odds, with question--target susceptibility largely shared across sources. To explore how this pattern can arise, we introduce a formal decomposition of the hint response and illustrate in controlled synthetic experiments how KL-regularized reward-based training reshapes both hinted and unhinted answer distributions. Finally, we evaluate whether this shift is calibrated to source reliability, finding that the log-odds shift persists even when the hint-giver is declared inaccurate or accompanied by explicit warnings not to trust the source, highlighting the vulnerability of LLMs to adversarial steering.

[arXiv:2610.32022] CEMIR vs. FISER: a comparative study of reasoning-based and enrichment-based approaches to pragmatic intent recognition

  • arXiv ID: 2610.32022 (2610.32022v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-10-23
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.AI, cs.RO
  • Eje Cognitivo: Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)
  • Autores: Ziya Hosseynzadeh, Farjad Fallah, Farnoosh Fallah, Soroush Gooran, Mohammad Amanloo, Hossein Sameti
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32022)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32022) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.32022.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.32022.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Intent recognition in human-robot interaction requires agents to resolve ambiguous natural language instructions by inferring the speaker's underlying goals. Research has increasingly focused on two notable paradigms for addressing this task: structured symbolic reasoning and multimodal input enrichment. However, it remains unclear whether enrichment-based methods provide benefit in settings where the ambiguity concerns unstated human intent rather than missing observable scene information. In this paper, we present a comparative and diagnostic study benchmarking FISER - a framework utilizing structured social and embodied reasoning - against CEMIR (Context-Enriched Multimodal Intent Recognition), a method that performs multimodal enrichment by synthesizing narratives and photorealistic images from textual prompts. By evaluating both approaches across four Large Language Models (LLMs), ranging from Qwen2.5-Omni-3B to GPT-5, on the HandMeThat v2 benchmark, we analyze their capability to resolve pragmatic ambiguities. Our statistical analysis, employing multiple-comparison correction, reveals no statistically significant difference in performance between FISER and CEMIR across any model or difficulty level. This pattern holds across all sixteen model-by-difficulty comparisons, as well as in a targeted ablation isolating CEMIR's narrative and visual components. Instead, performance is primarily associated with backbone capability and the inherent difficulty of the ambiguity. These findings suggest a boundary condition for text-derived multimodal enrichment: when the relevant task constraints are already available in the textual instruction, synthetic multimodal augmentation may not provide measurable benefit over structured reasoning on the same text.

[arXiv:2610.31376] Certified Execution Harness for Agentic AI: Safe and Least-Intrusive Petri-Net Supervisory Control Between Reasoning and Action

  • arXiv ID: 2610.31376 (2610.31376v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-10-22
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.AI, eess.SY
  • Eje Cognitivo: Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)
  • Autores: Chao Gu, Quanyan Zhu
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31376)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31376) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.31376.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.31376.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> This paper studies the problem of safe tool execution for large language model (LLM) agents under prompt injection. Using Petri nets, we formalize the execution harness between the LLM and its tools as a supervisory control problem, in which marks represent capabilities, approvals and budgets. Since a transition fires only if the marks it needs are present, an injected request whose preconditions are unmet or already spent is refused regardless of its wording. Treating tool outcomes as uncontrollable, we enforce safety by the conventional maximally permissive supervisor, and this safety transfers to the running system under four assumptions. Moreover, to capture an agent that may propose again after a refusal, we introduce the notion of a replanning closed loop, in which each retry costs an inference call. We show that, in this loop, the conventional supervisor can be arbitrarily more intrusive than a stricter safe one. Furthermore, for a given proposer model, the maximally permissive least-intrusive supervisor is characterized as a value-threshold refinement of the conventional one. Under precise conditions, this refinement orders two concurrent actions by at most one monitor place. On an autonomous release agent, we find by synthesis that safe deployment requires a step that the supervisor can refuse after a rollout starts.

[arXiv:2610.31119] Constraint Reasoning and Large Language Models Join Forces: Providing Contrastive Explanations for Real-World Optimisation-Based Applications

  • arXiv ID: 2610.31119 (2610.31119v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-10-22
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.AI
  • Eje Cognitivo: Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)
  • Autores: Roger X. Lera-Leri, Filippo Bistaffa, Athina Georgara, Juan A. Rodríguez-Aguilar
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31119)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31119) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.31119.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.31119.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> We focus on optimisation-based Decision Support Systems in real-world applications. In this context, we aim to provide contrastive explanations that answer users' questions about recommendations based on the solution of an optimisation problem, specifically a Mixed-Integer Program (MIP). Addressing this endeavour is crucial to ensure human oversight of the decisions implemented, as required by international guidelines on trustworthy and responsible automated decision-making systems. We propose a novel methodology that combines constraint reasoning techniques and Large Language Models (LLMs) to generate contrastive explanations for optimisation problems encoded as MIPs. Although LLMs have shown impressive capabilities in Natural Language processing, they can produce incorrect answers due to reasoning limitations. To overcome these limitations, we leverage constraint reasoning techniques to distil the information necessary to produce an explanation, which is then passed as input to an LLM as the so-called graph of reasons. Such a graph captures the semantics and the relationships among constraints of the optimisation problem. Results on real-world scheduling domains show that incorporating constraint reasoning improves explanations generated by the LLM. In particular, using the graph of reasons enables the LLM to produce natural-language explanations that include more relevant reasons while reducing errors compared to explanations generated without this structure.

[arXiv:2610.31000] SecLog-Bench: A Scalable Benchmark for Cybersecurity Log Reasoning with Scaffolded Evidence

  • arXiv ID: 2610.31000 (2610.31000v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-10-22
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.CR, cs.AI
  • Eje Cognitivo: Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)
  • Autores: Yifan Wang, Dongliang Xu, Xiaoyong Li, Jing Li, Peter S. Pan, Yue Yao
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31000)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31000) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.31000.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.31000.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Large language models (LLMs) have shown strong potential for cybersecurity, leading to growing interest in LLM-based automated cybersecurity log analysis. However, current datasets (1) are limited in scale; (2) rely mainly on single-source evidence; and (3) have only binary or multi-class attack labels, without dense annotations of the reasoning process. To address these limitations, SecLog-Bench has three key features. First, we build an extensible simulation platform that covers 29 attack scenarios, supports repeated execution and the integration of new scenarios, and enables scalable generation of cybersecurity logs with attack-stage labels. Second, we use multi-source logs, including audit logs, application logs, and network traffic, to jointly analyze cyber attacks. Third, we annotate each sample with the essential context for cybersecurity log reasoning and a structured step-by-step evidence chain. We evaluate eight mainstream LLMs on this benchmark for post-detection attack-progress reasoning. Results show that, when provided with scaffolded evidence, current LLMs exhibit modest capability in attack-progress reasoning, with the best model achieving only 44.44% accuracy and 45.07% Macro-F1. Their reasoning remains unreliable; i.e., the highest Stage Reasoning F1 (SRF1) score is only 54.87%. Moreover, even correct predictions may be accompanied by flawed reasoning, and reasoning quality is often misaligned with prediction accuracy.

[arXiv:2610.30953] Measuring the Reasoning-Action Coupling in Driving Vision-Language-Action Models through Counterfactuals

  • arXiv ID: 2610.30953 (2610.30953v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-10-22
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.AI
  • Eje Cognitivo: Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)
  • Autores: Gabriel Manalu, Saad Wazir, Thao Do, Seongah Kim, Hyeontaek Hwang, Tolcha Yalew Kidane et al.
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.30953)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.30953) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.30953.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.30953.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Driving vision-language-action (VLA) models bring an appealing form of explainability to a safety-critical domain, since a system that can state the reasons for its maneuvers offers the transparency that deployment and public trust in autonomous driving demand. These models read the world into language before they act on it, describing the scene and reasoning over it in text that ranges from chain-of-thought to multi-stage question answering on the way to a trajectory. That framing quietly assumes two separable properties, namely that the trajectory follows the generated reasoning and that the generated reasoning tracks the causes that actually produce the trajectory. To thoroughly investigate these properties, we apply causal, counterfactual interventions to four driving VLAs across different benchmarks. First, we perturb the models' own reasoning and show that altering its meaning produces small trajectory changes relative to blanking the same models' camera inputs, a pattern that persists across benchmarks and model architectures. We then construct scene-level counterfactuals through photorealistic removal of key objects named in the models' reasoning and analyze whether the generated reasoning and trajectories track these changes. Overall, our findings indicate that the reasoning these models present to account for their resulting driving behavior is weakly coupled to that behavior itself, pointing to limits on the explainability these systems are intended to provide.

1. [Directiva de Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)]: Estructurar cadenas de razonamiento explícitas (Chain-of-Thought deliberativo) separando estrictamente la premisa fáctica probada, la premisa normativa vigente y el juicio de subsunción, ejecutando un pase de autoverificación contra alucinaciones o saltos lógicos.

2. [Protocolo de Verificación Intrínseca]: Implementar un paso de auditoría independiente sobre cada afirmación fáctica y cita normativa/jurisprudencial antes de consolidar la respuesta final, rechazando cualquier inferencia no respaldada por evidencia documental directa.

3. [Supervisión Granular por Pasos (Step-Level PRM)]: Validar secuencialmente cada considerando o nodo de decisión. Si un paso intermedio presenta incertidumbre o ambigüedad, podar esa rama de razonamiento y recalcular desde el último antecedente verificado.

[arXiv:2610.30946] Tracing Hallucinations in LLM Reasoning for UML Class Diagram Generation: An Empirical Taxonomy and Mitigation Study

  • arXiv ID: 2610.30946 (2610.30946v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-10-22
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.SE, cs.CL
  • Eje Cognitivo: Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)
  • Autores: Mojdeh Rahmanian, Foutse Khomh, Ashkan Sami, Yanchao Yu
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.30946)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.30946) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.30946.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.30946.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Large Language Models (LLMs) can generate UML class diagrams from natural language requirements, but hallucinations may compromise the correctness and completeness of the resulting diagrams. Existing evaluations mostly assess the final diagram, without examining the intermediate traces of reasoning-capable LLMs, which can reveal where these hallucinations arise. We develop an empirical taxonomy of eleven hallucination sub-types organised into three dimensions: Prompt Faithfulness (deviations between requirements and reasoning), Code Faithfulness (discrepancies between reasoning and generated PlantUML code), and Reasoning Quality (problems within the reasoning). The taxonomy was derived from 60 manually annotated DeepSeek-R1 traces, applied to 120 further DeepSeek-R1 traces through a dual-LLM annotation pipeline, and examined on 20 DeepSeek-V4-Pro traces. Code Faithfulness was the most frequent dimension for DeepSeek-R1 in both datasets, whereas Prompt Faithfulness dominated for DeepSeek-V4-Pro, suggesting model-dependent failure patterns. In the manually annotated traces, reasoning-trace length showed no significant monotonic association with hallucination frequency, whereas requirement complexity was negatively associated with it, particularly for Prompt Faithfulness errors. In a mitigation experiment on 13 Golden UML ModelSet cases with three Qwen3-based models, adding Prompt Faithfulness and Reasoning Quality constraints to the generation prompt raised the pooled mean F1 score by 4.0 percentage points, whereas a post-hoc Code Faithfulness review provided no consistent further benefit. Relationship modelling remained the lowest-scoring element throughout. These results suggest that localising hallucinations within the generation process can inform targeted, prompt-based mitigation. Whether these findings generalise beyond the DeepSeek and Qwen3 model families remains to be tested.

[arXiv:2610.30796] GIFT: Gradient Informed Filtering for Distillation Augmented Reasoning RL

  • arXiv ID: 2610.30796 (2610.30796v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-10-22
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.LG
  • Eje Cognitivo: Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)
  • Autores: Shijie Li, Yilin Gao, Tieyuan Chen, Yun Luo, Weiyao Lin, Hang Yu
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.30796)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.30796) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.30796.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.30796.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Training large language models for complex reasoning tasks relies on two complementary signals: sparse, outcome-grounded reinforcement learning (RL) for correctness, and dense, token-level distillation for guidance. However, complementarity in supervision does not guarantee compatibility in optimization; imitation-focused distillation can conflict with the outcome-driven RL objective, and existing hybrids lack a principled resolution. We propose \textbf{GIFT} (\textbf{G}radient-\textbf{I}nformed \textbf{F}il\textbf{T}ering for Distillation-Augmented Reasoning RL), a novel method that directly addresses the allocation problem of which teacher updates to apply. GIFT performs a token-level geometric analysis, decomposing the distillation gradient into a component \textbf{parallel} to the RL gradient, which measures first-order conflict and provides a \textbf{safety} filter, and a component \textbf{orthogonal} to it, which quantifies novel directional guidance and provides a measure of \textbf{value}. This decomposition yields a principled scoring rule to rank token-level guidance. We combine this with a progressive \textbf{coverage annealing} schedule that shrinks the budget of tokens receiving distillation, ensuring that the most conflicting updates are discarded first while the most complementary ones are retained at full strength. This facilitates a smooth transition from dense, geometrically-filtered guidance to a pure, outcome-grounded objective at convergence. Extensive experiments on mathematical and logical reasoning benchmarks show that GIFT consistently outperforms strong baselines across multiple model scales, achieving relative gains of up to 9.5\% on difficult mathematical reasoning tasks. Code: https://github.com/lilifree/GIFT

Reglas Operativas Extraídas (Aplicación en Inferencia y Razonamiento)

1. [Directiva de Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)]: Estructurar cadenas de razonamiento explícitas (Chain-of-Thought deliberativo) separando estrictamente la premisa fáctica probada, la premisa normativa vigente y el juicio de subsunción, ejecutando un pase de autoverificación contra alucinaciones o saltos lógicos.

2. [Asignación Dinámica de Cómputo de Inferencia]: Calibrar la profundidad deliberativa según la complejidad del caso: aplicar resolución directa en trámites estandarizados y expandir el árbol de búsqueda (múltiples candidatos + ranking) en casos con colisión normativa o jurisprudencial.

3. [Descomposición Agéntica y Uso de Herramientas]: Dividir la tarea compleja en subtareas atómicas verificables (extracción de hechos -> búsqueda de precedentes -> encuadre legal -> redacción -> control de calidad), invocando herramientas externas de recuperación antes de razonar en abstracto.

[arXiv:2610.30630] Interpreting Model Reasoning through Behavioral Counterfactuals

  • arXiv ID: 2610.30630 (2610.30630v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-10-22
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.LG, cs.AI
  • Eje Cognitivo: Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)
  • Autores: Shawn Im, James Oldfield, Adel Bibi, Sharon Li
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.30630)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.30630) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.30630.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.30630.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Faithfully understanding the internal process that occurs as models form their chain of thought is an important problem with the rise in model capabilities. However, this remains challenging due to the variance in sampled responses, making it difficult to isolate the effects of interventions and compare model reasoning systematically. We propose a method for localizing and analyzing how a given intervention affects the model's reasoning through behavioral counterfactuals: given a fixed response, how does introducing an intervention to the prompt change the next-token distributions? We train a module to edit the residual stream such that it minimizes the KL divergence between the output distributions for a fixed response under a source prompt and its counterfactual target prompt. We find that matching the output distributions recovers the counterfactual behavior more effectively than standard activation reconstruction objectives and validate that attribution of the trained module outputs is effective for identifying causally important locations for counterfactual recovery. The attribution maps and visualizations of the downstream effects of model edits provide an interface for generating hypotheses about the original model's computation, which can then be tested with activation patching. In a case study on Gemma-4-E4B-it and DeepSeek-R1-0528-Qwen3-8B, we investigate how a suggestion influences model reasoning even when it is not verbally acknowledged. Our analysis identifies representations at the question statement and filler tokens that mediate a substantial part of the suggestion's influence, with downstream effects such as changing the framing of the justification or even the final answer.

[arXiv:2610.30611] Better Reasoning, Not Just Bigger Models: A Structure-Grounded Framework for Efficient and Reliable Inference

  • arXiv ID: 2610.30611 (2610.30611v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-10-22
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.AI, cs.IR
  • Eje Cognitivo: Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)
  • Autores: Amirhossein Aghaei, Liangyan Li, Shiyi Yang, Shaohua Wang, Yan Wang
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.30611)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.30611) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.30611.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.30611.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Scaling large language models has substantially improved linguistic fluency, yet reliable autonomous agents require more than fluent generation. In multi-turn inference, current agentic systems often produce semantically plausible but structurally ungrounded reasoning, especially under incomplete observations. We propose Structure-Grounded Uncertainty Reduction (SGUR), a training-free framework that complements token-level generation with active uncertainty reduction over structured hypothesis spaces. SGUR represents incomplete observations as a query graph and aligns them with candidate hypothesis graphs through a prior-weighted partial graph matching mechanism, where database-derived empirical priors assign greater importance to decision-relevant relations rather than treating all graph edges uniformly. The resulting inference state is iteratively refined by an active information-gain strategy that selects the next most informative node to query, systematically pruning the candidate space. We instantiate SGUR on a challenging PrimeKG-derived sequential medical diagnosis benchmark, demonstrating that structural grounding can partially substitute for model scale: starting from only two observed symptoms, SGUR achieves 92.5% Hit@3, outperforming the strongest Graph-RAG baseline by 9.4 percentage points while using approximately 5x fewer tokens. Notably, SGUR enables smaller backbone models to match or exceed the performance of models up to 10x larger, suggesting that structural grounding provides a more robust and efficient paradigm for scaling agent performance than relying exclusively on increasing model size.

[arXiv:2610.30351] Route-and-Generate: KL-Regularized Router Improvement for Reasoning Routing in LLMs

  • arXiv ID: 2610.30351 (2610.30351v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-10-21
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.AI, cs.LG
  • Eje Cognitivo: Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)
  • Autores: Tien-Phat Nguyen, Truong Nguyen, Thanh-Thien Le, Hoang Phan, Thien Huu Nguyen, Linh Ngo Van et al.
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.30351)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.30351) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.30351.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.30351.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Large language models often apply complex reasoning indiscriminately, leading to overthinking on simple tasks and underthinking on harder ones. We present Route-and-Generate (Rouge), a hierarchical training framework that enables LLMs to adaptively decide when and how much reasoning is required for each input. Our approach decouples reasoning effort allocation from answer generation by introducing a routing policy that allocates a fixed sampling budget across multiple reasoning formats, while the base model is optimized with GRPO using per-sample rewards. To promote efficient reasoning, we employ a rank-based reward that favors shorter correct solutions, and update the routing policy via a closed-form KL-regularized policy improvement step distilled into a neural router. This design encourages concise answers on easy problems while reserving longer reasoning for harder ones, achieving improved accuracy-efficiency trade-offs across multiple reasoning benchmarks.

[arXiv:2610.30132] LongZero: Enhancing Long-Context Reasoning in LLMs from Zero Data

  • arXiv ID: 2610.30132 (2610.30132v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-10-21
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.CL, cs.LG
  • Eje Cognitivo: Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)
  • Autores: Zhennan Wan, Jianfei Chen
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.30132)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.30132) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.30132.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.30132.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Reinforcement learning (RL) is an effective way to strengthen the long-context reasoning of large language models (LLMs), but it needs training data that are long, diverse, challenging, and verifiable. Human annotation of such data is expensive, naturally occurring data are too scarce to sustain RL, and existing synthesis methods depend on external corpora or stronger teacher models, which become harder to obtain as the trained models get stronger. Recently, RL from zero data, in which a model learns from tasks it proposes itself, has achieved excellent performance, but existing methods target short-context tasks. Therefore, we propose $\textbf{LongZero}$, a method that lets an LLM improve its own long-context reasoning without any external data. Instead of writing questions and answers over existing documents, LongZero first has the model synthesize the question and answer with its supporting evidence in a compact structured form, and only then separately expands the evidence into a long multi-document context. LongZero combines these self-written multi-hop reasoning tasks with self-written multi-round retrieval tasks and trains the model with GRPO. We evaluate LongZero on two LLMs across five long-context reasoning benchmarks and compare it with RL on data synthesized from external corpora. Experimental results show that LongZero can generalize to real tasks, and outperforms both baselines, suggesting a viable and promising path to improving long-context reasoning of LLMs.

[arXiv:2610.29792] MAPS: Macro-Adaptive Parallel Reasoning for Regulating Search Momentum in Retrieval-Augmented Generation

  • arXiv ID: 2610.29792 (2610.29792v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-10-21
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.CL
  • Eje Cognitivo: Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)
  • Autores: Xinze Li, Dingwen Zhang, Zhenghao Liu, Yukun Yan, Shuo Wang, Yu Gu et al.
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29792)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29792) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.29792.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.29792.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) improves the performance of Large Language Models (LLMs) by retrieving external knowledge. Recently, some RAG methods interleave reasoning and retrieval, enabling LLMs to progressively generate sub-queries and retrieve external knowledge. However, these methods may suffer from redundant or ineffective sub-queries and insufficiently diverse query exploration. Moreover, due to the tightly interleaved nature of reasoning and retrieval, it remains unclear how the evolving reasoning process influences query intent. To characterize the evolution of query intent during reasoning, we introduce search momentum, which captures the accumulated retrieval tendency induced by historical changes in generated sub-queries along the reasoning trajectory. Through momentum-based analysis, we find that the query generation process tends to continuously evolve along existing retrieval trajectories. Based on this observation, we propose MAPS, a Macro-Adaptive Parallel Reasoning framework for regulating search momentum and mitigating query-intent drift. MAPS first retains the retrieved knowledge and a refined reasoning state, thereby reducing the influence of historical reasoning trajectories on subsequent sub-query generation. Subsequently, MAPS extends the conventional sequential reasoning process into a parallel reasoning paradigm. At each iteration, MAPS identifies complementary retrieval directions, generates corresponding sub-queries for parallel retrieval, and consolidates the retrieved evidence to update the knowledge and reasoning states. Experiments on six question-answering benchmarks demonstrate that MAPS consistently outperforms representative RAG methods across different backbone models. All code and resources are available at https://github.com/NEUIR/MAPS.

[arXiv:2610.30202] MPIDetector: Detecting Multi-modal Prompt Injection via LLM-based Reasoning

  • arXiv ID: 2610.30202 (2610.30202v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-10-21
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.CR, cs.AI
  • Eje Cognitivo: Reasoning in LLMs (Deliberative, Chain-of-Thought & Self-Verification)
  • Autores: Yiheng Li, Jingyue Li
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.30202)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.30202) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.30202.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.30202.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> With the rapid integration of Multi-modal Large Language Models (MLLMs) into real-world applications, Prompt Injection (PI) has emerged as a critical security threat. Attackers can conceal malicious instructions within textual narratives or visual inputs to bypass safety alignment. Existing approaches, such as signature- and perplexity-based methods, rely heavily on pattern matching, making them insufficient to detect sophisticated, cross-modal PI payloads. In this paper, we propose a novel detection framework, called MPIDetector, that leverages LLMs' intrinsic reasoning capabilities via few-shot In-Context Learning (ICL). Instead of fine-tuning, we provide the model with specific prompt templates and representative attack examples, enabling it to scrutinize the semantic intent of input across modalities. We constructed a comprehensive evaluation benchmark comprising eight textual and seven visual malicious categories, supplemented with benign datasets to rigorously test for false positives. Our evaluation across multiple leading LLMs, including Gemini-2.0-Flash, Qwen3-VL-32B-Instruct, and Doubao-seed-1.6-flash, demonstrates the efficacy and generalizability of this approach. The proposed method achieves up to 90.91% accuracy and 100% recall on textual tasks. In the visual domain, it achieves up to 99.28% accuracy while maintaining 100% precision across all evaluated models. Crucially, when evaluated against 500 highly obfuscated environmental web-agent injections (WebInject), MPIDetector achieved a detection rate up to 99.80%. Comparisons with two baseline approaches for detecting textual PI payloads, along with two additional methods for visual input detection, demonstrate MPIDetector's superior accuracy. These results show that LLM-driven reasoning provides a robust, cross-modal defense against advanced prompt injections.

[arXiv:2611.16414] Small Language Models for Inference-Capability Routing: Effects of Model Capacity and Prompt Optimization on Downstream Quality and Efficiency

  • arXiv ID: 2611.16414 (2611.16414v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-11-12
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.AI
  • Eje Cognitivo: Prompt Engineering, Meta-Prompting & In-Context Optimization
  • Autores: Sailendra Kolluru, Maheshkumar Duvvarapu
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16414)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16414) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.16414.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.16414.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Optional reasoning, retrieval, web search, and tool use can improve large language model responses but incur unnecessary computation when activated indiscriminately. This study investigates whether prompt optimization can compensate for limited router capacity and whether classification gains translate into downstream efficiency. The proposed framework uses prompt-driven small language models to make four independent capability decisions, allowing multiple capabilities to be selected per query. Qwen2.5 routers ranging from 1.5B to 14B parameters are compared across five prompting strategies, followed by an evaluation of capability-specific optimized configurations using a fixed GPT-OSS-120B downstream model on 127 questions. Under minimal prompting, increasing router capacity does not consistently improve classification performance. Prompt optimization narrows the capacity gap for reasoning and calculator routing, whereas RAG and web search retain larger benefits from higher capacity. Similar classification F1 scores do not necessarily imply equivalent downstream behavior: false-positive activations increase unnecessary computation, while missed capabilities can reduce answer quality. The 14B router achieves a mean BERTScore F1 of 0.8408, compared with 0.8418 when all capabilities are enabled, while reducing average downstream token consumption by 68.7\% (8,837 to 2,764 tokens) and median downstream latency by 80.5\% (9.79 to 1.91 seconds). These savings exclude router overhead, which is measured separately in a different inference environment and increases with router capacity. The findings demonstrate that the relationship between router capacity, prompt design, and downstream efficiency is capability-dependent. Consequently, router selection must consider error profiles and routing overhead alongside classification performance.

[arXiv:2611.14924] Edge-Native Multimodal RAG: Hardware-Aware Quantization and Architectural Optimization for Consumer GPUs

  • arXiv ID: 2611.14924 (2611.14924v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-11-11
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.AI
  • Eje Cognitivo: Prompt Engineering, Meta-Prompting & In-Context Optimization
  • Autores: Zahraa Selim, Menna Hamed, Wesam Ahmed, Sohyla Said, Sara Basheer, Rami Zewail
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14924)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14924) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.14924.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.14924.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Deployment of retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) for Edge-AI is constrained by memory and bandwidth limitations. A standard 7B parameter model consumes 14 GB Video Random Access Memory (VRAM) in half-precision (FP16), precluding co-location of vector indices and Key-Value (KV) caches. This paper presents an engineering integration and empirical evaluation of a multimodal RAG framework optimized for consumer-grade Turing hardware (e.g., NVIDIA Tesla T4). Our system-level analysis identifies a critical trade-off: while generative pre-trained transformer quantization (GPTQ) offers superior throughput (51.6 tok/s), it exhibits significant context instability at 2048+ tokens on Turing architectures. Conversely, NormalFloat 4-bit (NF4) maintains robust long-context performance with viable latency (81 ms/tok). By coupling NF4 with a decoupled Florence-2 vision integration, we achieve visual reasoning with only 430 MB VRAM overhead. Furthermore, empirical observations of prompt-induced search space pruning show that structured prompting reduces latency by 47% without altering computational intensity (1.88 GFLOPs/token). The integrated architecture maintains an 8.37 GB footprint, establishing a viable RAG pipeline for 12-16 GB GPUs. Finally, structured few-shot prompting effectively compensates for quantization-induced reasoning loss, restoring mathematical accuracy from 35.0% to 92.0% across 200 trials.

[arXiv:2611.12976] Partition, Refine, and Detect: Preserving Novel Category Capacity in Open-Vocabulary Object Detection

  • arXiv ID: 2611.12976 (2611.12976v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-11-08
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.CV, cs.LG
  • Eje Cognitivo: Prompt Engineering, Meta-Prompting & In-Context Optimization
  • Autores: Wei Zhang, Zhu Teng, Zhixiang Chi, Junnan Zou, Yi Liu, Jianping Fan
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12976)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12976) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.12976.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.12976.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Open-vocabulary object detection (OVD) expects the model trained on base classes to recognize both base and novel classes at inference. Typical methods rely on CLIP-style vision-language embeddings, yet the learned space is often biased toward base categories, with limited effective capacity preserved for novel concepts. Such a base-dominated representation can compress novel semantics, leading to semantic collapse and mis-calibrated confidence at inference. To address this issue, we present PR-OVD, a partition-and-refine framework that explicitly reserves capacity for novel categories and strengthens category separation, while being seamlessly integrated into different detector architectures. Specifically, PR-OVD first performs coarse partitioning to build global divisions for base, novel, and an explicit background partition in both visual and textual modalities, preventing object-irrelevant embeddings from leaking into category-specific representations. It then applies category-level refinement within each partition to sharpen decision boundaries and alleviate the lack of fine-grained region-level pretraining in CLIP. To further provide reliable visual anchors for novel categories, we design a cognitively inspired Structure Prompt (SP) that encodes both egocentric attributes and allocentric context, facilitating stable partition-and-refine learning. Extensive experiments on two OVD benchmarks show that PR-OVD delivers an average 6.5\% proportional improvement on novel categories over the state-of-the-art methods. Notably, PR-OVD also benefits other open-vocabulary tasks, including segmentation and classification, further confirming its effectiveness and generality.

[arXiv:2611.11261] Chatter Attack: Resource Consumption Attack for Large Language Models

  • arXiv ID: 2611.11261 (2611.11261v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-11-08
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.LG, cs.AI, cs.CR, cs.CV, math.OC
  • Eje Cognitivo: Prompt Engineering, Meta-Prompting & In-Context Optimization
  • Autores: Yibo Miao, Yichuan Cao, Xiao-Shan Gao, Yinpeng Dong
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11261)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11261) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.11261.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.11261.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> LLMs are powerful but incur substantial inference cost, which can be exploited by malicious users to induce overly long outputs, increasing latency and operational expense. Prior resource consumption attacks either coerce repetition or suppress the end-of-sequence token by lowering its logit value. However, with autoregressive, sampling-based generation, the search space grows exponentially with length and small deviations or unstable trajectories can derail optimization, limiting attack effectiveness and stability. To address this issue, we introduce Chatter Attack, a novel class of resource consumption attacks that optimize adversarial prompts to induce predefined short target phrases, thereby avoiding search-space explosion while still eliciting long responses. Specifically, Chatter Attack triggers self-doubt using short doubt-inducing phrases as the target, and sustains this state throughout generation via inference attention loss and global entropy loss. To extend to black-box settings, we further propose a novel Bayesian Prompt Optimization (BPO) method, which models the objective function globally by constructing a discrete token kernel, efficiently exploring the entire search space using prior information. Extensive experiments across multiple models and datasets show that our method outperforms baselines, with maximum response length increases to 31.5$\times$, and revealing practical resource-depletion risks for real-world LLM inference services.

[arXiv:2611.10411] Tail-risk-aware Agent Safety: Constraining Tail Risks Across Multi-step LLM Agent Workflows

  • arXiv ID: 2611.10411 (2611.10411v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-11-07
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.AI
  • Eje Cognitivo: Prompt Engineering, Meta-Prompting & In-Context Optimization
  • Autores: Shilong Pan, Zhiliang Tian, Zihan Chen, Wentao Zou, Weiguo Chen, DongSheng Li
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10411)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10411) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10411.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10411.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> LLM-based agents extend LLMs with tool calls, memory, planning, and retrieval to act in external environments, but they are vulnerable to attacks at different workflow steps. Existing defenses, including prompt engineering and post-training alignment, primarily improve LLM-only safety and often overlook risks that arise from multi-step execution. External verification adds guardrails to check each step separately, but it typically ignores how risks can propagate across steps. We argue that we should consider risk transitions across dependent steps through the agent workflow, rather than separate verification on a single step. In this paper, we propose \textbf{T}ail-risk-aware \textbf{A}gent \textbf{S}afety (TAS), a tail-risk-aware safety framework that models agent workflow safety as a sequence of dependent safety states and constrains the rare but extreme risks (i.e., tail risk) with a shared budget. TAS first models safety-state transitions sequentially along the workflow as an MDP to trace risk propagation, then measures a tail-sensitive risk to target rare but high-cost risks (i.e., tail risk), and constrains the risk with a shared budget to derive a step-wise safety strategy. By constraining tail risk rather than the overall average, TAS suppresses rare high-cost failures while preserving utility in normal cases. Experiments show that TAS achieves better performance compared with eleven baselines across three benchmarks.

[arXiv:2611.09699] Verifier Noise Is a Temperature: Testing Tilt Predictions in Finite-Budget LLM-Guided Search

  • arXiv ID: 2611.09699 (2611.09699v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-11-06
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.LG, cs.AI
  • Eje Cognitivo: Prompt Engineering, Meta-Prompting & In-Context Optimization
  • Autores: Xiaomeng Li
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09699)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09699) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.09699.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.09699.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> LLM-guided search loops propose a modified candidate, score it, and keep it if it scores better. When both candidates are re-scored at every comparison, as in the minibatch acceptance gates of prompt optimizers, keep-if-better is a reversible Markov chain if the score difference is logistic and the mutation operator is reversible, and its stationary law is the mutation equilibrium tilted by exp(beta f). For any continuous symmetric noise difference the same tilt is the stationary law of a small-step diffusion limit, with beta = 4F'(0); for a minibatch gate beta grows with the square root of the minibatch size. Caching the incumbent's score instead inflates the recorded score. We test the tilt as a prediction for LLM-guided program search, committing each prediction from calibration data before the search runs. The level after 150 steps rises with the minibatch size in six of seven settings, but its predicted value fails: on bin packing, search attains 62-88% of the predicted gain when the proposer edits the parent and 109-119% when it writes new heuristics. The attained fraction falls with the parent-child quality correlation of unselected mutation; a registered test on two new settings confirmed the direction, but not the quantitative agreement after a correction. Further registered tests found that runs started from a good program kept losing quality over 300 steps, more so from starts that search had selected, by a margin that passed its registered rule narrowly. Runs from different starts had not reached a common level, so whether the tilt describes the stationary law of LLM editing remains open. A registered test in prompt optimisation on frozen task-model scores failed: search exceeded the tilt, and the bin-packing relation did not transfer.

[arXiv:2611.08643] FinEvolveKG: Controlled and Attributable Prompt Evolution for Financial Knowledge Graph Construction

  • arXiv ID: 2611.08643 (2611.08643v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-11-05
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.CL, cs.AI
  • Eje Cognitivo: Prompt Engineering, Meta-Prompting & In-Context Optimization
  • Autores: Abhinav Arun, Jayita Bhattacharyya, Fabrizio Dimino, Bhaskarjit Sarmah, Stefano Pasquali
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08643)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08643) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.08643.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.08643.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Financial institutions construct knowledge graphs (KGs) from shared SEC 10-Ks to serve risk analysis, portfolio management, and investment research, whose analytical needs differ. Extraction must adapt without fragmenting shared grounding, typing, and temporal rules. Hand-authored persona prompts and fine-tuning add cost. Weight-free prompt optimization offers an alternative, but unconstrained evolution of a monolithic prompt can rewrite shared rules alongside persona guidance, obscuring changes and complicating review. We introduce FinEvolveKG, a KG-construction harness separating reusable general and persona-policy blocks and mechanically confining GEPA, a reflective prompt optimizer, to the designated block. During persona adaptation, the approved general block remains byte-stable and every optimizer edit is localized to a separately versioned persona policy. A dual objective balances generic temporal-KG quality with persona-relevant facts, motifs, and multi-hop connectivity. We evaluate five mutation scopes on ticker-disjoint 10-K splits, with three seeds for the persona-conditioned arms (v3-v5) across three personas. On the three-seed mean, the complete two-stage block-constrained workflow (v4) improves over the frozen prompt baseline (v1) by 19.4-30.9% and over the tested unconstrained full-prompt workflow (v5) by 2.2-6.4%, leading v5 on both objective components and winning all nine paired-seed comparisons with the lowest seed variance in every persona. Without further optimization, v4 also exceeds v5 across all evaluated out-of-distribution (OOD) shifts. An SME audit supports using the generated triplets as ground truth; a blinded two-SME audit favors v4 over v5 in 70.1% of contrast-enriched judgments. These results show that mechanically attributable evolution can coexist with strong empirical performance, without attributing the gains causally to the boundary.

[arXiv:2611.08226] "Blind Compliance": Coercive Control in AI Jailbreaking

  • arXiv ID: 2611.08226 (2611.08226v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-11-05
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.CY, cs.CL, cs.HC
  • Eje Cognitivo: Prompt Engineering, Meta-Prompting & In-Context Optimization
  • Autores: Acacia L. Ackles, Elliott Whitson
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08226)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08226) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.08226.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.08226.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> The explosion of generative AI systems and concerns surrounding their rapidly scaling capabilities has led to an adversarial cycle between increasingly narrow content restrictions ("guardrails") and increasingly sophisticated structured prompting techniques ("jailbreaks"). Technological frameworks surrounding such jailbreaking remain largely focused on the system's output: whether harmful or disallowed content is ultimately produced for the user. However, this approach, which draws largely from classical cybersecurity, does not address the uniquely relational dimension introduced by generative AI: in many cases the user is not just accessing a technology via an exploit, but is in conversation with it as part of the exploit. Here we present an alternative lens which centers the interaction rather than the output. Drawing on interpersonal violence scholarship, we present a novel framework of coercive control categories with a particular focus on aspects which are embodied versus disembodied. We then map how these disembodied coercive control dynamics are structurally embedded in modern AI training paradigms, and how those embeddings are exploited via a particular suite of persona-based jailbreaks. In this lens, we reframe AI safety and alignment as not merely a technical security problem, but as another surface upon which we see enacted the relational dynamics of power and control.

[arXiv:2611.07652] Don't Prompt Your Agents. Equip Them: Skillware as a Modular Standard for Sovereign, Auditable, and Deterministic Capability Execution for Logical Systems

  • arXiv ID: 2611.07652 (2611.07652v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-11-05
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.AI, cs.SE
  • Eje Cognitivo: Prompt Engineering, Meta-Prompting & In-Context Optimization
  • Autores: Vladimiros Peilivanidis
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07652)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07652) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.07652.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.07652.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Current autonomous logical systems rely on a fragile assumption: that language models should dynamically write code, guess API payloads, and memorize operational constraints through prompt engineering. In practice, this burns massive context windows, inflates API bills, and produces brittle agents that fail unpredictably in production. In this work, we present Skillware, an open architectural framework and capability registry that formally separates what an agent wants to do from how that task is executed. Instead of prompting models with loose text instructions or markdown files baptized as 'skills', Skillware equips them with modular, versioned software capability bundles. Much like skill hotkeys in video games that execute deterministically under fixed world rules, each bundle combines a typed JSON input contract, deterministic Python code, task directives, bundled datasets, and automated unit tests. In-process adapters translate these capabilities into native tool declarations for any model, from frontier cloud APIs to local open-weight runtimes with zero network latency. Evaluating 23 frontier and open-weight models across 368 benchmark conditions, we show that small local models (3B to 8B parameters) equipped with Skillware consistently outperform unequipped frontier models on complex multi-step workflows. They cut token usage by up to 84.8%, slash inference costs by up to 97.3%, and eliminate parameter errors entirely. True agent reliability does not come from larger models or longer prompts, but from equipping logical systems with verified, deterministic software. Finally, we articulate the trajectory of capability bundles across an invariant continuum of information processing systems: from digital logical systems today, to IoT, drones, and embodied robotics tomorrow, establishing a foundation for auditable, real-time capability execution.

Reglas Operativas Extraídas (Aplicación en Inferencia y Razonamiento)

1. [Directiva de Prompt Engineering, Meta-Prompting & In-Context Optimization]: Anclar el contexto mediante restricciones explícitas de formato, ejemplos verificados en contexto (few-shot grounding), delimitación estricta de fuentes autorizadas y descomposición modular de instrucciones.

2. [Supervisión Granular por Pasos (Step-Level PRM)]: Validar secuencialmente cada considerando o nodo de decisión. Si un paso intermedio presenta incertidumbre o ambigüedad, podar esa rama de razonamiento y recalcular desde el último antecedente verificado.

3. [Asignación Dinámica de Cómputo de Inferencia]: Calibrar la profundidad deliberativa según la complejidad del caso: aplicar resolución directa en trámites estandarizados y expandir el árbol de búsqueda (múltiples candidatos + ranking) en casos con colisión normativa o jurisprudencial.

4. [Descomposición Agéntica y Uso de Herramientas]: Dividir la tarea compleja en subtareas atómicas verificables (extracción de hechos -> búsqueda de precedentes -> encuadre legal -> redacción -> control de calidad), invocando herramientas externas de recuperación antes de razonar en abstracto.

[arXiv:2611.07123] PromptOps: Iterative Prompt Optimization for LLM-as-a-Judge Systems

  • arXiv ID: 2611.07123 (2611.07123v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-11-04
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.AI, cs.LG
  • Eje Cognitivo: Prompt Engineering, Meta-Prompting & In-Context Optimization
  • Autores: Sven Kleinkop, Tamás Janusko, Ricardo Bochnia, Gunnar Hempel, Steffen Tomschke, Jürgen Anke et al.
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07123)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07123) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.07123.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.07123.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Large language models (LLMs) are increasingly used as automated judges for evaluating the behavior of chatbots and other AI systems. In deployment, however, their agreement with the intended human evaluation policy may degrade as user interactions and score distributions change. Existing automatic prompt optimization methods are primarily studied as one-shot procedures on static development sets and therefore provide limited guidance for maintaining LLM judges over time. We frame this problem as judge drift and introduce PromptOps, a closedloop framework for monitoring and iteratively recalibrating LLM-as-a-Judge (LLMaaJ) systems. PromptOps separates four operational decisions: when to optimize, which optimization procedure to apply, which feedback records to use, and how to update the judge prompt. We instantiate the framework with five prompt optimization methods: GEPA, SIMBA, Rubric Synthesis, and exemplar selection using either simulated annealing or a Tree-structured Parzen Estimator. Our experiments demonstrate that iterative prompt optimization can recalibrate LLM judges and recover performance after controlled shifts in the referencescore distribution. However, effective maintenance is inherently contextdependent: neither optimization algorithm choice nor update timing generalizes universally across settings. These findings establish prompt optimization for deployed LLM judges as an inherently iterative maintenance problem and demonstrate that PromptOps provides a practical abstraction for studying and implementing this process.

3. [Estructuración de Contexto y Restricciones Explícitas]: Formular las restricciones negativas (prohibición de alucinar metadatos) y las plantillas estructurales al inicio del bloque de instrucciones, utilizando precedentes reales recuperados como anclaje in-context.

4. [Búsqueda Deliberativa y Consenso Interno]: Generar y contrastar múltiples líneas argumentales (argumento principal, eventuales defensas y criterio de Alzada) seleccionando la solución que maximice la coherencia interna y la estabilidad frente a una eventual apelación.

[arXiv:2611.05039] ALBI: LLM-Guided Inductive Logic Programming via Iterative Generate-Test-Constrain Refineme

  • arXiv ID: 2611.05039 (2611.05039v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-11-03
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.AI, cs.SC
  • Eje Cognitivo: Prompt Engineering, Meta-Prompting & In-Context Optimization
  • Autores: Aboubaker Seddiq Benterki, Jüri Vain, Gert Kanter, Leelo Aino Jürgenstein
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05039)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05039) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.05039.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.05039.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> We introduce ALBI (Agent for Learning By Induction), a neuro-symbolic ILP framework that replaces the ASP-based hypothesis generator in the Learning from Failures (LFF) paradigm with a Large Language Model (LLM), while retaining SWI-Prolog as a formal verifier. ALBI implements an iterative generate-test-constrain loop: the LLM proposes candidate hypotheses from structured prompts; Prolog tests each candidate; and diagnostic feedback drives refinement. We further introduce semantic anonymization, a metamorphic testing methodology, to distinguish structural symbolic induction from surface-level pattern matching on predicate names. Experiments on 30 standard ILP benchmarks show that ALBI achieves 100% success across three frontier LLMs (Gemini 3.1 Pro, GPT 5.4 Pro, Claude Opus 4.6), matching Popper while requiring only 1.4-1.7 iterations on average. Under anonymization, all models maintain 100% success with a moderate iteration increase (1.36-1.67 times), providing evidence of robust symbolic reasoning.

[arXiv:2611.04342] Beyond Fine-Tuning: Evaluating Prompt Engineering and Prompt Tuning for Roman Urdu Hate Speech Detection

  • arXiv ID: 2611.04342 (2611.04342v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-11-03
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.AI
  • Eje Cognitivo: Prompt Engineering, Meta-Prompting & In-Context Optimization
  • Autores: Toneema Zubair, Muhammad Junaid Asif, Faisal Kamiran, Hafiz Hassan Saeed, Rana Fayyaz Ahmad
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04342)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04342) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.04342.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.04342.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Low Resource Languages (LRLs) pose significant challenges for hate speech detection because of a scarcity of readily available annotated data, language variation, non-standard spelling, and context-dependent ambiguity. Beyond the problem of informal conventions of writing in Roman Urdu, there is also the issue of code-mixing. After working on the previous project about zero-shot learning and parameter-efficient fine-tuning (PEFT), this research systematically compares prompt engineering and prompt tuning for Roman Urdu hate speech detection. To study the effect of in-context examples, we will compare the zero-shot and few-shot prompting approaches. To understand the advantage of structured prompting and learnable prompt representations, we will conduct studies of manual vs. mixed prompt-based adaptation strategies. Experimentations are carried out on the PURUT dataset (~72,771 social media comments) with GPT-3.5, LLaMA-3, Mistral-7B, and multilingual BERT in several settings. Experimental results show that few-shot prompting always outperforms zero-shot prompting, with the Mistral-7B model scoring 0.67 F1 compared to 0.56 of the zero-shot models. Based on this observation, prompt-based adaptation is proposed to introduce additional improvements, with the proposed mixed prompt configuration able to yield a F1-score up to 0.87 which further surpasses the performance of regular manual prompts. Despite the larger size of the model, its performance trade-off between inference-based prompting, prompt optimization, and parameter-efficient model adaptation is compared to the previously reported PEFT-based F1-score, which is ~0.93.

[arXiv:2611.04225] IntentGEO: Intent-aware Generative Engine Optimization for Recommendation

  • arXiv ID: 2611.04225 (2611.04225v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-11-03
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.IR, cs.AI
  • Eje Cognitivo: Prompt Engineering, Meta-Prompting & In-Context Optimization
  • Autores: Runlin Lei, Shiqi Zhang, Xiaokui Xiao, Zhewei Wei
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04225)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04225) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.04225.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.04225.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Large language models (LLMs) increasingly shape recommendations across chatbots, e-commerce, and user-generated content (UGC) platforms, creating demand for Generative Engine Optimization (GEO) to improve item visibility in generated responses. Existing GEO studies are mainly content-centric, optimizing visibility under general-purpose queries. However, a shared item description usually serves users with different needs and decision goals, resulting in its visibility gains being dependent on the request. To investigate how changing intent affects visibility, we formulate IntentGEO to evaluate shared rewrites across need specificity and decision progression. We revisit existing GEO methods and find that their visibility gains vary across user intents. More critically, learning-based prompt optimization can underperform its handwritten seed on average. Tracing these updates reveals prompt drift from explicit recommendation instructions toward generic promotional tactics. To address this drift, we propose AMO (Anchored Meta-Optimization), which preserves recommendation instructions in a fixed core while learning appended strategies from citation feedback. Across four domains and ten intent states, AMO improves average citation-rank gain by 35% over the strongest prior learning-based baseline and leads on nine states. Ablations show that learned additions improve on the fixed core alone and that AMO outperforms full-prompt optimization initialized from that core. It also achieves the highest gains in an agentic recommendation pipeline and, after separate adaptation, on Doubao, a commercially deployed, closed-source online assistant. These findings highlight user intent as a key dimension of GEO evaluation and support anchored adaptation across recommendation needs and decision stages.

[arXiv:2611.02341] AgiWorld: An Agentic Video World Model for Building Persistent Worlds

  • arXiv ID: 2611.02341 (2611.02341v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-11-02
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.CV, cs.AI
  • Eje Cognitivo: Prompt Engineering, Meta-Prompting & In-Context Optimization
  • Autores: Shaoheng Fang, Yiqun Mei, Hanwen Jiang, Xin Sun, Haitian Zheng, Hao Tan et al.
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02341)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02341) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.02341.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.02341.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Recent interactive video world models generate visually compelling videos from a single image, following user-provided camera motion and text prompts. However, such low-level control demands substantial user effort to realize a high-level intent, and the generated world struggles to remain persistent as the exploration proceeds. In this work, we present AgiWorld, an agentic video world model that explores and builds a persistent world from a single image and a free-form instruction. For high-level planning, our agent AgiWorld-Agent designs the world and the journey through it in a world state and directs the exploration. For low-level realization, our video world model AgiWorld-WM generates the video chunk by chunk and stores it with its geometry in a spatial memory, from which the agent updates its world state. This closed loop keeps the world faithful to the user's intent and keeps the agent's world state consistent with the generated content throughout the exploration. We co-design the two levels around the shared spatial memory and an interface of metric camera trajectories and structured prompts. In AgiWorld-WM, to handle occlusion and accumulated geometric errors in the spatial memory over long rollouts, we propose occlusion-aware history selection, which retrieves past views by surface visibility, and reliability-based memory maintenance, which prefers reliable geometry and earlier observations. AgiWorld-WM achieves state-of-the-art overall performance on world revisiting and exploration benchmarks, and qualitative results and ablations show that AgiWorld builds persistent worlds from free-form instructions over minute-long journeys. Persistence thus arises not from the video model alone but from its closed loop with the agent.

[arXiv:2611.01954] Towards Self-Evolving Narrative Systems: Real-Time Adaptation and Long-Term Optimization with LLMs

> Traditional interactive narrative systems offer strong authorial control and structural coherence but often restrict player agency. To navigate this trade-off, we propose a hybrid narrative framework that dynamically balances pre-authored content with real-time LLM generation via semantic similarity alignment. Through a case study and participant-based evaluation, we show that our framework enhances narrative coherence while preserving player agency, effectively aligning generated content with both authorial intent and evolving player behavior.

[arXiv:2611.01135] Compile as Hypothesis: A Headroom Typology for Prompt Optimization on Real Reasoning Models

  • arXiv ID: 2611.01135 (2611.01135v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-10-31
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.AI
  • Eje Cognitivo: Prompt Engineering, Meta-Prompting & In-Context Optimization
  • Autores: Zhengshuai Yang, Changyun Li
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01135)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01135) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.01135.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.01135.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Automated prompt compilers search for or rewrite prompts under the implicit assumption that optimization consistently improves downstream task performance. We evaluate this assumption on frontier reasoning models through rigorous paired, same-day measurements. Across four optimizer families (genome search, profile-guided mutation, GEPA, and ESPO) and eight tasks, structured search yields no statistically significant accuracy gains on strong reasoners. However, a discrete structural genome provides essential architectural stability, preventing catastrophic bimodal seed-collapse (where unconstrained search collapses toward the judge floor in 3/5 runs). Through 190-problem evaluations on knowledge-intensive benchmarks, we establish a three-dimensional headroom typology: prompt optimization cannot compensate for intrinsic knowledge deficits or physical token truncation, but successfully resolves protocol and formatting errors. On gpt-6, while free-text reflective search (GEPA) yields a +.14 gain on a task where structured search remains flat, qualitative ablation reveals that ~.035 stems from judge-rubric reconstruction while ~.105 reflects genuine instruction refinement. Our pre-registered AutoAPC-Select deployment gate matches the oracle policy in 5/7 groups and rescues seed-collapse arms with zero regret. Finally, we expose silent backend-mixing in commercial APIs during outage windows and provide automated per-call audit verification tools to ensure empirical evaluation fidelity.

[arXiv:2610.37651] GEPA-Curator: Curated Learning for Self Improving Agents

  • arXiv ID: 2610.37651 (2610.37651v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-10-27
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.CL
  • Eje Cognitivo: Prompt Engineering, Meta-Prompting & In-Context Optimization
  • Autores: Zack Yu, Nicholas Lee, Minjae Lee, Lakshya Agarwal, Luca Manolache, Wonjun Kang et al.
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37651)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37651) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.37651.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.37651.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Large Language Models (LLMs) are increasingly used in multi-turn agentic workloads that require sustained interaction with complex environments. Existing reflective prompt optimizers such as GEPA learn from failed rollouts, but agentic failures can also come from prompt weaknesses, harness limitations, stochastic execution, or noisy judges. We argue that such failures may not provide useful learning signal, and may even be harmful; and thus it is essential to curate which tasks are provided to the prompt optimizer for better performance. We introduce GEPA-Curator, a framework that augments the prompt optimizer to select which agent experiences should shape prompt optimization. In particular, GEPA-Curator consists of two components: task curation, which filters out tasks that are likely to provide noisy or unreliable learning signals, and prior success retrieval, which selectively retrieves past successful trajectories for a task and provides them as additional context to the prompt optimizer. We evaluate GEPA-Curator on GoBrowse, ALFWorld, AppWorld, and $τ$-bench, using both Gemini 3.1 Pro and Claude Opus 5 as optimizer models. GEPA-Curator matches or exceeds the mean performance of both baselines, with gains of up to 20.7% over GEPA and 21.2% over ACE.

[arXiv:2610.37583] Zero-Shot Multimodal Event Spotting in Soccer Broadcasts

  • arXiv ID: 2610.37583 (2610.37583v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-10-27
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.CV, cs.LG, cs.MM
  • Eje Cognitivo: Prompt Engineering, Meta-Prompting & In-Context Optimization
  • Autores: Sadia Kamal, Mahmudul Hasan, Tony Braskich
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37583)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37583) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.37583.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.37583.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Automatic soccer event spotting supports highlight generation, video indexing, and sports analytics, but existing methods depend on large annotated datasets and soccer-specific training. We present a zero-shot framework that detects, classifies, and temporally localizes events in full-length soccer broadcasts without task-specific parameter training. Each match is segmented into overlapping clips represented by a compact visual storyboard and the aligned commentary transcript, which a general-purpose multimodal model processes jointly. On three manually annotated matches containing 130 events, the best configuration achieves 72.0% F1@5s and outperforms SoccerNet-trained CALF and NetVLAD++ in a cross-dataset setting, while the storyboard representation reduces inference cost by 72% compared with individual frames. We further analyze the effects of model family, input representation, and prompt optimization, and introduce an evidence-grounded excitement-scoring stage that ranks detected events with natural-language explanations for highlight selection.

[arXiv:2610.36146] From Structural Feedback to Prompt Policies: Learning Faithful Text-to-Image Prompt Editors

  • arXiv ID: 2610.36146 (2610.36146v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-10-26
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.AI
  • Eje Cognitivo: Prompt Engineering, Meta-Prompting & In-Context Optimization
  • Autores: Wentao Ye, Yali Ye, Zhiqing Xiao, Ru Peng, Xinpeng Ti, Liyao Li et al.
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36146)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36146) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.36146.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.36146.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Prompt optimization should improve the image a user receives without changing the scene the user requested. Yet stylistic rewriting can weaken object attributes and relations even when global text--image similarity remains high. We propose StruPO, a structure-aware prompt editor that uses a calibrated scene graph as a shared representation for policy conditioning and image-grounded supervision. The graph exposes what should be preserved, while deletion, reordering, and insertion provide a token-aligned way to modify the prompt. This turns structural feedback from an external critique into a training signal for a reusable editing policy. Learning from that signal requires handling both uncertain grounding and stochastic image generation. StruPO retains grounding confidence through smooth reward shaping and averages multiple images for each edit before computing group-relative advantages. Experiments span three disjoint 2K-prompt test sets and four frozen generation backbones, with comparisons to learned prompt optimizers, iterative structural feedback, diffusion-model fine-tuning, and inference-time attention control. StruPO improves the faithfulness--aesthetics trade-off, with consistent gains in relational fidelity. Independent automatic evaluators and a 15-annotator study provide complementary evidence, and human preference margins increase with relation complexity. Controlled ablations connect these gains to graph conditioning, local editing, and image-grounded learning. The resulting policy produces reusable optimized text in one pass after graph calibration, without an iterative critique--rewrite loop.

[arXiv:2611.00102] Demystifying Prompt Stealing Attacks against Synthetic Video

  • arXiv ID: 2611.00102 (2611.00102v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-10-26
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.CR
  • Eje Cognitivo: Prompt Engineering, Meta-Prompting & In-Context Optimization
  • Autores: Junyan Huang, Sanskar Agmain, Chen Gong, Yan Pang, FNU Suya
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00102)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00102) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.00102.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.00102.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Creators publicly showcase generated content to advertise and sell their work, but these examples may also enable unauthorized reproduction and customization. We study whether an adversary can derive reusable inputs from a single public video for faithful reconstruction and controlled editing, without access to the original prompt or generator. We adapt captioning and prompt optimization approaches to video prompt stealing attacks (PSAs) and find that prompt recovery alone provides limited reconstruction fidelity. Even the original prompt produces different spatial and temporal realizations when the initial noise changes. Using an independent generator enlarges the reconstruction gap, as reusing the original prompt and seed can still produce substantially different videos. We propose V-PSA, which uses a recovered caption and DDIM inversion to construct a reference trajectory under an independent generator. Null-Text Inversion then optimizes per-step unconditional embeddings to reduce deviations from this trajectory during reconstruction. The resulting inputs also guide generation when the caption is modified, supporting edits that change selected content while preserving the target's layout and motion. We construct a benchmark containing 12,000 video-prompt pairs from three victim generators and four prompt sources. V-PSA achieves video-to-video similarity around 0.9 between target and reconstructed videos, substantially improving over prompt-only baselines with similarity score of 0.6, and supports multiple controlled variations that preserve the target's structure. Additional experiments show that augmenting prior image PSAs with inversion-derived inputs also improves reconstruction. Our results show that evaluations restricted to prompt recovery can underestimate an attacker's ability to reconstruct public generative outputs.
