2. [Asignación Dinámica de Cómputo de Inferencia]: Calibrar la profundidad deliberativa según la complejidad del caso: aplicar resolución directa en trámites estandarizados y expandir el árbol de búsqueda (múltiples candidatos + ranking) en casos con colisión normativa o jurisprudencial.

3. [Descomposición Agéntica y Uso de Herramientas]: Dividir la tarea compleja en subtareas atómicas verificables (extracción de hechos -> búsqueda de precedentes -> encuadre legal -> redacción -> control de calidad), invocando herramientas externas de recuperación antes de razonar en abstracto.

[arXiv:2611.03066] Towards a Trustworthy and Governable Internet of Agentics: An Architectural Perspective on Protocols, Systems, and Services

  • arXiv ID: 2611.03066 (2611.03066v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-11-02
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.AI, cs.DC
  • Eje Cognitivo: Agentic Workflows, Multi-Agent Systems & Tool-Augmented Reasoning
  • Autores: Haoyi Xiong, Yi Liu, Jiang Bian, Peng Diao, Bin Zhu, Tingting Hu et al.
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03066)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03066) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.03066.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.03066.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Large language model applications are increasingly being organized as tool-using and multi-agent workflows rather than as isolated chat interfaces. This perspective paper examines the architectural problem that follows from that shift: agents may reason, invoke tools, and delegate work, but dependable deployment still requires interoperability across frameworks, runtimes, data boundaries, and organizational control domains. We study this problem through the lens of the \emph{Internet of Agentics} (IoA), an emerging service-computing view in which heterogeneous agents are treated as governable service participants rather than only as stand-alone model endpoints. The paper organizes the landscape as a three-layer progression: from today's fragmented agent ecosystem, to interoperable agents, and ultimately to trustworthy and governable autonomy. First, it examines the protocol stack centered on the Agentic AI Foundation, including MCP for agent--tool interaction; A2A for agent--agent coordination; AGENTS.md and Skills-style procedural manifests; and discovery, transport, and trust mechanisms such as AGNTCY and SLIM. Second, it reviews the systems abstractions that make agents deployable as service components, including logical agent architectures, orchestration patterns, gateways, registries, runtime control loops, and hardware-aware inference infrastructure. Third, it situates these developments within services computing, arguing that agentic systems reinterpret classical concerns from SOA and microservices---discovery, composition, governance, pricing, and trust---at the level of cognitive capabilities rather than deterministic functions.

[arXiv:2611.02104] Consolidating Reasoning with Test-Time Training

  • arXiv ID: 2611.02104 (2611.02104v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-11-01
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.CL, cs.AI
  • Eje Cognitivo: Agentic Workflows, Multi-Agent Systems & Tool-Augmented Reasoning
  • Autores: Haotian Wu, Zeming Chen, Hao Zhao, Antoine Bosselut
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02104)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02104) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.02104.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.02104.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Effective multi-agent collaboration requires finalizing solutions from reasoning that is distributed across the individual attempts of many agents. However, useful intermediate reasoning can remain unexploited when collaboration is limited to voting on conclusions reached among individual agents, or aggregating long, noisy collections of reasoning trajectories in context. In this work, we propose TTT-Consolidate, a framework that uses multi-agent reasoning trajectories as adaptation data to improve a model's subsequent reasoning. TTT-Consolidate jointly consolidates trajectories into parametric representations through test-time training, then generates a new solution by conditioning on the consolidated information. We meta-learn how to consolidate using bi-level optimization at training time, where an inner loop encodes reasoning trajectories into a lightweight LoRA adapter, and an outer loop learns meta-parameters for the inner loop such that the consolidation is helpful for the model to synthesize a final solution. As no large-scale corpus of diverse reasoning trajectories exists for training aggregation methods, we construct the OpenParallelThinking corpus composed of 12,810 math problems paired with a mixture of trajectories from six LLMs. Evaluated on challenging math and STEM benchmarks, TTT-Consolidate outperforms the best aggregation baselines by up to 20 percentage points, even under matched compute and runtime settings, with the gap widening at larger model scales. TTT-Consolidate also recovers from unsuccessful individual trajectories more robustly, an ability that improves as the number of available (even incorrect) trajectories increases. Finally, we demonstrate that TTT-Consolidate benefits from both SFT and RL-based outer loop objectives. We will release TTT-Consolidate and OpenParallelThinking for use in future work on reasoning aggregation.

[arXiv:2611.01840] Self-Evolving Agentic Workflows via Semantic Decomposition of Complex Tasks

  • arXiv ID: 2611.01840 (2611.01840v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-11-01
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.AI, cs.CL, cs.MA
  • Eje Cognitivo: Agentic Workflows, Multi-Agent Systems & Tool-Augmented Reasoning
  • Autores: Zichuan Fu, Junjia Qi, Xian Wu, Wenlin Zhang, Yimin Deng, Kaifeng Guo et al.
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01840)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01840) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.01840.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.01840.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Agentic workflows organize complex tasks into reusable procedures. However, invoking an LLM at every step incurs repeated inference costs and delays. We propose a framework for self-evolving agentic workflows through semantic task decomposition. Each subtask has explicit applicability conditions and a procedure that executes code or invokes a model as needed. An agent uses execution feedback to refine the decomposition and update these procedures. Experiments in the GLEE competition show the framework on bargaining, negotiation and persuasion. Most observed final-decision procedures are implemented in code. Code is available at https://github.com/Applied-Machine-Learning-Lab/NeurIPS2026_SeaFlow.

[arXiv:2611.01836] NEIGHBOR: Aligning Service Behaviour and Evidence Use in an LLM Agent for Community Resident Services

  • arXiv ID: 2611.01836 (2611.01836v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-11-01
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.AI
  • Eje Cognitivo: Agentic Workflows, Multi-Agent Systems & Tool-Augmented Reasoning
  • Autores: Zongyou Yang, Yinghan Hou, Xiaokun Yang
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01836)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01836) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.01836.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.01836.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Residents who contact a community service desk about a repair or a service charge need answers they can act on, with facts that hold for their case and commitments the desk can keep. In tool-augmented LLM agents, such facts come from documents and records, and the conduct of a service answer is usually specified in a system prompt. We present NEIGHBOR, an LLM agent for community resident services that trains this conduct into the model that drives its tools. NEIGHBOR is deployed on a live resident-service platform for a district of an East Asian city of more than ten million people. It adapts Qwen3-235B-A22B with LoRA on 7,824 supervised fine-tuning examples built from public community-service materials around six service-behaviour targets, among them commitment control and human referral, and the adapted model plans document retrieval and read-only text-to-SQL over the resident's own records. In an offline evaluation with seven configurations, an independent domain expert rated all 350 answers to 50 stratified held-out queries. NEIGHBOR scores 7.74/10 against 5.86 for the same agent running the un-tuned model (+1.88; paired Wilcoxon $p<10^{-8}$; higher on 46 of 50 queries) and improves all six rated dimensions. No tool-augmented answer received a red-line flag (standalone models: 8-14% of answers). An LLM judge ranks the seven configurations much as the expert does (Spearman $ρ=0.93$). Its rubric counts specifics absent from the reference answer as fabricated, so it scores tool-grounded answers 1-2 points lower, enough to change which configuration ranks first. For resident-facing agents, service behaviour is a practical fine-tuning target that raises expert-rated answer quality with the tools held fixed.

[arXiv:2611.01566] Principled Human-AI Collaboration in Agentic Workflows

  • arXiv ID: 2611.01566 (2611.01566v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-11-01
  • Categorías arXiv: stat.ML, cs.AI, cs.LG, stat.ME
  • Eje Cognitivo: Agentic Workflows, Multi-Agent Systems & Tool-Augmented Reasoning
  • Autores: Young Hyun Cho, Will Wei Sun
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01566)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01566) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.01566.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.01566.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Organizations adopting AI face the challenge of deciding which outputs can be released without human review while meeting the quality standards of human experts. We develop a statistical framework for delegating tasks between experts and black-box generate-verify workflows, in which AI-generated answers are verified and revised over multiple rounds. Before deployment, human experts evaluate selected outputs, and their judgments are used to calibrate the verifier threshold and number of attempts. Our method selects the policy with the highest estimated automation rate among statistically approved candidates, without modifying the generator or verifier. We establish finite-sample control of the error rate among released outputs, characterize the expert evaluations sufficient to attain a target automation rate, and derive a minimax lower bound establishing rate optimality in the quality margin and required confidence. In a MultiHiertt financial question-answering case study, average held-out automation increases from 2.50% to 24.17% as expert evaluations accumulate, while released-output error rates remain around 1.20%. In contrast, heuristics that release answers after consistently high verifier scores either violate the 5% error tolerance or automate at most 1.30% of tasks. Synthetic experiments further examine these theoretical predictions under controlled settings.

[arXiv:2611.01446] I-PTC: Interactive Programmatic Tool Calling for Stateful Tool-Augmented Agents

  • arXiv ID: 2611.01446 (2611.01446v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-11-01
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.AI, cs.LG
  • Eje Cognitivo: Agentic Workflows, Multi-Agent Systems & Tool-Augmented Reasoning
  • Autores: Huanzhi Mao, Chengkun Cao, Shuo Yuan, Joseph E. Gonzalez
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01446)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01446) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.01446.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.01446.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Large language models are increasingly capable of writing and executing code, suggesting a natural paradigm for tool-augmented agents: expressing tool use as programs rather than isolated calls. Programmatic tool calling (PTC) captures this idea through loops, batching, abstraction, and structured computation, but existing PTC approaches are largely stateless, limiting their ability to reuse intermediate results and adapt over time. This is problematic for real-world tasks, where tool environments and problem-solving processes are inherently stateful. We therefore present I-PTC, an interactive framework that executes stateful PTC: models issue code snippets that call tools, maintain reusable state, and evolve the tool interface through API corrections. To evaluate the effectiveness of I-PTC and the general PTC setting, we introduce PTC-Bench, a benchmark of long-tail, production-style user requests whose difficulty comes from large initial environments, many dependent API interactions, extended planning, and recovery across follow-ups and transient failures. Experiments on PTC-Bench show that I-PTC improves performance on complex multi-step tasks with large tool sets while substantially reducing token consumption over conventional tool-calling baselines and the original PTC baseline, highlighting stateful programmatic tool calling as a promising direction for scalable tool-augmented agents.

[arXiv:2611.01377] A Self-Exploring and Self-Evolving Paradigm for Adaptive Edge AI Development

  • arXiv ID: 2611.01377 (2611.01377v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-11-01
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.AI, cs.SE
  • Eje Cognitivo: Agentic Workflows, Multi-Agent Systems & Tool-Augmented Reasoning
  • Autores: Leming Shen, Qiang Yang, Xinyu Huang, Zijing Ma, Yuanqing Zheng, Chris Xiaoxuan Lu
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01377)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01377) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.01377.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.01377.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Large Language Models (LLMs) exhibit powerful code generation abilities, fostering agentic systems to automate edge AI application development. However, existing agents typically operate via fixed agentic workflows and predefined design spaces, struggling to handle rapidly evolving demands of real-world scenarios (e.g., heterogeneous data modalities, diverse task pipelines). Unlike prior systems that treat LLMs mainly as code generators following rigid workflows, we fully exploit their reasoning capabilities to allow agents to determine their own workflows on the fly. To this end, we propose SE2, a self-exploring and self-evolving paradigm for adaptive edge AI development. Inspired by human learning processes, we distill development into four atomic operations (retrieve, reason, code, test), and empower agents to explore workflows by dynamically and adaptively selecting an appropriate next operation based on the current context. Extensive evaluations show that, with adaptive workflows, the synthesized programs achieve up to 26% higher accuracy and consume 28% lower resources than state-of-the-art baselines, while maintaining superior adaptability to unforeseen edge AI scenarios. This highlights the potential of reasoning-driven adaptive agentic workflow in edge AI development.

[arXiv:2611.00620] Discovering Models for Experimental Quantum Systems with Agentic AI

  • arXiv ID: 2611.00620 (2611.00620v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-10-31
  • Categorías arXiv: quant-ph, cs.AI, physics.optics
  • Eje Cognitivo: Agentic Workflows, Multi-Agent Systems & Tool-Augmented Reasoning
  • Autores: Hannah C. Kleidermacher, Bennet Windt, Miguel Bello, James Zou, Jelena Vučković
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00620)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00620) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.00620.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.00620.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> A central obstacle in realizing color center cavity-QED-based quantum technologies is inhomogeneity, or disorder, among individual emitters. As the number of emitters increases, so does the complexity of the experimental data, making it difficult to quantify the present physical mechanisms of disorder. Here we introduce an artificial intelligence-driven model discovery framework in which a large language model (LLM)-based agent, equipped with tools and a structured workflow, proposes candidate Hamiltonian and Lindbladian models, generates computational tools, and quantitatively evaluates competing physical hypotheses. We apply the workflow to three previously published experimental cavity-QED datasets collected from color centers in diamond and silicon carbide. Across the experiments, the agent successfully identifies physical models for the system and characterizes the disorder. In assessing reproducibility, we find that run-to-run variability is concentrated primarily in the research plan generation step, while the computational analysis itself is substantially more consistent. Our results suggest that structured LLM agent workflows can support model discovery from quantum optical experimental data while simultaneously allowing for human expert supervision and collaboration.

[arXiv:2610.42147] Latent Sparse Attention: Towards Subquadratic Ultra-Long Context Attention

  • arXiv ID: 2610.42147 (2610.42147v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-10-30
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.AI
  • Eje Cognitivo: Agentic Workflows, Multi-Agent Systems & Tool-Augmented Reasoning
  • Autores: Ales Kubicek, Andrea Caciolai, Liangzu Peng, Sukrit Sumant, Benjamin Hoover, Fraser Greenlee et al.
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.42147)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.42147) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.42147.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.42147.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Agentic workflows are increasingly shifting towards long-horizon tasks, such as open-ended scientific work, where models benefit from long context windows. However, at ultra-long context (above 1M tokens), full attention is prohibitively expensive. A promising approach is sparse attention, in which a dedicated indexer selects a subset of keys for the attention. Yet current methods of indexing either still have quadratic cost in context size or lose accuracy at ultra-long context scale. We address this with Latent Sparse Attention (LSA), an efficient attention method that maintains accuracy at ultra-long context. In LSA, keys are hierarchically clustered in a learned latent space, and queries score the hierarchy of clusters instead of scoring every key, making prefill, decode and training faster at long context. We propose two variants, Distilled-LSA (D-LSA), which can be applied to pre-trained models via distillation, and Native-LSA (N-LSA), which is trained natively on the next-token loss alone. On GLM-5.3-FLASH (QWEN-3.8-27B) at 10M tokens, D-LSA speeds up one attention layer by 22.7x (72.4x) in prefill and 4.6x (42.5x) in decode over its original sparse attention (full attention), while keeping similar accuracy across a variety of tasks. N-LSA matches the accuracy of full attention with a training speedup of 1.6x per layer at 128k tokens. LSA offers practical and efficient attention for models to benefit from ultra-long context.

[arXiv:2610.41998] SecPO: Principled Adversarial Training for Prompt Injection Security

  • arXiv ID: 2610.41998 (2610.41998v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-10-30
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.CR, cs.AI
  • Eje Cognitivo: Agentic Workflows, Multi-Agent Systems & Tool-Augmented Reasoning
  • Autores: Sizhe Chen, Yibo Peng, Jaewon Chang, Chawin Sitawarin, David Wagner
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41998)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41998) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.41998.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.41998.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Recognized as a top threat to AI agents, prompt injection allows instructions from untrusted data (email, documents, websites) to manipulate the agent's operation. The existing best-defended LLM, Meta-SecAlign, is trained with the DPO loss but still suffers from attack success rates (ASRs) approaching 100% under adaptive prompt injections. One reason is that DPO anchors the model around the undefended reference model, which is insecure. We show an idealized reference model---an injection-invariant, utility-preserving oracle---can nevertheless be approximated, as its output distribution in training is interestingly accessible. This new observation motivates a theoretically principled SecPO loss. Another Meta-SecAlign shortcoming is that it trains on weak simulated attacks. We implement an efficient adversarial training (AT) pipeline that synthesizes strong injected inputs (best@240), and train the model on them. Our AT reduces ASRs by 1.5-14.5$\times$ across three SoTA adaptive attacks, compared with Meta-SecAlign; the SecPO loss reduces them by a further 2.4-4.6$\times$ compared with using DPO loss. In agentic workflows, our SecPO + AT Qwen3.6-27B is much more secure than Meta-SecAlign: 8.8% vs. 78.8% AgentDojo ASR@800 under the AttackerMovesSecond attack. Moreover, our defended model shows no noticeable reduction in benign utility in nine benchmarks covering agentic tool calling, instruction following, science, and math. We release our $\href{//github.com/Sizhe-Chen/SecPO}{\text{code}}$, $\href{//huggingface.co/datasets/Sizhe-Chen/Qwen3.6-27B-Instruct-SecPO-trainset}{\text{training data}}$ (with optimized injections from AT), and $\href{//huggingface.co/Sizhe-Chen/Qwen3.6-27B-Instruct-SecPO}{\text{model}}$.

[arXiv:2611.00171] Reimagining Text-to-SQL: A Survey of Large Language Model Architectures and Strategies

  • arXiv ID: 2611.00171 (2611.00171v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-10-30
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.AI
  • Eje Cognitivo: Agentic Workflows, Multi-Agent Systems & Tool-Augmented Reasoning
  • Autores: Shikhar Dave, Bhaskarjit Sarmah, Stefano Pasquali
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00171)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00171) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.00171.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.00171.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Text-to-SQL systems translate natural language questions into executable SQL queries, democratizing access to relational data. Early approaches relied on handcrafted rules and template matching, while subsequent neural methods introduced encoder-decoder architectures, schema linking, and constrained decoding to handle increasingly complex queries. The emergence of large language models (LLMs), exemplified by systems such as ReFoRCE, Agentar-Scale-SQL, Snowflake's Arctic-Text2SQL-R1, and the modular XiYan-SQL framework, has dramatically advanced the field, pushing execution accuracy to new state-of-the-art levels on challenging benchmarks such as Spider, Spider 2.0, and BIRD. In parallel, modular frameworks decompose the task into sub-problems (schema retrieval, sketch-based generation, repair, and ranking), while agentic systems coordinate multiple specialized agents that communicate and dynamically allocate sub-tasks. This survey systematically reviews these LLM-driven approaches within a unified taxonomy of five paradigms (in-context learning, supervised and parameter-efficient fine-tuning, agentic and tool-augmented orchestration, retrieval-augmented generation, and reinforcement learning) and characterizes them by model granularity (monolithic vs. modular), level of supervision (zero-shot, fine-tuned, or reinforcement-based), and reasoning style (direct decoding vs. decomposed multi-step reasoning). We further analyze evaluation metrics, datasets, and open challenges, including domain generalization, efficiency, and real-world deployment, and we examine the role of the semantic layer in production systems. As LLMs increasingly access sensitive databases, we also discuss responsible AI practices. We survey over 100 papers and technical resources, focusing on work published between 2022 and 2026.

[arXiv:2610.31974] Agentic AI Defense: Decode-Time Provenance Binding for MCP

  • arXiv ID: 2610.31974 (2610.31974v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-10-22
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.CR, cs.AI, cs.MA
  • Eje Cognitivo: Agentic Workflows, Multi-Agent Systems & Tool-Augmented Reasoning
  • Autores: Eric Yocam, Varghese Vaidyan, Paris Kalathas, Patrick Tague, Bruce DeBruhl, Yong Wang
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31974)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31974) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.31974.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.31974.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Agentic AI systems using the Model Context Protocol (MCP) are vulnerable to indirect prompt injection through untrusted tool outputs. We demonstrate cross-modal trust laundering, in which instructions concealed in an email-signature image using least-significant-bit (LSB), discrete cosine transform (DCT), or metadata steganography bypass text scanners and enter a multi-agent workflow when decoded by a tool. To counter this threat, we propose decode-time provenance binding, which combines HMAC-authenticated trust labels with an MCP-maintained provenance ledger and context-taint tracking. The mechanism labels decoded content as untrusted and blocks critical tool calls influenced by that content, even when a compromised agent substitutes a valid trusted label. We evaluate the approach using a real MCP testbed with three steganographic carriers and 24 adaptive payloads. The undefended pipeline exhibits 100% attack success, while keyword and regex defenses each permit 62.5% of attacks. Our mechanism blocks all attacks (0% success), including adversarial label substitution across mediated agent boundaries. A four-component ablation validates the roles of decode marking, cryptographic authentication, lineage tracking, and sink enforcement. An audited trusted-promotion mechanism increases legitimate-task completion from 67% to 100% without compromising protection. The approach provides carrier-independent containment within fully mediated MCP execution environments. Code and data are publicly available.

2. [Protocolo de Verificación Intrínseca]: Implementar un paso de auditoría independiente sobre cada afirmación fáctica y cita normativa/jurisprudencial antes de consolidar la respuesta final, rechazando cualquier inferencia no respaldada por evidencia documental directa.

3. [Asignación Dinámica de Cómputo de Inferencia]: Calibrar la profundidad deliberativa según la complejidad del caso: aplicar resolución directa en trámites estandarizados y expandir el árbol de búsqueda (múltiples candidatos + ranking) en casos con colisión normativa o jurisprudencial.

4. [Descomposición Agéntica y Uso de Herramientas]: Dividir la tarea compleja en subtareas atómicas verificables (extracción de hechos -> búsqueda de precedentes -> encuadre legal -> redacción -> control de calidad), invocando herramientas externas de recuperación antes de razonar en abstracto.

[arXiv:2610.31916] Permission to Retry: A Study of Authorization and Recovery in AI Agent Workflows

  • arXiv ID: 2610.31916 (2610.31916v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-10-22
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.AI, cs.SE
  • Eje Cognitivo: Agentic Workflows, Multi-Agent Systems & Tool-Augmented Reasoning
  • Autores: Deekshith Marla, Kushagra Bhatnagar
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31916)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31916) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.31916.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.31916.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> A durable agent can resume a consequential action while its authority changes and the action's external outcome remains uncertain. We study execution-time reliability of tool-using AI agents, isolating recovery decisions from language-model behavior. Checkpoint state, permission to dispatch, evidence of an external effect and permission to reconcile are distinct obligations. We present a finite conformance study that checks these obligations through histories scored against a provider effect ledger hidden from the recovering application. A prospectively frozen campaign executes 336 histories on native LangGraph and Temporal hosts, crossing 14 schedules, three provider contracts and four matched application policies. All 168 paired host cells have identical checked outcomes. Per host, retained-ledger reconciliation passes all 42 histories: 21 checked completions, 15 denials and six unknown outcomes with reservations retained. Cached retry, revalidation before retry and stable-key retry pass 30, 36 and 31 histories respectively. A separate 16-case component study of InfinityOS, an enterprise AI platform developed by Arya.ai, exercises exported authority, worker, recovery and settlement code over repository fakes. The results isolate the roles of current authority, provider contracts and retained observations in recovery, with safety, completion and unresolved obligations reported separately for the declared application profile.

[arXiv:2610.31709] Mining the Design Space of Multi-Agent Workflows

  • arXiv ID: 2610.31709 (2610.31709v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-10-22
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.SE, cs.AI
  • Eje Cognitivo: Agentic Workflows, Multi-Agent Systems & Tool-Augmented Reasoning
  • Autores: Xavier Cadet, Junyan Cheng, Peter Chin, Kyle Richardson
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31709)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31709) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.31709.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.31709.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Designing large language model (LLM) multi-agent systems (MAS) still relies on trial and error and informal "folk wisdom." Manual studies test only a few communication structures or topologies (e.g., sequential, parallel, shared-memory, or debate), while automated optimizers discard search trajectories after selecting a task-specific workflow. We present a two-phase pipeline for mining reproducible design principles from workflow code and execution traces. We repurpose AFlow's MCTS optimizer across six composable operators and 26 deterministically verified Enigmata tasks, retaining all intermediate rounds to build a corpus of 28,568 executable workflows. A sandboxed AutoResearch loop then mines orchestration code and prompt templates under an $L_0$-penalized validation objective and tests candidate principles on held-out searches. Across 300 feature-mining trials, we identify effects that persist on held-out searches across three levels of workflow design: workflows beginning with independent solvers and one-shot aggregation (Star-first) perform worse ($Δ= -0.045$); persistent shared documents (SharedMemory) outperform debate ($Δ= +0.135$); and prompt specificity and placeholder-based context routing yield gains comparable to communication-structure selection ($Δ= +0.118$). More broadly, our pipeline and results show the feasibility of turning discarded search trajectories into a statistical testbed for discovering and validating multi-agent workflow design principles.

[arXiv:2610.31148] Prompt Sensitivity in Tool-Augmented Model Comparisons

  • arXiv ID: 2610.31148 (2610.31148v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-10-22
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.CL, cs.AI, cs.LG
  • Eje Cognitivo: Agentic Workflows, Multi-Agent Systems & Tool-Augmented Reasoning
  • Autores: Mateusz Modrzejewski, Tomasz Radzikowski
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31148)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31148) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.31148.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.31148.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Language models are compared on question-answering benchmarks that pose each question in one wording, and a reworded question can change which of two models scores higher. We ask whether giving the models tools makes such comparisons more stable. An accuracy gain cannot answer this, because tools change several steps between the question and the scored answer at once. We propose a matched protocol that separates those steps: the answer readout, reasoning, the decision to call, the tool's answer, and the report supplied without a call. We apply it to six open models in five domains under five wordings. We find that tools raise accuracy where a program answers the question and widen the wording sensitivity of many pairwise comparisons. The widening follows how often a model calls, and call frequency follows the wording for some models and not others. It also depends on the interface and the instructions: a native tool-calling interface and a second tool description leave no detected widening in molecules, and sensitivity persists where tools return intermediate results. These findings show that deterministic reports do not guarantee stable comparisons. We recommend reporting call frequency and comparison stability under several wordings beside accuracy.

[arXiv:2610.31113] Can AI Co-Design Structures? An AI Agent for Early-Stage Structural Design

  • Eje Cognitivo: Agentic Workflows, Multi-Agent Systems & Tool-Augmented Reasoning
  • Autores: Ricardo Maia Avelino, Mennatallah El-Assady, Tom Van Mele, Philippe Block
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31113)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31113) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.31113.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.31113.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Recent advances in large language models and agentic workflows offer a new paradigm to early-stage structural design translating design intent into structural models. Although text-to-3D approaches have demonstrated that natural language can be used to produce visual, mesh-based geometric models, structural design requires domain-specific geometric arrangements and semantically rich data types. This paper introduces a proof-of-concept workflow for prompt-to-structure generation using an AI agent connected to a COMPAS-based structural modelling API through a Model Context Protocol (MCP) server. Through controlled modelling operations, the agent creates and modifies a graph-based structural data model composed of explicit building elements. The resulting artifacts can be inspected, modified, serialised, and further connected to downstream AEC workflows. The approach is demonstrated through selected prompt-to-structure examples and by progressive and editable design sequences that resemble typical iterative structural design workflows. The results are preliminary, but demonstrate the possibility of combining natural language, agentic workflows, and domain-specific structural data structures as a first step toward AI-assisted structural co-design.

[arXiv:2610.30917] Harnessing Language Models as Optimization Modeling Agents

  • arXiv ID: 2610.30917 (2610.30917v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-10-22
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.AI
  • Eje Cognitivo: Agentic Workflows, Multi-Agent Systems & Tool-Augmented Reasoning
  • Autores: Yunxiang Luo, Xin Xu, Tianhao Chen, Zhixi Xiong, Peiqi Jiang, Fan Zhang et al.
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.30917)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.30917) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.30917.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.30917.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Large language models (LLMs) can translate natural-language optimization problems into solver programs, but reliably producing correct formulations and implementations remains challenging. Existing approaches improve optimization modeling through agentic workflows or task-specific post-training. This raises a practical question: can a lightweight harness using a single model role achieve competitive performance without additional training? We introduce OPTICYCLE and OPTISTATE, two lightweight inference harnesses for optimization modeling, and evaluate them on nine benchmarks comprising 2,634 problems using 11 Qwen variants spanning 0.6B-27B parameters. Our results show that execution-guided revision consistently improves macro accuracy over direct generation for all evaluated models, with gains of up to 49.46 percentage points. Despite their simplicity, both harnesses outperform six adapted agentic workflows in same-model comparisons and four evaluated post-trained optimization models at comparable model sizes, without additional training. Explicit formulation state provides further but model-dependent improvements, suggesting that additional structure is not universally beneficial.

[arXiv:2610.30573] Diagnosing Personalization Failures in Mobile GUI Agents

  • arXiv ID: 2610.30573 (2610.30573v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-10-22
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.AI
  • Eje Cognitivo: Agentic Workflows, Multi-Agent Systems & Tool-Augmented Reasoning
  • Autores: Zhixin Lin, Dongliang Xu, Jungang Li, Shidong Pan, Jun Feng, Yanbin Sun et al.
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.30573)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.30573) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.30573.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.30573.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Unlike conventional GUI agents, personalized GUI agents require user preferences to guide sequential execution over changing GUI states. This raises two challenges: determining HOW each preference should alter execution strategy and WHEN it should take effect along the interaction trajectory. Existing benchmarks primarily evaluate overall task success, offering limited insight into these questions. We introduce PrefDiag-Bench with two components: (1) a categorization of preferences into five types, characterizing HOW they affect execution; and (2) a stage-wise diagnostic framework with 1,664 probes, tracing WHEN preferences should take effect and where failures occur. We evaluate eleven GUI agent systems, including end-to-end agents and agentic workflows, under two forms of user context: explicit user profiles and behavioral memory logs. Although existing agents achieve relatively high completion rates on non-personalized tasks, performance is substantially lower on preference-personalized tasks. Even the best-performing model, Seed-2.0-Pro, achieves only 47.17% on Process preferences under explicit user profiles. Agents struggle particularly with Ranking and Process preferences. Further analysis reveals two key capability bottlenecks: selecting relevant evidence from user history and recognizing when preferences should take effect during execution. Controlled interventions that explicitly provide relevant evidence improve end-to-end task success by up to 7.06%. PrefDiag-Bench offers a more systematic evaluation of GUI agents' personalization capabilities beyond overall task success. Code and data are available at https://github.com/Zhixin-L/PrefDiag-Bench.

2. [Supervisión Granular por Pasos (Step-Level PRM)]: Validar secuencialmente cada considerando o nodo de decisión. Si un paso intermedio presenta incertidumbre o ambigüedad, podar esa rama de razonamiento y recalcular desde el último antecedente verificado.

[arXiv:2610.28282] ChartEvo: Self-Evolving Agent Harnesses for Long-Tail Chart Reasoning

  • arXiv ID: 2610.28282 (2610.28282v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-10-20
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.AI, cs.CV
  • Eje Cognitivo: Agentic Workflows, Multi-Agent Systems & Tool-Augmented Reasoning
  • Autores: Qi Wang, Peijie Wang, Jun Cao, Haoyang Tong, Xiao-Hui Li, Fei Yin et al.
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28282)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28282) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.28282.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.28282.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) have substantially advanced chart understanding and reasoning, yet their performance remains highly uneven across chart types. While modern MLLMs perform well on common charts, their accuracy drops sharply on long-tail charts with diverse visual structures and reasoning requirements. Tool-augmented agents can improve complex chart reasoning by introducing specialized visual capabilities, but manually engineering strategies and tools for the heterogeneous and evolving long tail is difficult to scale. We introduce \textbf{ChartEvo}, a recursive self-improvement framework that enables chart agents to adapt to underperforming chart types through experience-driven harness-level self-improvement. ChartEvo jointly evolves reusable chart-specific Skills and executable Tools, while learning reusable local tool-call chains to compose evolved capabilities and control the inference overhead of tool-augmented reasoning. On long-tail charts from ChartBench and ChartX, ChartEvo improves accuracy over its base MLLM, Qwen3.7-Plus, by \textbf{45.6\%} and \textbf{32.5\%}, respectively, demonstrating the effectiveness of recursive self-improvement for long-tail chart adaptation.

[arXiv:2610.27797] Toward Agentic AI for the 6G Physical Layer: From Algorithm Discovery to Verified Baseband Silicon

  • arXiv ID: 2610.27797 (2610.27797v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-10-20
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.AI, cs.NI, eess.SP
  • Eje Cognitivo: Agentic Workflows, Multi-Agent Systems & Tool-Augmented Reasoning
  • Autores: Eric Mittag, Lukas Brechtel, Maximilian Stark, Hans D. Schotten
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27797)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27797) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.27797.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.27797.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> 6G will be designed faster than it can be built: AI-driven search already proposes physical-layer algorithms at machine speed, while carrying a single one onto embedded baseband silicon remains specialist work. That silicon imposes hard memory and numeric limits, but also exposes cost structures a GPU lacks, and a design flow that never runs on the target sees neither. We present an agentic workflow in which an LLM coding agent implements algorithms on a commercial baseband SoC, optimizes them down to agent-written vector assembly, and validates them on silicon. The agent is guided by a knowledge base of measured platform facts, checked at every step against evaluators it is instructed never to edit, and metered per phase. We showcase the workflow on an AI-discovered orthogonal time frequency space (OTFS) detector portfolio on the open 6G4All platform. It yields twelve kernels that run cycle-exactly on silicon, their optimized versions at 18k-405k cycles (30-660 $μ$s) per frame. It also yields a new detector shaped by the chip's measured costs that needs 13.9% fewer cycles than the port of the discovered detector while matching its error rate, with a measured edge our evaluation cannot statistically resolve, a structure that a hardware-blind search objective had no reason to find. What is not specific to OTFS is the workflow: an LLM agent exploring an implementation space under real hardware limits, with the cost of every choice measured rather than assumed.

[arXiv:2610.27572] RaspAssistant as an Agentic Framework for Automating Adsorption Simulation Research in Porous Materials

  • arXiv ID: 2610.27572 (2610.27572v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-10-20
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.AI
  • Eje Cognitivo: Agentic Workflows, Multi-Agent Systems & Tool-Augmented Reasoning
  • Autores: Marko Petković, Susanne Gooijer, José Manuel Vicent-Luna, Vlado Menkovski, Sofía Calero
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27572)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27572) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.27572.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.27572.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Porous-material adsorption studies require coordinated structure preparation, force-field selection, simulation, validation, and interpretation, making end-to-end automation difficult. We test whether RaspAssistant, a hierarchical multi-agent framework, can carry an adsorption study from force-field preparation through validation and downstream separation analysis. Across twelve multi-agent workflow-reproduction runs spanning four adsorption and separation benchmarks, overall scores ranged from 8.50 to 10.00 on a rubric evaluating workflow correctness, simulation validity, and analysis consistency. In a separate eight-case force-field extraction benchmark, the mean weighted score was 0.967, combining parameter agreement and RASPA readiness. In open-ended studies, the system diagnosed and refined an acetylene force field and, after validating a CO$_2$ force field against MFI experiments, screened 197 pure-silica zeolites that passed automated input checks. These results demonstrate autonomous workflow execution after task specification and input provision: RaspAssistant coordinates, validates, adapts, and documents extended adsorption-simulation workflows while exposing model sensitivity and methodological limitations for expert review.

[arXiv:2610.27234] EGI-BENCH: An Enterprise General Intelligence Benchmark Suite

  • arXiv ID: 2610.27234 (2610.27234v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-10-19
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.AI
  • Eje Cognitivo: Agentic Workflows, Multi-Agent Systems & Tool-Augmented Reasoning
  • Autores: Zhiwei Liu, Zhujun Lan, Jielin Qiu, Zixiang Chen, Liangwei Yang, Ming Zhu et al.
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27234)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27234) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.27234.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.27234.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Enterprise adoption of LLMs in agentic workflows is gated by evaluations that span a wide arc of abilities: CRM operations, heterogeneous-document retrieval, personal data understanding, multi-turn planning, tool use, function calling, long-horizon research, and long-context software engineering. These evaluations live today in separate repositories with divergent runners, dependencies, and scoring conventions, which makes cross-benchmark comparison difficult and obscures what a single aggregate number actually represents. We release EGI-BENCH, a unified LLM agent benchmark suite that consolidates eight enterprise facing agent benchmarks (CRMARENA, HERB, PERSONABENCH, USERBENCH, MCP-UNIVERSE, MCPEVAL, BENCHFORCE, LOCOBENCH) behind a single command-line interface with two runtime-budgeted execution modes. On top of the suite we define the Enterprise General Intelligence (EGI) Score, a six-dimensional framework (M1-M6) whose buckets are populated by fine-grained sub-metrics routed from each benchmark rather than by benchmark-level averages. We describe the suite, the sub-metric routing, and the quick/deep budgets, and report results for fourteen frontier and cost-efficient LLMs spanning the GPT, Claude, and Gemini families under quick mode. Top-line EGI ranges from 39.6 (GPT-4o mini) to 65.6 (gpt-5.5), and no single model dominates every capability axis: gpt-5.5 leads task outcome, planning, and retrieval grounding; Claude Sonnet 4.5 leads tool actioning and safety/abstention; and Claude Haiku 4.5 leads engineering quality. Code is available at https://github.com/SalesforceAIResearch/egi-bench.

[arXiv:2610.26676] Which Model for Which Role? Cost-Efficient Pareto Recommendation for LLM Agentic Workflows

  • arXiv ID: 2610.26676 (2610.26676v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-10-19
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.LG, cs.CL, stat.ML
  • Eje Cognitivo: Agentic Workflows, Multi-Agent Systems & Tool-Augmented Reasoning
  • Autores: Qian Xie, Wenyue Hua, Yueli He, Sripad Karne, Armaan Agrawal, Nikos Pagonas et al.
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26676)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26676) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.26676.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.26676.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> LLM agentic workflows increasingly employ different models for specialized roles. Users may prefer different trade-offs between task quality and deployment cost, sometimes also considering latency. Finding good assignments incurs a separate search cost from evaluating multiple candidates. We introduce Cost-Coupled Gittins (CC-Gittins), a multi-objective extension of single-objective cost-aware Gittins search for Pareto recommendation. It exploits the coupling between evaluation and deployment costs: assignments that cost more to deploy also cost more to evaluate. The search alternates between seeking higher quality and lower deployment cost, reusing each evaluation to inform both searches. After each batch, CC-Gittins recommends assignments that are nondominated under uncertainty-adjusted estimates of quality and deployment cost. We evaluate CC-Gittins on eight quality--cost matrices from workflows with two to five roles and up to 1,681 candidate assignments. We also evaluate the method with latency as a third objective. Across the eight benchmarks, CC-Gittins recovers a substantial fraction of the true Pareto frontier at roughly 10% of the exhaustive-evaluation cost, and recovers most of the frontier with few false positives by 30%.

[arXiv:2610.25919] LEAPS: Long-context Evaluation of Adaptive Pattern Learning and State Tracking

  • arXiv ID: 2610.25919 (2610.25919v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-10-19
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.CL
  • Eje Cognitivo: Agentic Workflows, Multi-Agent Systems & Tool-Augmented Reasoning
  • Autores: Howard Yen, Nitya Mani, Alex Renda, Christopher S. Hardin
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25919)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25919) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.25919.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.25919.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Long-context language models (LCLMs) power agents that act over long trajectories, from improving a codebase to running research experiments to forecasters that condition on lengthy histories. These applications draw on three distinct long-context capabilities: recall, where the answer is a fact present in the context; state tracking, where the outcome can be computed by performing a series of transformations to a specified state; and pattern learning, where the goal is to make a prediction by sampling from a distribution inferred from many observations. Existing benchmarks primarily focus on the first capability. In this work, we introduce LEAPS (Long-context Evaluation of Adaptive Pattern learning and State tracking), a collection of 12 novel datasets, spanning agentic workflows, behavioral modeling, and forecasting of market and world events. By construction, LEAPS is resistant to data contamination, extends to millions of input tokens, and uses reliable metrics. We evaluate 30 API and open models from 8K to 512K input tokens. LEAPS distinguishes models that perform near perfectly on recall tasks, finding that such models' state tracking and pattern learning abilities can vary significantly and degrade much more dramatically with increasing input lengths--even for pattern learning, where more observations should make learning a theoretically easier task. Moreover, thinking models often underperform their base counterparts, exhausting their generation budget or oversimplifying the task. We hope LEAPS can guide the development of the next generation of LCLMs.

[arXiv:2610.25540] Auditing Tool Traces in Agentic LLMs: Source Localization and Counterfactual Dependence Testing

  • arXiv ID: 2610.25540 (2610.25540v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-10-18
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.AI, cs.CL
  • Eje Cognitivo: Agentic Workflows, Multi-Agent Systems & Tool-Augmented Reasoning
  • Autores: Junhao Liu, Tianxiao Li, Yongxian Wu, Ziheng Duan, Ren Li, Jing Zhang et al.
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25540)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25540) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.25540.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.25540.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Agentic LLMs increasingly support scientific prediction by grounding decisions in external evidence. In biology, they promise accurate, interpretable predictions for important problems by integrating complex experimental and computational evidence. However, serialized tool results can contain hidden label shortcuts, such as dataset identifiers or template artifacts, that create apparent success without biological reasoning. To address this risk, we introduce a two-stage audit. Stage 1, Per-Tool TF-IDF Decomposition (PT2D), localizes label-predictive patterns to individual tool results while avoiding signal dilution in the concatenated tool trace. Stage 2, Token-Class Redaction, counterfactually tests whether the frozen LLM depends on the localized shortcut. We apply this audit to TCR-pMHC binding, where tool augmentation substantially outperforms a no-evidence baseline. At face value, this suggests that the LLM has learned to use scientific evidence. The audit overturns this interpretation: PT2D localizes the strongest signal to identifiers in one tool result, and redacting them reduces performance to chance. These findings show how hidden label shortcuts can masquerade as scientific reasoning or discovery, motivating source-level audits before benchmark gains are trusted.

Reglas Operativas Extraídas (Aplicación en Inferencia y Razonamiento)

1. [Directiva de Agentic Workflows, Multi-Agent Systems & Tool-Augmented Reasoning]: Orquestar flujos agénticos en etapas especializadas: (1) Agente Investigador/Recuperador (búsqueda documental exacta), (2) Agente Analista/Verificador (contraste de precedentes y normas), y (3) Agente Redactor/Crítico (proyección formal y auditoría de consistencia).

2. [Descomposición Agéntica y Uso de Herramientas]: Dividir la tarea compleja en subtareas atómicas verificables (extracción de hechos -> búsqueda de precedentes -> encuadre legal -> redacción -> control de calidad), invocando herramientas externas de recuperación antes de razonar en abstracto.

3. [Estructuración de Contexto y Restricciones Explícitas]: Formular las restricciones negativas (prohibición de alucinar metadatos) y las plantillas estructurales al inicio del bloque de instrucciones, utilizando precedentes reales recuperados como anclaje in-context.

[arXiv:2610.25281] Bridging Concepts: Multi-Agent Reasoning over Scientific Concept Graphs for High-Impact Research Prediction

  • arXiv ID: 2610.25281 (2610.25281v1)
  • Fecha de Publicación: 2026-10-18
  • Categorías arXiv: cs.AI, cs.IR, cs.LG
  • Eje Cognitivo: Agentic Workflows, Multi-Agent Systems & Tool-Augmented Reasoning
  • Autores: Donggyu Yoon, Minsung Hwang, Juneha Baek, Donghyuk Shin, Joyce Jiyoung Whang
  • Enlaces Oficiales: [Abstract (https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25281)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25281) | [PDF (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.25281.pdf)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.25281.pdf)

Resumen Técnico (Technical Abstract & Mechanism)

> Within a research community, new topics often emerge when previously unconnected concepts are first studied together. We construct scientific concept graphs (SCGs)--domain knowledge graphs whose nodes are fine-grained research concepts extracted from paper text by LLMs and whose edges capture their co-occurrence in papers--and study the task of forecasting high-impact concept pairs, i.e., discovering concept pairs that are currently unconnected but will have high impact when connected. To this end, we introduce new benchmark datasets constructed from CIKM, KDD, and SIGIR conference papers, with varied prediction settings, including impact forecasting for emerging concepts unseen during training. We propose MiroGraph, a Multi-agent reasoning framework with scientific concept Graphs, in which a feature designer agent designs predictive features for a given concept pair from structural, semantic, and trend perspectives, and then three LLM agents compute the actual feature values from the enclosing subgraph of the concept pair. Using these feature values, a classifier designer agent constructs a classifier that predicts the final impact labels. MiroGraph outperforms various impact forecasting and temporal link prediction methods.

Reglas Operativas Extraídas (Aplicación en Inferencia y Razonamiento)

1. [Directiva de Agentic Workflows, Multi-Agent Systems & Tool-Augmented Reasoning]: Orquestar flujos agénticos en etapas especializadas: (1) Agente Investigador/Recuperador (búsqueda documental exacta), (2) Agente Analista/Verificador (contraste de precedentes y normas), y (3) Agente Redactor/Crítico (proyección formal y auditoría de consistencia).

2. [Asignación Dinámica de Cómputo de Inferencia]: Calibrar la profundidad deliberativa según la complejidad del caso: aplicar resolución directa en trámites estandarizados y expandir el árbol de búsqueda (múltiples candidatos + ranking) en casos con colisión normativa o jurisprudencial.

3. [Descomposición Agéntica y Uso de Herramientas]: Dividir la tarea compleja en subtareas atómicas verificables (extracción de hechos -> búsqueda de precedentes -> encuadre legal -> redacción -> control de calidad), invocando herramientas externas de recuperación antes de razonar en abstracto.

4. [Búsqueda Deliberativa y Consenso Interno]: Generar y contrastar múltiples líneas argumentales (argumento principal, eventuales defensas y criterio de Alzada) seleccionando la solución que maximice la coherencia interna y la estabilidad frente a una eventual apelación.
